# The migration to one codebase

On 2026-10-17 the repo stopped being two codebases: the prototype (the "Kingmaker V2 PoC", at the root) and the production game beside it in `v2/`. The milestones:

- **M1:** the prototype is dropped, `v2/` is flattened to the root, and the site's `play/` is the game's browser build (below).
- **M2:** the scenes, re-authored as beats on the two-part GM.
- **M3:** the Obsidian plugin authors them.
- **M4:** the scenario, its maps and its places in Obsidian: the game's files first, then the plugin's editors for them.
- **M5:** problem reports.
- **M6:** the zoo and a combat ring: the fight on the map as simulation state and moves, the other side's turns played one at a time with a moment written for each thing they do, started from the zoo's rings, played headless, and on its own screen with every move played out; and the zoo as a campaign of its own, its combat ring an exhibit authored as data, opened from the title, the terminal, the page and headless play (below).
- **After the migration:** spells and blows said in a scene, resolved by the rules before the GM speaks (below).

Each entry says what changed, where the old thing is in git history, and why where there was a choice. The prototype's last commit is `bc2ab1f`: `git show bc2ab1f:<path>` shows any file of it.

## M1: v2 is the game

### Removed (in history at `bc2ab1f`)

- `src/`: the prototype's code (app, cli, eval, game, llm and lore shims, report, rules, runtime, web, world), with its component READMEs.
- `scenes/`: its 25 TypeScript scenes and their worlds. The game's campaign has the arc's six as data (`campaigns/caerwyn`).
- `evals/` and `.github/workflows/evals.yml`. The eval history they published stays on gh-pages, and the site still carries it.
- `lore/`: the prototype's copy of the Caerwyn lore, the same Cast, Places and World as `campaigns/caerwyn/lore` (once #661's edits were ported, below), plus its README, the plugin's imported `.kmscene` and `.kmscenario` files and two Objects notes.
- `vendor/`: the prototype's rules engine build (`dnd-srd-engine.js`, its typings, `patch.ts`), its pack and the SRD spells. The game's `vendor/` has the module-ready bundle made from it, the same pack, NOTICE and LICENSE, and the same `spells.json`.
- `assets/fonts/`: byte for byte the game's `web/fonts/`.
- `tools/v2/` (the converters and recorders that ran the prototype: `scene-files` with its hand-written table of each scene's facts and effects in `scenes/`, `map-files`, `v1-campaign`, `export-vocabulary`, `record-v1-golden`, `record-spell-facts`, `module-engine`, `golden-replies.json`), `tools/record-golden.mts`, `tools/ansi2png.py`, `tools/door-options.ts`, `tools/preview.py`, `tools/sheet.py`.
- `moon.yml`, `.moon/`, and the moon check workflow (`check.yml` at `bc2ab1f`).
- The root `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and `tsconfig.json`: the game's took their place.
- `.agents/skills/*` and `.claude/skills` (a link to them): V1-era skills about `packages/`, `scripts/`, `content/` and `AGENTS.md`, none of which is in this repo.

### Moved

- `v2/*` to the root, in one rename-only commit; `v2/docs/*` into `docs/`. `.gitignore` merged: the root's lines, and v2's `runs/`.
- The prototype's docs to [docs/prototype/](prototype/README.md), not edited: its README, ARCHITECTURE.md, `Kingmaker V2 prototype.md`, LOG.md, FINDINGS.md, `design/`, `guide/` and `experiments/`. Their links point where things were.
- The pitch and the roadmap (the user's claude.ai artifacts, as saved) from `src/web/site/` to [web/site/](../web/site/), not edited. Their repo copies are synced there now.
- `.github/workflows/v2.yml` to `check.yml`, named `check`.

### Changed

- **#661's fix to Sergeant Kell's lore** (53f45f3, which only reached the prototype's lore): the campaign's `private.md` and `knowledge/Sallow.md` take the same two edits. The GM's golden calls embed her lore, so the same words are put into them by hand ([ai/gm/golden](../ai/gm/golden/README.md)).
- **The site:** `npm run build:site` ([hosts/web/build-site.ts](../hosts/web/build-site.ts)) builds the pitch, the roadmap and the game's page at `play/` into `dist/site`. It fails if a link swap misses, a claude.ai artifact link is left, or anything like a key is in it. `preview.yml` publishes that site for each PR, its comment linking `play/` and `play/?fake`. `pages.yml` builds main's the same way. `web:serve` and `web:check` serve the page under `/kingmaker/pr-preview/pr-0/play/`.
- **Paths:** every `v2/` path and `cd v2` that code, scripts, CI and the live docs follow. What's left names the prototype's tools in history (`tools/v2/...`), is a copy's provenance (its first line), or is a record (the design docs, `docs/live`). The AI service's READMEs now describe the game alone.
- **The plugin** previews maps with the game's `client/map/render.ts`, and its fixtures read the campaign's lore.

### Judgement calls

- **`.prototools` stays, without moon.** Proto's default Node on this machine is 24.18, and the game needs 26.4 or later; the file pins 26.9 inside the repo.
- **`tools/srd-spells` stays.** It builds and checks `vendor/srd-spells/spells.json`, the only copy now (`--check`: 339 spells against the PDF). The prototype's account of where the words come from is folded into [vendor/srd-spells/README.md](../vendor/srd-spells/README.md).
- **`tools/glyphs` and `assets/glyphs` stay.** The terminal client draws sprites with the Kingmaker Glyphs font when it's installed. `build.py` now writes `web/fonts` and `client/look/kingmaker-glyphs.ts` (the fonts rebuild byte for byte). The npm scripts are back, except `glyphs:check`, whose `kingmaker-check.tsx` has no counterpart in the game.
- **The prototype's README and ARCHITECTURE.md are archived too**, beyond the docs the plan named: they describe only the prototype. ARCHITECTURE.md is new, the game's.
- **The docs service's default lore** (`ai/docs/config.ts`) is the campaign's, in place of the root `lore/`. Two of its test files read the default (the backstory's lore check and the fronts', seven tests), and every host sets its own.
- **`.containerignore` is an allowlist** (package.json, ai/, shared/, vendor/, hosts/ai.ts). The image's build context is the repo's root now, not `v2/`. The image builds and answers its health check on the fake model.
- **The design docs and live records are left as written**, with `v2/x` paths, since they're records of each vertical; two broken links in them are repointed. Provenance comments (a copy's first line) are left too.
- **The plugin's importer tests read the prototype's scenes from history:** `tests/prototype-game.mjs` takes `scenes/`, `lore/` and `package.json` out of `bc2ab1f` with `git archive`. The five-scene conversion test read the user's own checkout before.
- **The plugin's `node:` aliases** point at the game's `web/node` stand-ins. Only fs, os and path exist there, and the renderer imports none of them.
- **`build:site` is a script of its own** beside `build:web`. CI's check job builds the whole site; the browser job plays `build:web`'s page.
- **The preview's comment keeps the old header** (`pr-preview-v2`), so on a PR that had the `/v2/` link the new comment takes its place.
- **`pages.yml` no longer waits on the deleted evals workflow**, and still merges gh-pages' `pr-preview/` and `evals/`.
- **The page's IndexedDB name** keeps its scheme, `kingmaker-v2-web <its folder>`, now `…/play/`. The prototype's page was at `play/` too, with its database named `kingmaker-v2 <the site's root>`. A browser's old prototype games stay apart from the game's, and are left behind.
- **`hosts/ci.test.ts` parses every workflow's run steps**, not only the checks'.
- **The hanged clerk importer** labels new notes `campaigns/caerwyn/...`. The committed examples keep the `v2/` path they were imported from.

### Checked

At the end of M1, from the root: `npm ci`; `npm run check` (the type checks, 951 tests, the screens PASS 712; a first run lost one timing test under load, `ai/service/stream.test.ts`'s "a period between posts", and passed again); `npm run build:site`; `npm run web:check` (PASS, under `/kingmaker/pr-preview/pr-0/play/`, and on `dist/site/play` too); `npm run web:screens` (PASS 712); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems); `npm run e2e` in namespace mode (PASS, all 39 steps) and `npm run arc` (15 of 16, as before); the bindings regenerated unchanged; the AI service's image built from the root. From `tools/authoring`: `npm ci`, typecheck, `test:host` (91), build, Playwright (59).

### Reviewed

M1 was reviewed on its own commits before going anywhere, from the worktree's root against `bc2ab1f`:

- **Nothing lost.** Of `bc2ab1f`'s 1,817 files, v2's 998 are all at their new paths (915 byte for byte, 83 with the edits above). Of the other 819, 97 are still at their paths (some, like README.md and package.json, now with the game's), 178 have an identical copy kept (the archived docs, the site's pages, the lore's Cast, Places and World, the fonts, the Objects notes, the engine's typings), and the 544 deleted outright are the prototype's alone: `src/` (378), `evals/` (96), `scenes/` (31), `tools/` (20), `lore/`'s README and the plugin's imported scene files (8), the engine build and `patch.ts`, the evals workflow, moon and the skills, and `v2.yml`, which is `check.yml` now. The two rename commits are renames only, but for `.gitignore`'s merge, and `git log --follow` goes through them. The pitch and the roadmap are byte for byte main's. In the ten goldens, Kell's edits are exactly her lore's two line edits, wherever the recordings quote them.
- **Nothing broken.** Beyond the checks below: the site served at depth (`/kingmaker/pr-preview/pr-7/` and `/kingmaker/`) in Chromium, where the pitch's buttons go to `play/` and `roadmap/`, `play/` asks for a key and starts both workers on the fake GM with every request under its path, and the roadmap's links lead back to the pitch. The workflows parse and point at what exists. The glyph fonts rebuild byte for byte; the bindings regenerate unchanged; the AI service's image, built from the root, holds only `package.json`, `ai/`, `shared/`, `vendor/` and `hosts/ai.ts`, and answers its health as CI asks. Every relative link outside docs/prototype resolves, anchors too.
- **What it changed,** a commit each: `hosts/web/serve.ts`'s first line gave its base as v2's `/v2/`, and run by hand it announced `dist/web` whatever `--dir` it served; four first lines named a design doc at `v2/docs/`, and `ai/gm/fixtures.ts` a recorder that never was at `tools/v2/record-golden.mts`; three READMEs called the game v2; this log was short of the diff (`tools/v2/`'s tables and `v1-campaign`, the seven tests of the docs service's default, what's left of `v2/` paths, the `V2` constants); and docs/research had 65 broken links.
- **Judgement calls.** docs/research's links are repointed rather than left: it's the research behind the game, linked from the README and ARCHITECTURE.md, and every link resolved before M1. Only the targets change, and what only history has gets a permalink at `bc2ab1f`. A first line's doc path is brought up to date but not its provenance: `v2/docs/x.md` becomes `docs/x.md`, while what a copy's first line says of its source, commit and edits stays, but for `serve.ts`'s base, which M1 changed. docs/prototype stays as archived.
- **Checked again,** after the fixes: `npm ci`; `npm run check` (the type checks, 951 of 951 tests, the screens PASS 712, in 44 s); `npm run build:site` (28 files); `npm run build:web` then `npm run web:check` (PASS, 80 checks), and `web:check` on `dist/site/play` (PASS, 80); `npm run web:screens` (PASS 712); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems); `npm run e2e` in namespace mode (PASS, all 39 steps, 433 s; run as the review's fixes went in, which change no code the e2e runs). From `tools/authoring`: `npm ci`, typecheck, `test:host` (91 of 91, none skipped), build, Playwright (59 of 59).

### Revisit

- **#661's improvisation guide isn't in the game's GM.** `ai/prompts/gm-improv.md` and `gm-improv-remind.md` are there, but `ai/gm` doesn't send them (the prototype did, with `guide: "improv"`). #661's `scenes/hanged-clerk.ts` half is not ported either: the pay-cob-to-talk route, Kell testing only a case put to her, and Cob's crackle shown once. M2 re-authors the scenes.
- **Prompts nothing in the game loads:** narrator, actor, scribe, judge, player-sim, fight (V13 will want it), and the lite memory's memory-review and memory-scene with `ai/docs/remember.ts`.
- **docs/research and docs/qa** keep their text. The 65 links in docs/research that the move broke (all resolved before it) point where their targets are now: the archive in docs/prototype, the game's files, the campaign's lore (the same docs), or, for what only history has (`src/`, `scenes/`, `evals/`), a GitHub permalink at `bc2ab1f`. docs/prototype's links still point where things were (168 don't resolve): the same repointing would mend them, if the archive is to be edited.
- **The engine's rebuild pipeline is only in history:** the prototype's build, `patch.ts` and `tools/v2/module-engine.mts`. An engine upgrade brings it back.
- **The golden recorders are only in history** (`tools/record-golden.mts`, `tools/v2/record-v1-golden.mts`, `record-spell-facts.mts`). Recording again runs them from a checkout of `bc2ab1f`, where the game was `v2/`.
- **The campaign's converters are only in history:** `tools/v2/scene-files.mts` with its tables in `tools/v2/scenes/`, `map-files.mts` and `v1-campaign.mts` made `campaigns/caerwyn` from the prototype's scenes, and only their output is committed. Making it again runs them from a checkout of `bc2ab1f`; M2's re-authored scenes take their place.
- **`campaigns/caerwyn/vocabulary.json`** was exported from the prototype's 25 scenes (`tools/v2/export-vocabulary.mts`, in history). M2's scenes may change what it needs to be.
- **No counterpart in the game:** the glyph tests (fonts and code against the bitmaps, a sprite one cell wide), `kingmaker-check.tsx`, the sprite gallery, and the frame dump `tools/glyphs/frame.py` draws. `client/look/kingmaker-glyphs.ts` keeps a provenance line the generator drops at its next rebuild.
- **`V2` constants** in `hosts/` and a few tests (`const V2 = resolve(import.meta.dirname, ...)`) name the repo's root. Left as they are.
- **The plugin has no vault in the repo:** the root `lore/` it was installed into is gone, and M3 brings it to `campaigns/caerwyn`. Its `import-main-campaign.mjs` reads the prototype's TypeScript scenes, so it's obsolete once M2 lands.
- **Still only the prototype's** (in history): problem reports (`src/report`, M4), fights on the map (V12) and words typed in a fight (V13), the quick co-GM, the actors approaches, the zoo, and the page's door and lamp pictures.

### Dice in tests (2026-10-18)

The user, after a CI run went red on two dice flakes (#673's e2e, whose six Fire Bolts at the watch house door all missed, about one run in a hundred; M6's headless fight, whose page host rolled dice of its own): "we shouldn't be using a real dice roll in tests." Every die a game rolls comes from its `dice` row's seed (the checks, the rules engine, the town's reactions, a fight's), and the seed is `tx.seed()`, drawn once as the game is made. The memory host's was a fixed sequence already; SpacetimeDB's and the page host's were real randomness.

- **SpacetimeDB:** a private table, `dice_seed`, and the owner's reducer `set_dice_seed({ seed })` ([simulation/src/dice-seed.ts](../simulation/src/dice-seed.ts)). While its one row is there, `tx.seed()` is its seed plus how many games it has seeded since (`given`); none takes it away. Anyone else is refused: not_admin, "Only the database's owner may fix the dice." The memory host runs the same reducer and the same `seedOf` (its Caller's `setDiceSeed`), and the wire test holds `setDiceSeed` off the page's wire, beside installCampaign and dispatchWaiting. The bindings are regenerated: the reducer and `DiceSeed`'s type, nothing else.
- **The page host:** `HostConfig.seed`, where the memory host's sequence starts ([hosts/web/page-host.ts](../hosts/web/page-host.ts)). The contract test gives it 7. The page leaves it out and draws one from crypto at each start, as before: nothing web:check plays turns on what its dice give, so the page takes no seed of its own.
- **The e2e:** step 1 fixes the dice (1018) once the campaign is in, and checks that the AI service's `set_dice_seed` is refused. The town's step fixes them again (19) just before its new game, so its first Fire Bolt hits the door (a d20 of 17: 22 against AC 15), and the loop that cast it up to six times is gone; its check says the cast hit. Step 24 fixes its game's (24), and checks that its ruling's check is a natural 1, a failure, where it took success or failure.
- **The arc check** (CI runs it after the e2e) fixes its dice too, once the campaign is in. None of its checks reads a roll yet, but it plays on SpacetimeDB like the e2e.
- **README's rules:** a test never rolls real dice, and asserts what its seed rolls.

#### Judgement calls

- **The seed plus a counter, in a row.** The n-th game made after `set_dice_seed(s)` gets s + n (seeds go to 999,999,999, then round to 0). Reading the row with SQL shows what's fixed and how far it has gone. Taking it away is the same reducer with none.
- **`tx.seed()` itself honours it,** not only `createGame`: both hosts build `seed()` from `seedOf` (the module's `txOf`, the memory host's transactions), so anything that draws a seed later is fixed with it. It counts in the caller's transaction, so a game that's rolled back hands its seed back.
- **A step that checks what its dice gave pins its own game's,** just before it makes the game (the town's, step 24's). Step 1's seed alone makes every run the same, but a step's dice would move whenever a step before it made one game more or fewer, and M2 and M6 change those steps.
- **The outcome is asserted, not the numbers.** The Fire Bolt's line has to end in hit, not say 17 + 5 = 22, and its damage isn't checked; step 24's has to be a natural 1, which fails whatever the wizard's modifier. The other dice lines (step 26's darts, 27's blow, 28's fire, 29's spectacles, 37's Ray of Frost) were never either-or checks: each step is about where a line comes, whatever it rolled, and they stay as they were.
- **The memory host's tests are left as they are,** either-way checks too (hide's success or failure): their dice were never real (the host's own seed, 7 in most).

#### Checked

The type check clean; `npm test`, 954 of 954 (the three new: the owner's alone, the seed and one more a game, and two hosts rolling alike); the campaign check, no problems; the bindings regenerated, the reducer and `DiceSeed` alone. `npm run e2e` in namespace mode on ports 3361 and 8861, three times: PASS each (435.5 s, 432.8 s and 435.1 s), every dice line the same in all three: C's Insight checks (15 + 4 and 18 + 4), step 24's natural 1 and its plain check's 5, the darts' 8 force, Kell's club (9 + 3 = 12 against AC 12, a hit), the Fire Bolt's 17 + 5 = 22 at its first cast, and the door burned from 17 hit points to 4. `npm run arc` on ports 3362 and 8862: 15 of 16, as before. `npm run build:web` then `npm run web:check`: PASS, 80 checks.

## M2: scenes as beats, played by the two-part GM

### The format in the game (the scenes' foundation)

- **The format's code** is [shared/scene/document.ts](../shared/scene/document.ts) (the types as [scene-format.md](scene-format.md) has them, and a parse that holds a file to its shape and the references the game follows, naming the file and every problem), [load.ts](../shared/scene/load.ts) (`sceneOf`: a file as the runtime's Scene, extended rather than replaced) and [beats.ts](../shared/scene/beats.ts) (where a scene is: `reachedOf`, `stageOf`, `targetsOf`, `openRoutes`, `enteringAt`, `takeRoute`), the same code on both sides. Tested on the hanged clerk ([beats.test.ts](../shared/scene/beats.test.ts), [document.test.ts](../shared/scene/document.test.ts)).
- **GmState** gains `reached` and `twoPart` (roles, notes by turn, `moved`, `held`), kept as gm_state's `reached` and `twoPart` columns; the bindings are regenerated, the AI service's view carries them, and a scene opens with its start reached and as its stage.
- **The campaign is .kmscene files:** `scenes/<id>.kmscene`, read by the simulation's install and its scene defs, the AI service's campaign, the pack and the in-process host, the page's content (the allowlist takes `.kmscene`), the live record and the client's screen fixtures. The old folders (scene.json and Markdown: `git show 779c6b5:campaigns/caerwyn/scenes/<id>/scene.json`), the old loader and `SceneFile`, `RouteFile`, `CastFile` and `SceneInstall` are gone. The campaign check holds every scene to the format's rules (below), and [campaigns/README.md](../campaigns/README.md) says how to write and check a scene.
- **The scenes:** the hanged clerk as committed with the design; the Drowned Rat, the Heron warehouse and the King's morning copied in byte for byte from kingmaker-scratch/v2/m2/scenes, then their integration items in a commit of their own; the two interludes and the talk template converted from their folders by `tools/scene-folders.mts` (in history at `779c6b5`, deleted with the folders since nothing old is left to convert). The format's checker (`validate_kmscene.py`) finds no problems in the seven files.

### Judgement calls

- **The simulation keeps each scene's file whole** in its `scene_def` row (about 330 KB for the campaign), where it kept scene.json's structure without the Markdown before: the two-part GM's commits read words (a person's attitude and the stage's notes, for the roles it gives anyone new), so the simulation needs them. The install holds each file to the format's shape, then loads and validates it as the simulation reads it; SpacetimeDB took the larger install_campaign call in the e2e.
- **The conversion to the runtime's Scene** (load.ts), so the copied GM code keeps working: the start and the beats are its beats, each with the routes that leave it as its transitions and `anytime` empty, as asked; a route's condition is its `when`, and a route into a discovery with no condition reads "The player has come to understand this: <revelation>"; a cue is a cue, a dice route an attempt with its check (success to its target, failure to `onFailure`, its facts on success), anything else an outcome. The format has no words for how a check goes, so a check's success and failure are the stand-in's ("It comes off: <the node's title>."), and it has no reason (a route's label is a title, not "to …"), so the dice line is the roll's alone. Facts are sentences in transitions (the copied code's) and ids in the flat routes and the beats (the format's), with the scene's `facts` to look them up; essentials are the beats with `carry`, their paths' vias in order, each once, as where they're placed, and the cast among them as carriers.
- **The talk template.** The format has one description for the start, where the old template had an opening and a play note: the start's description is the play note then "As the talk opens: …" (scoped, so later turns don't greet again), and the GM's words for a talk's opening change accordingly (town.test.ts expects them). The conversion has a talk rule so the talk plays as it did: its ways into its ending are anytime attempts (the old goodbye was, judged before the reply from either beat), and its way into told is the talk's own (prepareTalk's confided route, worded for each talk), so the template's route there (which the file needs for told to be reachable) isn't a transition. The template has a truth written for it (the checker asks for one) and its goodbye is a good ending.
- **The interludes,** which the story skips: the downtime beat's play note and the opening are the start's description, its pressure the scene's clock (the format's start has no pressure), the old essential `lead` a discovery the story carries, with two paths written for it (Kell's errand, free; the town's gossip, for asking around) and a way in from the start, and Kell's `whyHere` written; `onward` is an outcome now (it was an attempt; it isn't played).
- **The tests of code that plays a scene have a hanged clerk of their own:** [shared/scene/testdata/hanged-clerk.kmscene](../shared/scene/testdata/hanged-clerk.kmscene), the old one in the format (its five beats stages with their old routes, a route of a later beat with an earlier one's id renamed `<id>-<beat>`, self-loops kept, `make-case` a start route, its essentials discoveries nothing leads to, no truth or whyHere), so they don't follow the campaign's content. The simulation's test pack installs it (`caerwynPack`, unless `asIs`), and so do the AI service's endpoint tests (`withTestClerk`) and the local host's (`localHost`'s new `files`). What the format couldn't carry of it: a check's reason and words, a check's facts on failure, `given`, `essential` and `towards`, an attempt without dice, `make-case` beyond the yard, the yard's pressure.
- **The GM code's tests keep what the goldens were recorded on:** the old hanged clerk as the runtime's Scene with every word (ai/gm/testdata/scenes/hanged-clerk.json, beside the prototype's other scenes) and the vocabulary as it was (ai/gm/testdata/vocabulary.json). The parity test still installs the campaign (its lore, title and creation world), but plays those scenes.
- **vocabulary.json** drops the entries for the six scenes the campaign installs, and the check finds an entry for an installed scene instead of comparing one with it. The AI service read an installed scene from the campaign anyway; comparing made every edit to a scene's hook, cast or prep fail the check until the vocabulary was written again, which the plugin's authoring (M3) shouldn't need. The story's `all()` now lists the installed scenes after the vocabulary's 19, which only the naming of someone carded in both could notice (Holt, uncalled in the prototype's blocked-entrance).
- **The rules the check enforces, as checked** (scene-format.md now says these exactly): "never locked behind one roll" is a route into each discovery with no dice; "endings that need evidence say so in requires" is, in a scene with a required discovery, every route into a good ending requiring a beat and each required discovery required by one (the Rat's taken-in needs the loft and the Heron, not the master, as its author chose, and passes); a `via` of one word names a cast id; `enters` names an event beat; a beat's carried things are its place's or carried by another scene; the scene's id is its file's name. A file of the wrong shape is held to its shape alone, and the runtime's own validation runs only once the format's rules find nothing, so a problem isn't said twice.
- **Play semantics the design left open:** nothing is open once an ending is reached; `takeRoute` takes the turn too (for `closing` and the path), steps the path (source `post`), marks a carried beat reached as landed (so the boundary owes it no more: "a beat reached is landed"), refuses `failure` for a route without dice, and leaves a failed check with no `onFailure` open to try again. `presentCast` counts the nodes reached as visited.
- **The play semantics' tests are on the hanged clerk,** as asked, but its routes have no dice: the dice test adds two to a copy of it (a check at the cart that fails into the mixed ending, and a climb with nowhere to fail to). The Rat, the Heron and the King's morning have dice routes of their own.
- **gm_state's twoPart** is an option column (a row from before it has none, read as nothing yet: the memory host's defaults test compares a default's text, which an object can't pass), and a note's turn is signed (the opening's roles are note -1).

### The scenes' integration items

- **Applied:** Hobb's brass heron token, round his neck in the Rat's place; the Heron's day book (Scrope's desk), the Patience's bill of lading (the bargees) and Hep's tally stick (her apron); the Heron's fight ending's two `startFight` set-ups from the old scene.json (Hep's, `unless master-here`, can't hold while the fight comes after midnight: kept, as the format says to keep a combat ending's); Hobb called "the big man with the bandaged hand" at the Heron as at the Rat (the King's morning has him in prose only, "the big porter", left).
- **Checked, already handled:** Sallow at the King's morning when the night put him in the cells. The boundary's presence step asks of each of the next scene's people the hand-off names whether what happened rules them out, and one ruled out is absent, so he's neither placed nor brought on by `send-for-sallow` (the same holds for Kell). On the fake, the boundary rules nobody out.
- **Left, as unsound for now:** Sallow's order of sale at the Heron and his papers at the King's morning, as things on him (he arrives later, and a thing in someone not yet there lies in the place's root from the start); the Marshal's written order (it exists only if the night gave him something); the split wheat-ear sack (the wharf's pressure makes it); the palace guard and the Purveyor's party (people need cast cards); Holt's custody of a witness (invented lore, the user's to keep or cut); Cob's heron penny (optional); Moll at the Rat (a card and a place entry).
- **Left, for the next agent:** the Rat's `prouts-page` stood in for the clerk's page by `missed`, which the format hasn't got. Now the boundary may owe `hanged-clerk:motive` into the Rat beside the Rat's own `prouts-page` (both carry `ledger-page`, which a boundary could match them by), and neither is hidden once the player has the page. And lore/World/Fronts.md names the old endings (case-made, open-case, wrong-man; the Rat's trail-lost isn't among its ticks): the fronts step reads it as prose, the titles still match ("Murder by persons unknown"), and editing it means editing the goldens that embed it, as M1 did for Kell's lore.

### Tests moved or updated

- **Onto the tests' own hanged clerk** (they test code): every simulation test through `caerwynPack` and `openedGame`; the AI service's pipeline, consider, world, background, town and server tests; the local host's. Where the clerk's checks lost their words, the tests expect the roll's own line (endings, say, rules, pipeline, world, local: the same push again's answer is the stand-in's failure), the decision's route without a reason (pipeline, world), pressing Moll at doubt by its new id `catch-moll-doubt` (say), and the prototype's dice compared without the reason (rules).

### Checked

`npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (966 of 966); `npm run screens` (PASS 712); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` ("6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems"); `validate_kmscene.py campaigns/caerwyn` (7 scenes, 0 problems); `npm run build:site` (28 files, the page's content.json with the seven `.kmscene` files) and, beyond the named checks, `npm run build:web` and `npm run web:check` (PASS, 80 checks: the yard opens and a line is answered on the fake); the bindings regenerated with no change after the schema's last edit. The e2e and the arc, once each, on ports of their own (another worktree's e2e held 3310):

- **`npm run e2e -- --port 3410 --ai-port 8897`:** steps 1 to 13 pass (90 checks, the campaign installed in both, the yard opened and played on the old pipeline); step 14, "An ending", fails: its fake takes the outcome `make-case`, the old clerk's route, so the scene never closes ("A waited 60.0 s for the scene to close"), and the e2e stops there (3 min 36 s). The steps after it name the old clerk's ids too: the creation's `attempt: catch-moll`, the full GM's co-GM route from `yard` to `doubt`, the story's endings (`open-case`, and its chapter's exit key) and the town's `end --exit open-case`.

As a diagnosis only, not committed: with that one exit renamed `murder-unknown`, the arc passes all 15 (the Rat, the Heron, its fight and stand-in, the summons and The End on the new scenes), so what fails is the check's ids, not the story's machinery.

The two-part design gives these to the next agent (the fake co-GM, the renamed checks). *Done (below).*

### The two-part engine (c3fdfe3 onwards)

The scenes of the story are played by the two-part GM, as [two-part-design.md](two-part-design.md) has it; its "As built" section says how. The commits: the user's two prompts copied in (`c3fdfe3`), a fought fight spent in the format's play (`cf935d9`), the AI side (`3a54f44`), the simulation side (`867e5c8`), the notes removed (`e3fa323`), the dev fake on the two-part GM (`328ec90`), a local-host test on the campaign's own clerk (`f02a017`), `npm run status` showing the two-part side (`52b52c8`), the e2e on the two-part GM (`7c3484a`), the two fixes the live game showed (`d301106`, the co-GM's deadline; `00d030e`, the revelation in its routes), the arc and the live story run's ending (`29e208e`), then the docs.

#### Judgement calls

- **A campaign-level switch, `gm: "two-part" | "pipeline"` in campaign.json** (`CampaignFile.gm`; none means two-part), read by the simulation's `playsTwoPart`; the AI service's endpoints follow the request's `twoPart` marker. About ninety tests script the old pipeline's steps (decide, reactions, consider) on the tests' own hanged clerk; the test pack (`caerwynPack`) and the local host's tests say `pipeline`, so those tests keep testing the code that stays, and the two-part GM has tests of its own. Caerwyn says nothing. A talk plays the old pipeline whatever the campaign says.
- **The two-part GM's opening and turns read no memories and open no disclosure,** as the prototype's didn't: what happened in earlier scenes reaches it as the prep's story so far, and the per-turn note is the design's list. The memory reviews still run after each line (the design keeps them), and a talk with Kell reads them; the e2e's "brought back" check moved to the talk (step 39). Wiring them into the mouthpiece or the co-GM (whose tools section says a remembered reason can win) is the user's call. *Changed: the memories are the GM's notes on each person now, and the co-GM reads them (below, "The old pipeline's salvage").*
- **The aftermath after a fight stays the old GM voice's one call** (`gm.aftermath`, resumeAfterFight): the prototype's two-part had no fights, and the design says the aftermath "still applies". The scene picking up keeps its roles (the opening alone sets them), and its co-GM walks on from where it was.
- **The roles' default is the stage's** (the latest reached of the start and the events: `stageOf`), where the prototype's was the current beat's, since a reached discovery isn't a stage in the format; the moved directive compares `moved` with the stage likewise.
- **The fought fight is spent** (cf935d9): after the Heron's stand-in, its ending is dropped from what's reached, and `openRoutes` shuts every route into a fight already fought, so the co-GM can't walk into it twice (`whyShut` says so). The fake co-GM, given a list of endings, takes the first whose walk is still open, so a recipe with the fight and then caught in the act walks the Heron to the fight, then on to the arrest.
- **A check that isn't whole is dropped** (an unknown skill, a DC that isn't an integer, an outcome missing): the narration stands alone and nothing is rolled. A check on the opening is rolled as a reply's is.
- **The wind-down's `closes`** is forced true once the closing has waited (`!mayHold || closes`), so a closing never waits twice, and a model that leaves `closes` out closes.
- **No settle on a wind-down turn,** and no lines are asked after the reply that closed the scene; one that waited asks for lines as any reply does.
- **The mouthpiece's reasoning effort is none and its cap 2500 tokens** (the prototype's); the co-GM's effort low and 16 rounds at most (the prototype's), with 20 s a round (asked again once) and 45 s in all, which the prototype hadn't (the live game, below), so the settle answers inside its minute.
- **The co-GM's open routes into a discovery carry its revelation** (the live game, below): the format gives such a route no words of its own, and "its revelation has landed with the player" alone, with the revelation far above in the prep, left the co-GM holding the scene back. The mouthpiece's per-turn note is as built: the revelation is in its prep, and repeating it beside the routes is pointing. Their operations are `two-part.opening`, `two-part.reply`, `two-part.closing` and `two-part.co-gm`, the prototype's names, which the call logs and the ledger show.
- **The co-GM's moves are taken twice:** at once on a copy in the AI service (so the co-GM is answered as it goes, and its later moves start from where the earlier left it), and again on the rows in the settle commit, which are the authority. A move the rows refuse is logged in the module's log and left; a role for someone not in the scene's cast is dropped.
- **The forced act's directive and the rules' directives lead the mouthpiece's "For this reply"**, in the old reply's order (the rules', then the act's), and the closing's or the move's come after them; nothing reacts to the act (the reactions step is the old pipeline's), which the README and the e2e say.
- **The fake mouthpiece's check** is a recipe of its own (`mouthpiece.check`: the same skill and DC on every reply but an opening or a closing, five plain outcomes), as the design asks for "one recipe with a check"; the fake co-GM's walk reads the prep's route lines, which `routeLine` writes in one fixed form for that reason, tested on every scene of the story.
- **The e2e's steps that were the old pipeline's** keep their numbers and say what they were for: 8 restarts SpacetimeDB in the settle (the decide's 30 s deadline is the settle's 70 s, so the step takes about 75 s); 14 is the co-GM's walk to murder by persons unknown, four lines and the wind-down; 23 is the two-part co-GM's roles and moves; 24 is the mouthpiece's check; 26 is the map's act at Kell from the lane (the only mechanic a scene still forces), with its dice before the first draft for both clients; 28's fire is lit by the stage manager (the recipe's `ignite`) and burns on at the next line's start; 30 compares the place's rows from once both clients watch the yard. Gone, with nothing of the two-part GM's to test: 25 (walking out on an ending of the player's own), 27 (a blow left hanging: nothing reads a threat after a reply), 29 (the reactions kept by the AI service as it died), and 24's plain check when a ruling is too slow. The notes' steps became a spectator joining once the opening is in (3), a line waiting on the settle and reading the co-GM's note written for it (9), and the lines kept by the AI service as it died (12).
- **The arc ends each scene by the owner, as the prototype's flow does,** so it says no line and the two-part GM speaks only the openings; its yard ending is `murder-unknown` now. The fight's own check (12, V12's) stays off: 15 of 16. The co-GM's walk through the whole story is the e2e's (31 to 34).
- **The live story run's yard ending** is `murder-unknown` too (hosts/live/story.ts). The live run's `gm` and `world` scripts still play, but record the old pipeline's rulings, reviews and reactions, which a two-part scene doesn't make: their columns come out empty.

#### What scenes no longer use (in the tree until the QA round)

Nothing of it is deleted; the user decides after the QA round what to salvage. A talk still plays the old pipeline's decide, reply and settle, and a campaign that says `gm: "pipeline"` (the tests') plays all of it.

- **The AI service:** the `decide`, `reactions`, `consider` and `repair` endpoints for a scene ([decide.ts](../ai/service/endpoints/decide.ts), [reactions.ts](../ai/service/endpoints/reactions.ts), [consider.ts](../ai/service/endpoints/consider.ts), [repair.ts](../ai/service/endpoints/repair.ts)); the reply endpoint's old path for a scene (`directivesFor` and `gmReply` with a decision); settle's `afterReply` read for a scene (the director's `director.after`: an outcome route, what landed or was hinted, walking out, a threat left hanging); the old opening's disclosure and `gm.opening` (open.ts); and in ai/gm, for a scene: the director's before-questions and readings (`beforeQuestions`, `readBefore`), the rules' acts read from words ([director.ts](../ai/gm/director.ts) `withMechanic`, and [mechanics.ts](../ai/gm/mechanics.ts) with it), the ruling ([consider.ts](../ai/gm/consider.ts)), the background co-GM ([repair.ts](../ai/gm/repair.ts) `runRepair`), the reactions (mechanics.ts `decideReactions`), and disclosure (turn.ts `disclosed`). Prompts: [consider.md](../ai/prompts/consider.md) and [repair.md](../ai/prompts/repair.md) (no scene asks them); the improvisation guide ([gm-improv.md](../ai/prompts/gm-improv.md), [gm-improv-remind.md](../ai/prompts/gm-improv-remind.md)) is wired into neither GM.
- **The simulation:** for a scene, the decide, reactions and consider commits ([say-commits.ts](../simulation/src/moves/say-commits.ts) `afterDecide`, `afterReactions`, `afterConsider`; [gm/consider.ts](../simulation/src/gm/consider.ts)), the old reply and settle commits (`afterReply`, `afterSettle`: the outcome route rolled after the reply, `moveOnAfterReply`, `endingReached`, `holdEnding`, `whyItWaits`, `reachEnding`, a threat kept, walking out), the co-GM's review and its merge ([gm/repair.ts](../simulation/src/gm/repair.ts), [moves/repair.ts](../simulation/src/moves/repair.ts), bookkeeping.ts's `wantsReview`, the `co_gm_result` rows), and the old opening's disclosure. The rules code they called stays in use where the two-part GM still needs it: `rulesFirst`, `takeAct` (a forced act), `inPlay` and `playedOut`, `checkThroughEngine`.

The speakers' colours are as they were (the screen check has them).

Fixed in `d301106`: a round limit and a deadline for the tool loop, the co-GM at 20 s a round (asked again once) and 45 s in all, inside the settle's minute; tested in ai/llm/seam.test.ts. Probed on the live game's own transcript with the real model: the murder taken 4 times in 4 (2 in 3 before), and nothing taken at line 3, in 3 tries. In play after the fix it took the murder on the next line and Hobb on the one after.

## M2: the GM loop on the game (QA tooling)

The prototype's GM loop (draft PR #663, branch `claude/gm-loop` at 5f01806) ran blind QA rounds on the prototype's scene harness: persona testers played a scene, a blind reviewer judged each game against a rubric, and I triaged every finding and published a report. Its round half (steps 1 and 2 of its skill) is ported onto the game, so the re-authored scenes and the two-part GM can be played blind as they land. The cases, fixtures and evals half (steps 3 to 5) isn't, yet.

### Ported (from `origin/claude/gm-loop` at 5f01806)

- `evals/loop/rubric.md` and `truth/hanged-clerk.md` to [qa/loop/](../qa/loop/README.md), as they were.
- `evals/loop/personas.json`, each persona with `build` added: the ready-made build nearest them, for when their sentence won't make a character.
- `evals/loop/tester.md` and `reviewer.md` to `qa/loop/brief/`, rewritten for the game's commands, its speakers' names, a later scene's notebook and its handed-on prep, and a command's five-minute timeout.
- `src/eval/loop/{round,play,review,triage,report,html,paths}.ts` to `qa/loop/`, and `cli.ts`'s round commands to [hosts/loop.ts](../hosts/loop.ts) (`npm run loop`), reworked for the game. Each file's first line says what it's after.
- `.claude/workflows/gm-qa-round.js` and the skill (`.agents/skills/kingmaker-gm-loop/SKILL.md` there) to `.claude/workflows/` and `.claude/skills/kingmaker-gm-loop/`, steps 1 and 2 rewritten for the game, steps 3 to 5 marked not ported.
- The scratch adapter that drove the two-part GM through the loop (`kingmaker-scratch/v2/mouthpiece/qa`: `qa`, `char.mts`, `run.mts`'s `writeState`) showed what another runtime needs: a game's state written from its own transcript, and the prep swapped into the pack. The game's record and pack do that; nothing of it is copied.

### New

- **Playing the game:** `start` and `say` (qa/loop/play.ts) drive a dev stack through the headless client's verbs, each game its own session and player. `start` makes the character, takes the story to the game's scene (reach.ts) and prints the opening; `say` plays a line and prints what answered it, as the player saw it.
- **Scenes mixed in a round:** `--scenes hanged-clerk:3,drowned-rat:2,heron-warehouse:2,kings-morning:1`, the arms taking turns within a scene and each persona played on every arm before the next.
- **The loop's stack:** `npm run loop -- stack up [--fake]` runs the dev host in the background (hosts/loop/stack.ts), and `stack down` stops it.
- **The report's calls** come from the stack's call log (`<stack>/ai/games/<uid>/calls.jsonl`), by each call's key; the calls that made the character and ended the scenes on the way are listed apart. The report keeps a copy of each game's log in the round (`calls/<game>.jsonl`), so a round outlives its stack (`--fresh` wipes the stack's store) and its archive is whole.

### Judgement calls

- **The commands' composition root is `hosts/loop.ts`, not `qa/loop/cli.ts`.** The README's rule: the hosts are the only code that reads the environment or argv. qa/ is handed its config (the loop's folder, the stack's connections, its owner's rows, its campaign, its call log), and `qa/imports.test.ts` holds it to that and to its imports (node:, itself, client/, ai/, shared/).
- **An arm is a stack, not a prompt overlay.** The prototype's variants were prompt folders laid over one harness (`KM_PROMPTS_DIR`); the game's AI service takes no overlay. A round's arm names a dev stack's folder, and a candidate is a stack run from another checkout, so any change, prompts or code, can be an arm.
- **The loop has a stack of its own:** `runs/loop-stack` on ports 3330 and 8817, clear of a dev stack being played on (3300), the e2e (3310) and the arc (3320). It's the dev host run detached with `--no-keys`, ready when its screen is in its log. A round can name another (`--stack runs/dev`).
- **A later scene is reached through `end_scene`, not a new reducer.** The owner-only reducer the QA console's `end` calls already ends a scene through a named exit, and the story moves on through its real boundary. The loop picks the exit: the scene's first good or mixed ending, read from the runtime scene the AI service's loader gives (the M2 format's `result`). Today's scenes don't grade their endings, so until M2 an exit of kind progress or persuaded counts as good, leave as bad, and other and combat as ungraded. If the game refuses an exit, the next is tried. No module change, and it works on the fake and on real models.
- **The character is made from the persona's own sentence**, the GM picking the build, with no backstory, as the prototype's harness had none. That's reliable headless (the e2e and `npm run live -- --creation` do it). If it never takes after the backoffs, the persona's ready-made build is used instead, and the record says why.
- **The suggested lines are off for the loop's players.** The testers write their own lines, as the prototype's harness offered none. The rubric judges the GM's replies, not the lines, and it saves a model call a turn.
- **What a tester and a reviewer see:** the narration, the dice lines, the game's own lines, and each spoken line after its speaker's name as the player knew them then. On a player's screen that's the speaker's colour. The notes on marked words are left out, as the prototype loop left out its ⟨⟩ lines (and M2 drops the notes). A quote is checked against the turn's reply with the names or without.
- **A busy turn is waited on, not only retried.** The module already tries a failed model call again every 15 s for ten minutes. A 402 from the key's in-flight cap reaches it as the AI service's 500 model_failed. The headless verbs wait three minutes. So the loop waits on a busy turn a backoff at a time (20, 40, 80, 160 s), carries a failed turn on with `retry_turn`, and logs each wait on the turn with the 402s the call log shows. The day's ceiling (KM_AI_DAILY_USD) isn't waited on.
- **A fight a line brings on** is fought out by the game's stand-in, as fights aren't built yet; the tester sees its line and the aftermath. A scene that ends in a summons (the Heron's) is recorded as it last stood before the seat moved on.
- **The prep is the runtime scene written out loosely.** prep.ts shows today's fields, the M2 format's (truth, clock, a discovery's revelation and paths, the people's roles, graded endings, a route list), and any prose field it doesn't know, under its own name, so a new format drops nothing. The pack adds what the boundary handed on to a later scene (gm_state's adjustments, rewritten opening, lead, owed and absent, read as the stack's owner as the scene opens) and the player's notebook.
- **A say past the limit, or after the scene's end, exits 0,** as the prototype's did, so a tester agent doesn't take the game's answer for a failure and try again. A turn the game couldn't answer even after the waits says so and exits 1, and the tester is told to say the same line again in a minute: a line said again after its turn failed carries that turn on (retry_turn) and isn't said twice.

### Checked

`npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (981 tests, 30 of them qa/'s); `npm run check` (the type checks, 981 tests, the screens PASS 712); `npm run e2e` in namespace mode (PASS, all 39 steps, 438.5 s).

A round on the fake model, played with the commands and no agents (`npm run loop -- stack up --fake`, round `fake1`, kept in `runs/loop/fake1`): two games, the hanged clerk and the Drowned Rat. The Rat's was reached through the hanged clerk's real boundary, ended for QA on `case-made`, the story moved on and the lead walked to. Each game took three lines and a fourth past the limit. Both review packs were built (82 kB and 107 kB). Two hand-written reviews were checked: review-check caught a quote that isn't in its turn, then passed. The triage worksheet was written from, the report built (`runs/loop/fake1/report/`, 2.2 MB, an index and two game pages) and read in Chromium, where a turn's call opens as its own transcript. The round archived to a scratch folder.

On real models (`KM_AI_DAILY_USD=0.2`, the dev key, its own stack folder `runs/loop-real`), round `real1`: a start and two lines in the hanged clerk. The GM made the investigator a human rogue 3 with the sage background from her sentence, and a Persuasion check rolled with its dice line. The cost was $0.0204 in 40 calls: the character $0.0005, the opening $0.0034, and about $0.008 a turn with its background memory reviews. It took 10 s to start and 6 and 10 s a turn, with no waits and no 402s. Its pack and report were built. Then a later scene on real models, round `real2`: the Drowned Rat reached through the hanged clerk, ended on `case-made`, with the boundary's own hand-off in the player's notebook and the Rat's opening the GM's, in 30 s for $0.0120 in 21 calls (the hanged clerk's opening $0.0029, its boundary $0.0026, the Rat's opening $0.0050, notes and the character's pick the rest). On the fake, round `fake2`: the Heron reached through two boundaries in 24 s, and the King's morning through three in 31 s, the last a summons as the Heron's `proof-taken` ends; a line played in the King's morning. Real spend for all of it: $0.0324.

### Revisit

- **The cases, fixtures and evals aren't ported:** `src/eval/loop/{fixture,eval,judge,eval-report,import}.ts`, `evals/loop/{cases,variants,judge.md}`, and the commands `case`, `fixture`, `run`, `run-report`, `rejudge`, `sample` and `import` (a playtest's problem report brought into a round). The game's state is SpacetimeDB's rows and the AI service's stores, not a session folder, so a case would be cut from them, and the GM it replays will be M2's two-part GM.
- **Truth sheets:** only the hanged clerk has one (the prototype's), and the Rat's, the Heron's and the King's morning's packs have the prep alone. When M2's re-authored hanged clerk lands with its own `truth`, the sheet needs bringing in step with it, or retiring. *Done: in step, kept (below).*
- **Ending grades before M2** are read from an exit's kind (above): the report shows "ungraded" for the many endings of kind other. *Done: every ending is graded by its result (below).*
- **The report's "Make it a case" column** waits for the cases.
- **The skill's costs** are from one game of two lines on real models. *Measured again on the two-part GM (below).*

### On M2's scenes and the two-part GM (the merge)

`claude/v2-qa` (PR #668) is merged into `claude/v2-scenes` (`9aeb442`), so the loop plays the re-authored scenes on the two-part GM; both had branched from `claude/v2-only` (M1). Only README.md and this log conflicted, and both sides are kept whole: the checks table has the campaign check as M2 words it with the loop's row after it, and this log has both M2 sections. The merge itself drops `notesComing` and `marks` from two of qa/'s tests, which M2's removal of the notes left unable to type-check; the three tests that read the old clerk's content (its opening's words and its endings' ids) are brought in step in the commits after it.

#### Judgement calls

- **An ending is graded by its `result` alone** (`f21c0bf`, `45b7740`). Every ending of the scene format has one and the loader's runtime scene keeps it, so the guess from an ending's kind the loop made before M2 (progress and persuaded as good, leave as bad) is gone. "Ungraded" stays for an ending a scene's file hasn't got: the game's own improvised one (`own-ending`, shared/scene/graph.ts), which the two-part GM has no route into. A fight's ending is still tried last whatever its grade (the Heron's `fight` is mixed), since a fight wants playing before the scene can end.
- **The reach test plays the client's demo against the tests' own hanged clerk.** The demo (client/demo.ts) is the client's canned game, and its yard still ends through the old clerk's endings (case-made, open-case, …), which the tests' own clerk (shared/scene/testdata) has and the campaign's doesn't. Re-authoring the demo would be the client's change, with its screens; the test reads a copy of the campaign with that clerk in the yard's place instead, as M2's other tests of code that plays a scene use that clerk.
- **The prep is the scene in the two-part GM's order, each route in the GM's own words** (`routeLine`, imported from ai/gm/two-part.ts), so a reviewer reads a route as the GM read it, under the node it goes to. It's the scene's prep for both parts at once: the stages' roles and the cards' role palettes are the co-GM's, and an ending's closing reaches the mouthpiece only at the wind-down, but a reviewer judges both parts' work. What isn't the scene's (the per-turn note, the world, the player's sheet) isn't in it, and what the boundary handed on is the pack's section after it, as before. The scene is read typed now: the loader only makes the runtime Scene from a .kmscene file, so the loose reading that let the format's fields show before M2 had nothing left to guard against; prose a scene has that no part shows still comes at the end, under its own name.
- **The hanged clerk's truth sheet is kept, in step with the scene, rather than retired** (`67180f0`). The scene's own truth, cards and discoveries say what's held and by whom, but not what the campaign's public lore lets anyone say, which the GM had and a reviewer doesn't (the warden and Nat Pike by name, the morning's talk, that the lost wagons are written off and countersigned by the Purveyor's office), and without it a reviewer takes those for leaks. The sheet says the prep is the authority where they differ, and nothing of the build. The other scenes still have none.
- **The rubric's checks, ids, severities and title (v1) are as they were** (`dd9394f`), so rounds before and after compare. Only its opening (what the prep holds) and its note on dice lines (the words after a check's dice line are the GM's) changed; the reviewer's brief describes the prep as it is now and how to read a check, and the tester's says what a dice line tells a player.
- **say needed no change for the checks.** The reply commit writes the narration that sets the check up, the dice line with its band's word and the outcome that came up as three entries of the turn, in that order, and say prints a turn's new entries as they are; a test holds it, and the fake round showed it on a stack.
- **The e2e's step 26 counts Kell down as hurt** (`8572889`): its check wanted hurt or badly hurt, but Magic Missile's three darts drop the guard about half the time, and the battery's first e2e stopped there ("down, bloodied" after 2, 5 and 4 force damage). It's this branch's own check (`7c3484a`), so it's fixed here.
- **The fake round ran with every reply calling a check** (`KM_AI_FAKE='{"mouthpiece":{"check":{"skill":"medicine","dc":12}}}'` before `stack up --fake`), so the dice showed in say end to end; the skill says how.

#### Checked

- **The battery:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 987 of 987 (16 s, 32 of them qa/'s); `npm run screens` PASS 685; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems"; `npm run e2e -- --port 3470 --ai-port 8957` (namespace mode) stopped at step 26 on the dice (above), then, with the check fixed, PASS, every step in 390.5 s (346 checks; step 8, SpacetimeDB restarted in the settle, 76 s; step 19's longest gap 1362 ms of the 5000 allowed); `npm run arc -- --port 3480 --ai-port 8967` PASS, 15 of 16 checks on (12, V12's, off); `npm run build:web` 26 files, 5.53 MB (1.49 MB gzipped), and `npm run web:check` PASS, 80 checks in 36 s; `npm run build:site` 28 files.

They started in 5.0 s (the yard), 9.0 s (the Rat, one boundary), 13.2 s (the Heron, two) and 17.7 s (the King's morning, three, with its long rest and level up), each scene before ended on its first good ending by its result (murder-unknown, hobb-talks, proof-taken). The replies ran 62 to 123 words, every spoken line tagged and the kids by what the player calls them. Two checks, each called on an attempt with the dice in it: **Perception DC 12: 6 + 4 = 10, failure (narrowly)** (the torn heels glimpsed, not judged), and **Persuasion DC 13: 8 + 0 = 8, failure (badly)** (Cob backs off and Moll steps between). The co-GM set a role for each of the three every turn and moved nothing, rightly: no discovery's revelation had landed, so the scene stayed at its start. Latency by the turn rows: the opening's first words 0.9 s and done 2.6 s; each line's first words 1.0 to 1.1 s and the turn done, the settle in, 7.9, 11.5, 11.5, 9.6 and 9.5 s (the mouthpiece 1.9 to 5.3 s, the co-GM 5.2 to 7.6 s, the stage manager beside it 1.8 to 3.7 s, the memory reviews 11 to 21 s in the background). The game cost $0.0242 by the stack's ledger: the character $0.0005, the opening $0.0020, and $0.0208 for the five lines (the co-GM $0.0065, the memory reviews $0.0066, the mouthpiece $0.0048), so a 20-turn game is about $0.09. What a reviewer would likely flag, as the GM's and not the loop's: the look's setup told the torn heels before the roll; Moll, unasked, said Cob stole the boots "off a dead man"; and Kell, not the player, set the drizzle against Moll's moonlight.
