The notebook has each hand-off's "how things stood", and the Heron's opening is the two-part GM's. Real spend for all of it: $0.0390.

#### What a QA round should know

- **A scene ended for QA owes its carried discoveries on.** end_scene takes the ending without its required discoveries reached, so the boundary owes them into the next scene that can hold them, as it would if the player had missed them: the fake's Rat was owed the clerk's motive and Hobb, the real Heron the clerk's motive and the Rat's page (both "with Cob", who isn't there). A later scene's GM is told to bring them out, which a played game wouldn't need; the pack shows it under what the story handed on, so triage can tell. *Fixed: a skipped scene ends thorough (below).*
- **Only the hanged clerk has a truth sheet:** a reviewer of the Rat, the Heron or the King's morning may flag a public fact from the lore as a leak. *Fixed: every scene a round can play has one (below).*
- **The fake co-GM walks to the first good ending a route a line:** a fake game ends on the third line in the Heron and the King's morning and on the fifth in the yard, whatever the tester says.
- **A natural 20 or 1 reads twice in the dice line** ("success (natural 20!) (with room to spare)"): describeRoll's mark and the band's word, the game's line as the player sees it. *Fixed: it says so once (below).*

### Before the QA round: the skipped scenes, the truth sheets, the dice line

Three of the notes above, fixed as small as they could be before the round: a scene skipped for QA owed its discoveries on, only the clerk had a truth sheet, and a natural 20 read twice.

#### Judgement calls

- **A skipped scene ends as a thorough player's would, through an option on the owner's end_scene** (`thorough`, an option bool): every discovery the scene can still reach is reached first, through the shared takeRoute (shared/scene/beats.ts `reachDiscoveries`), so its facts are settled for the chapter and the boundary and the carried ones landed, and the boundary owes nothing on. An option rather than a reducer of its own: it's one call, made in the same transaction as the end, and everything between the loop and the module carries it as it is (the owner's HTTP call sends it as SpacetimeDB's option, the port and the memory host pass it through); only end_scene's binding changed. Every other caller (the QA console's `/end` and `play end`, the arc, the e2e, the demo) leaves it out and ends as before.
- **Only discoveries, each by its open route in the scene's order** (a dice route on its success, the first route of several: the Heron's true book is "the book in hand", which suits proof-taken, the ending the loop skips it on). No event or ending is taken: an event moves the stage, brings people on and settles facts an ending can contradict (the Heron's fire settles `book-gone`, against proof-taken). So a discovery behind an event isn't reached, which on the story's scenes is only the King's Sallow giving himself away once he's sent for, and the King's morning is the last scene, never skipped. The world isn't touched: a discovery's things stay where they were (the page in Cob's boot), as if the player found it and left it there; the endings' effects that read the facts follow them (the Rat's notebook line says the page was in Cob's boot all along, and its give is recorded). *Changed: a thorough end takes the things of the discoveries it reaches into the player's hands, and an ending's give is done (below).*
- **A natural 20 or 1 says so once, in describeRoll's words** ("Medicine check: 20 + 2 = 22 vs DC 12: success (natural 20!)."): its mark stands for the band's word rather than the other way round, since it's what the game's other check lines say (the map's hiding and purse-lifting, a jump, the old pipeline's checks), and "with room to spare" or "badly" says less than a natural roll does. The band is still major either way and picks the outcome as before; only the line changed (shared/gm/two-part.ts `diceLine`, which the reply commit writes and the tests compare with). The e2e's and the local host's patterns for the line take the two natural forms too.
- **A truth sheet for each scene a round can play, in the clerk's shape** (qa/loop/truth/drowned-rat.md, heron-warehouse.md, kings-morning.md): the hook and the scene, what anyone can see from the start, the public knowledge, what's held as the scene's discoveries with who or what holds each and the cards' other secrets, who knows what, and how the GM discloses with the scene's own coin rules. Each is written from its scene's file and the campaign's lore by the lore's marking: the public docs (the place, Caerwyn now at the scene's hour, the cast's public profiles, the grain crisis, the Purveyor's office, the watch, the palace and the Assembly) are fair game, and the private and gm docs (the cards' knowledge, the "(truth)" docs, the fronts) are held. A later scene's sheet adds what the player may know already: the discoveries of the scenes before, which a skipped scene now hands on whole, are the player's to use and no leak when said back, and the notebook and what was handed on say which.
- **A sheet's public section names what the prep's own "what any local knows" covers in a line and lists what's beyond it,** rather than copying the prep, since a reviewer reads both; beyond it is what a reviewer would otherwise flag: the barge's name (the Patience, which only the GM's truth names in the Rat's prep), Sallow's look, signature and key to the granary (public in his profile), Hep Grist's name in the Rat's dock talk, the Crakes, the watch's law of arrest. The new sheets don't say the GM had the lore, as the clerk's does: the two-part GM reads none (its prep has the scene's own "what any local knows" and the story so far), and a public fact is fair game whoever says it. The clerk's sheet keeps its words.
- **A test holds the sheets to the scenes** (qa/loop/review.test.ts): every scene a round can play has one, and each names every discovery of its scene by its id, so a re-authored scene with a discovery its sheet doesn't know fails until the sheet is brought in step. The pack's test now reads the Rat's own sheet where it said the Rat had none.

#### Checked

- `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 992 of 992 (13.5 s), the five new among them (every scene's discoveries reached, the clerk's in detail, the thorough end on the local host, the dice line, every playable scene's sheet); one earlier full run had ai/service/stream.test.ts's timing check fail under a load average of 13 from other worktrees' stacks, and it passed alone five times and in every run since. `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn`: "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems". `npm run simulation:generate` changed end_scene_reducer.ts alone.
- **On the fake** (`npm run loop -- stack up --fake --root runs/loop-fix --port 3496 --ai-port 8986`, round `fix-owed-1018`, the investigator): a game at the Heron started in 13.4 s through the yard's and the Rat's boundaries, and one at the King's morning in 17.5 s through three. Each scene before was ended thorough on its first good ending (murder-unknown, hobb-talks, proof-taken), and the owner's rows have every discovery reached (the clerk's three, the Rat's five, the Heron's three), the carried ones landed (motive and hobb; the-master, the-heron and prouts-page) and **every scene's owed empty, the Heron's and the King's morning's included**. Both packs (122 kB and 98 kB) have no "still owed" line (what the story handed on is the skipped scenes' sentence alone, the fake's boundary rewriting nothing), the scene's truth sheet ahead of the prep, and the notebook with the thorough endings' lines ("Prout's page was in Cob's boot all along…", "Big Hobb owned to the loft…", "We have the Heron's true book…").

#### What a QA round should know

- **A scene ended for QA moves nothing in the world,** as before: an ending only records what it gives, and only what the story owes goes on with whoever holds it, so now that nothing is owed a skipped scene's things stay where the world had them. After a skipped yard, Prout's boots with the page in them stayed in the yard, and Cob comes down to the Rat without them, so the Rat's page can't be found there; after a skipped Heron, the true book stayed in the Heron. The notebook says the player found the page and has the book, and the King's morning's GM sees neither in the player's hands. The Rat's, the Heron's and the King's morning's sheets say so, for triage; giving a skipped ending's things for real would be the story's effects' change (`give` is recorded, story/effects.ts), not the loop's. *Fixed: a skipped scene hands on its things, and an ending's world operations are done (below).*

### Fixes from QA round qa1-1018

QA round qa1-1018 played eight games of M2's scenes on the two-part GM (build `5518c16`); its folder (`runs/loop/qa1-1018` in the `claude/v2-scenes` worktree) has the games, the triage's verdicts and the why of each finding in its triage.json. What it found went to fix jobs, each cut from M2's branch at `5518c16` and merged back in turn, and each job's part is its own heading below, in the order they were merged.

#### The co-GM's routes (`claude/v2-fix-cogm`)

The triage found three hard failures and a double event, all in what the co-GM moved and when.

What failed: **H1** (g08, the Drowned Rat): the eleventh bell's own outer limit ("about eight exchanges after Arlo's challenge, or fifteen into the scene") passed at turn 14, the bell never rang, and at turn 20 the scene was still going with three good endings open; the co-GM was told only how long the scene had sat at its stage. **H2** (g06, the Rat): with all of Hobb's story told, the bell rang and the co-GM took after-him into the mixed "Hobb goes to work", whose condition matched the player's plan to follow him out; it saw an ending's title on a route, not what's true there. **H3** (g02, the hanged clerk): one turn after the dead-cart came it took signed-murder because "Kell has enough evidence", though the route needs the player to have made the case, and the closing had Kell make it. **g08/1**: Arlo's third challenge played twice.

What changed:
- **A cue says its outer limit in exchanges** (`806f426`): a route of the format takes `limit`, a list of counts, each so many exchanges from the scene's start or from when a beat (`after`) was reached, stopping once a beat of `until` is, the first to run out being the limit (document.ts OuterLimit, parsed with its references, kept on a cue's runtime route, the check holding `after` and `until` to beats). shared/scene/beats.ts has `reachedTurns` (the turn each node was reached on, from the path's moves) and `limitOf` (each count's run after a reply, and overdue once the next reply reaches a count still running). Every cue of the campaign has one, its condition saying the same in words: the Rat's Arlo by the sixth exchange until Hobb is talking or bolting, its bell 15 into the scene or 8 after Arlo's challenge; the clerk's dead-cart about twelve into the scene; the Heron's Master six on the wharf without getting in or five once inside, and its second bell eight after he comes; the King's Sallow about eight into the scene, the time pressing about twelve in unless Sallow is in the room, and his say four after he comes. scene-format.md says what a limit is, how play counts it, and the check's rule.
- **The co-GM counts the turns and reads where each ending leaves the story** (`ce7485e`): its input says the scene's turn beside the exchanges at the stage, and the turn each node was reached on ("[arlos-challenge] (on turn 6)"); each open cue, count by count, how far its limit has run ("15 exchanges into the scene (the next reply is the 15th), or 8 after Arlo's third challenge [arlos-challenge] (reached on turn 6: the next reply is the 8th after it)"), and "Overdue: bring it about now." once due; each open route into an ending, whether it's good, mixed or bad and what's true there.
- **The co-GM's tools section** (`e31b856`, ours, after the user's prompt, which is untouched): a route only when its condition has happened as written, all of it, and a condition about the player's act needs that act (not someone doing it for them, asking them to, or the player planning it); a move only for a route whose moment has come, never to note that one hasn't, so not going through them one by one; the route whose ending matches what the player achieved when several fit; an overdue cue brought about unless its condition says it's no longer needed; roles as behaviour to show and what each person holds back, never analysis of the game; once something has happened, everyone's role moves past it; a cue brought about by taking its route, not through a role; and a route whose event a reply already played taken at once. The move's `why` says never what hasn't happened.
- **g08/1's double event**: Arlo's role brought the challenge about in reply 4, the co-GM took the cue two turns later, and the moved directive had reply 7 play it again ("The scene has just moved on to … let it show"). The directive now ends "If an earlier reply has already played it, it has happened: go on from it, and don't play it again." (`e31b856`).
- **The Rat's ways out divide on whether Hobb told the heart of it himself** (`ff7f680`): after-him and wait-for-him say "without having told the player the heart of it himself (his part in the loft, who his master is, where he works)" where "(or all of it)" let a Hobb who'd told everything but his token count, and each names the routes for a Hobb who has told it; let-him-go holds "whether or not they follow him down to the wharves"; "Hobb goes to work"'s description, which the co-GM now reads on its routes, says the heart of it in place of "all he knows". The clerk's, the Heron's and the King's ways into their endings were reread for the same overlap and already divide on what the player achieved.
- **The co-GM no longer fails on its own deadline** (`d0aaebc`): a round gets 20 s or what's left of the 45 s, and what's left is fractional, which AbortSignal.timeout refuses ('The value of "delay" is out of range'): a non-timeout error, it failed the whole co-GM with nothing logged. The probe hit it on 2 of its first 18 tries, and it's very likely what lost g08's co-GM on turn 13, the turn after Hobb was caught. The wait is whole milliseconds now.
- **A move that denies itself is refused, and an overdue cue is named again** (`d2ae486`, `90277f5`): reading every move of the round, the co-GM took a route while its reason said it hadn't happened six times in eight games (g08's page, g06's master's name, g01's proof, g05's way in, g07's motive, g02's murder), each marking a discovery or a way in the player hadn't made; replaying g08's turn 15 it walked the open routes one by one, "Not reached; …" on each. Such a move is answered with an error and nothing is taken; a cue's never is, since its move is what brings it about. And a move's answer names any cue still overdue, since a co-GM that had just recorded a discovery often stopped there and left the bell unrung.
- **The probe** (`a78952b`): `npm run loop -- probe <round> <game> <turn>[-<turn>]` (qa/loop/probe.ts), below.

##### Judgement calls

- **The limit is data, not prose read by code:** the conditions' outer limits are worded every which way ("by the sixth exchange in the room", "fifteen into the scene", "six exchanges or so"), and "overdue" needs a number. The prose stays (the author's words, and the nuance a count hasn't got, "it rings then, whatever is happening"), and says the same number. The counts' meaning was taken from the campaign's own cues: "whichever first" for the Rat's bell, and `until` for the Heron's Master (six on the wharf counts only until the player is inside, then five inside does), so one rule covers both.
- **How a count runs:** from the opening (reply 0), or from the reply after its beat was reached (an event shows in the next reply), and overdue once the next reply would reach it, so a move made now shows within the limit: "fifteen into the scene" is overdue after reply 14 and rings in reply 15, and "eight after Arlo's challenge", which showed in reply 7, rings in reply 15 too.
- **The counts chosen where the prose had none:** the clerk's dead-cart twelve exchanges in (the round's three clerk games reached their discoveries between turns 9 and 20, one never finding Hobb, and the cart coming doesn't end the scene: "whatever they haven't found can still be found while Tull waits"); the Heron's second bell eight after the Master comes, two past its "six exchanges or so" for a dithering player, so a moment the player is waiting for can come first (the condition states it now as the outer limit); the King's from the Marshal's minutes, about two to an exchange ("Twenty-eight minutes" is his first call): fifteen minutes about eight exchanges in, five minutes about twelve, and Sallow's say his "about four exchanges".
- **No check rule that every cue has a limit:** the format's own example of a cue ("the player setting off") has none to give, and the M3 plugin has no field for it yet. A test holds every cue of Caerwyn to one instead, and the check holds what a limit names to beats.
- **The turn a node was reached on comes from the path,** whose moves takeRoute already stamps with their turn, rather than a new field (no schema change, and a state from before keeps working: a node with no move has no turn).
- **The co-GM's extras stay out of the mouthpiece's note** (the limits, the endings' truths), as the revelations did (`00d030e`): it would be pointing at what's held. The prep's route lines are as they were, so the fake co-GM reads them as before; its reading of the input's "Reached so far" line still works with the turns in it.
- **The double event is mended in the directive's words,** not a flag from the co-GM saying the event already happened, which would have meant a protocol field and the simulation's commit for one case the mouthpiece can see in the transcript.
- **Refusing a move on its reason is a heuristic on the model's own words,** kept to the ways the round said it: on the round's 45 moves (`90277f5`'s message says 41, wrongly) the patterns catch its six denials and nothing else, and they pass "no one caught him" and "not caused by hanging". A refused move costs a round and the co-GM can take it again with what happened; a denial let through marks a discovery the player never made, which the boundary and the endings' effects then believe.
- **The probe is kept** (a command of the loop, with tests) for trying the next fix on the round that found the fault. It splices the round's transcript, roles and story so far into each request rather than rebuilding them, since a call log can't (a tool loop logs only the first 400 characters of its instructions).
- **The clerk's signed-murder keeps its words:** its condition already says the player makes the case; the instructions are the lever, and the probe kept off it 3 of 3.

##### Checked

- `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1003 of 1003, eleven of them new (the new: the format's limit, the check's rule and every cue of the campaign with one, the counts on the Rat and the Heron, the co-GM's turns and limits and overdue, the endings on its routes, its tools' lines, the moved directive's, the move refused on its reason and the overdue named again, the round's deadline nearer than a round's limit, and the probe on a made-up log); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems"; `validate_kmscene.py campaigns/caerwyn` 7 scenes, 0 problems. `npm run e2e -- --port 3517 --ai-port 8997` PASS, every step in 387.3 s (344 checks; its fake co-GM reads the new input's "Reached so far" line, and its walk to murder by persons unknown, the Rat and the Heron's fight went as before; step 19's longest gap 1364 ms of the 5000 allowed). (The worktree needed its own `npm ci` first: without a node_modules of its own, three tests that look for it fail.)
- **The probe on the round's own transcripts,** real models (`openai/gpt-6-luna`), three tries a turn, the bookkeeping rebuilt exactly as the round's co-GM had it on every turn probed:
  - g08, turns 15 to 20, where the bell should ring: 13 of 18 on the first run (2 tries failed on the deadline's fraction, 3 recorded What would turn him and left the bell); 18 of 18 once that and the overdue reminder were in, though turn 15's tries all took What would turn him on a reason that denied it, and turn 17's one Prout's page; with the wider refusal, turn 15 again: the bell 3 of 3, every denying move refused. (In the round: 0 of 6.)
  - g06, turn 15: the bell 3 of 3, "Hobb goes to work" 0 of 3; turn 16, after the bell: "What Big Hobb knows" by let-him-go 3 of 3. (In the round: after-him.)
  - g02, turn 18, the cart just come and the case not made: no move 3 of 3, signed-murder 0 of 3. (In the round: signed-murder.)
  - Each co-GM try took 6 to 22 s and cost about $0.002 to $0.006; the probes cost $0.146 in all.

##### Open

- **The M3 plugin has no field for a cue's limit:** a file keeps it on save (unknown keys are kept), but an author can't see or set it there yet, and M3's rules (shared/scene/rules.ts on its branch) need this branch's rule for what a limit names when the two meet.
- **The Heron has no ending for leaving early with the proof sworn but not the book** (Scrope's or Hep's word, before midnight): give-up says "without the proof" and away-with-book "with the true book in their hands", so neither fits, and the scene would go on to midnight. A gap rather than H2's overlap; the author's to decide.
- **A planned act still slipped once:** in one of the 18 g08 replays the co-GM took Taken in on Kell agreeing to take Hobb in "afterward", before she had.
- **The co-GM's input is longer:** about 3,500 to 4,100 characters more in the Rat, with every ending's truth on its routes (of a request of about 135,000, the prep being 110,000), and 1,000 more in the clerk. A co-GM that walks the open routes one by one spends rounds on refused moves; its 45 s held in every probe try once the deadline's fraction was fixed.

#### The mouthpiece's narration and checks (`claude/v2-fix-mouth`)

The round found the two-part GM's narration and checks wanting in the same few ways. The patterns: names the player hadn't heard (7: Moll, Hep, Dilly, Duchess), hidden things named (7: Cob's loose board three times, the crown in Moll's fist, the loft's smashed lantern, Hobb's "right" hand), meaning in the prose (4: "The promise has found the fear. What do you promise him?", "rather than taking the decision from you"), the player's bracketed reasoning heard, closings restaging the last reply (the Heron's arrest, the King's ruling), Prout's written note said by nobody, Hobb's whisper in Hep's colour, Kell taking the player's moment, and the checks (an outcome undoing its setup, a success repeating it, a roll on a compliment, no roll on the noose or the Crakes). The user's two prompts are untouched; everything is in what's appended in code ([ai/gm/two-part.ts](../ai/gm/two-part.ts), [ai/gm/unheard.ts](../ai/gm/unheard.ts)).
- `98baf82` **Who says a line:** quoteSpeaker's sentence leading into an untagged line starts after the line before it ([speakers.ts](../ai/gm/speakers.ts)), so Hobb's whisper after Hep's call is his.
- `ee70c53` **Names, two ways:** the per-turn note lists whoever's here unheard with what to call them, in full and then the short way; and `asKnown` swaps such a name in the narration's own words, as it streams and once it's whole, and in each outcome, before anything is shown or kept (`249910b` trims the words a named animal is called by).
- `835acd6` **What the player sees and hears** (SEEN, after the user's prompt): names, what's hidden, the player's bracketed words, the notes behind the screen; written words in single quotes, untagged. The per-turn note's new parts: what's hidden right now, and the player's asides.
- `1bdcc3b` **Checks, closings and the companion:** a check at a turning point and never on a remark, outcomes that follow their setup and add what's new; the closing goes on from the last reply, whose lines it's given; Kell, as the companion, leaves the case to the player.

##### Judgement calls

- **The name pass covers everyone unheard with a `called`, here or not** (a name dropped for someone away is still theirs to hear first), and the place's creatures and people off the cards who go by a name (the Heron's Duchess), called what their description says they are ("the mastiff"; else "the animal" or "the stranger"): no `called` was added to the place format. The note lists only whoever's here, as asked.
- **The short way** ("the girl", from "the girl in the shawl") is for a second mention and for what they own ("the girl's fist" reads better than "the girl in the shawl's fist"), unless two of them share its last word, when both keep their whole one. A title goes with the name ("Master Sallow" is "the gentleman with the silver-topped cane"), and "the" isn't doubled.
- **What keeps a name:** a speaker's tag (the screen's; nobody hears it) and anything in quotes, double or single, said or written and read: either is how the player hears it, so the name is heard from there on in the reply and its check's outcomes. A name the narration writes that the pass swaps is never heard, since the reply is kept swapped and the note's list is worked out from what was kept.
- **As it streams,** the pass holds back the last word (it may be a name, or own something), any titles before it, a quote mark that may open quoted words, and a tag not yet closed; the final post is the narration whole (`narrationOf` says when its JSON string has closed), so the draft only grows and ends as the kept reply has it. Tested at every prefix of three replies.
- **What's hidden comes from the cards, not new world data.** The loose board and the crown aren't tagged hidden in granary-yard.json: a hidden thing is drawn faint on the map, and nothing in play takes the tag off once it's found (only an essential's things are "found", and only a handed-over thing loses the state), so the list would claim them hidden for good. Instead the per-turn note lists, beside the world's things out of sight, each card's secrets by their first sentence (Cob's names the board, Moll's the crown, Cob's the smashed lantern), and the model judges from the transcript what has come out (the GM gets every fact and its status, "The GM must know the facts"). The world paragraph's own out-of-sight sentence moved into this part, so it's said once.
- **The tells are listed** (each card's "Shows:", its first sentence) as what can show, "now and then when the moment suits (not every reply)", since the round also had the fist pointed at three replies running.
- **The player's asides** are quoted in the note when their turn has brackets or asterisks: done or thought, never heard as words; "people see what's done (and hear what the player says their character tells them)", so "(I tell her she can trust me)" is still said.
- **Written words in single quotes:** the markup reads only double quotes as lines said, so a page read in single quotes can never be a line. The parser's own test for written words (a "note" within fifteen characters before the quote) was left as it was; g07's note had a long lead-in.
- **The companion is found, not named in code:** whoever a `follow` effect in this scene's endings or a played scene's walks with the player (Kell from the granary on), so a campaign without one has no companion part.
- **The closing's go-on clause comes last,** after the ending's "what it should feel like" (the Heron's asks for "Kell's proper words"), with the last reply's lines listed by who said them: put before it, without the lines, 2 of 3 tries still restaged the arrest.
- **The check rule says the dice decide a risky attempt, not the GM** ("whether it works is for the dice to say, not you"), with frightening first among the attempts: the first wording, a list with "call one at a turning point", called no check on the noose in 3 of 3.
- **The stage manager wasn't touched,** nor the place files, nor the co-GM. Kell's hints at the player's next move ("There may be another way in") are the companion clause's to stop, as far as it does (below).

The script and every reply are in `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/v2/mouth-probe` (probe.mts; tally.py counts them). $0.164 in all. #### Story items handed over (`claude/v2-fix-items`)

In the round's Heron game (g05) the GM put Hobb's brass heron token in the player's hand, and the player, who had never been given it, had to stop and say so. Three things kept what the story hands over out of the player's keeping in the next scene's world, and a fourth left the next scene's prep guessing; each is fixed:

- **What the player carried never left the first scene's place on a map** (`c129fbd`). carriedFrom read the place the player's seat was in, but walking out of a scene moves the seat onto the map's own place, which has no world, while the character stays in the scene's place until the next scene opens (V3: the map's take and lifted pennies go there). So nothing went on, and the character moved into the next place alone, leaving everything it held where it stood: every game on the map came into the Rat, the Heron and the King's morning without its purse and gear. The round shows it: its Rat and Heron openings have no "The player carries" line, and its King's morning had only the true book, handed over in a played Heron, whose summons opens while the seat is still there. carriedFrom reads where the character's entity is now (or the seat's place while there's no character yet).
- **An ending's world operations were only recorded** (`6586951`), as the prototype's recording world had them, so nothing an ending gave was ever in anyone's hands. Now each, once its facts hold, goes through the stage manager's gate (applyOps) on the scene's place as the ending applies (world/place.ts `scriptOp`): a give puts the thing in the player's keeping (`$player`) or a person's, a remove takes someone or something off the place, and the rest do what the stage manager's do. Then the place is written back, the sheet's pockets and the stage follow, and what the player holds goes on with them as everything they carry does. A combat ending's effects are still recorded for the fight.
- **A skipped scene handed nothing on** (`2cf425a`). A thorough end reached every discovery but left the world alone (the judgement call above). Now it also takes each reached discovery's carried things into the player's hands, through the same gate (story/effects.ts `takeFound`): Prout's page out of Cob's boot in the yard, and Hobb's token in the Rat. With the endings' gives done, the Heron's proof-taken puts the true book in their hands. The truth sheets of the Rat, the Heron and the King's morning say what a skipped scene hands on, where they said it moved nothing; the review pack, the loop's README and the skill say a skipped scene's things are taken.
- **The boundary guessed who holds what** (`2e62c27`). Its hand-off and its verdict (the next scene's adjustments and rewritten opening) read it from the transcript alone, which says nothing of what an ending gave or of a scene ended for QA: after the round's skipped Rat the hand-off had "his token, if you took it" and the Heron's opening "Grunda has Hobb's directions and token if she took them". Their input now says what the player carries as the scene ends, by the world's record, as the GM's world view lists it.

Every ending effect of the campaign's scenes that moves something, checked by forcing each ending of each story scene with no facts and with every fact established (the scenes before it ended thorough): the Rat's six endings take Hobb off its floor and its onEnd gives the page once it's out, and Hobb walks into the Heron fresh (its place's hoist, not gone); the Heron's proof-taken gives the true book, caught-in-the-act sends it off with Sallow as evidence unless the player has it in hand (`a538739`, below) and the two bad endings give it if it was in the player's hands and didn't burn, caught-in-the-act takes Sallow off the floor (not done when it's forced before the midnight bell, since he arrives with it and its one route leaves from there), and up-in-smoke makes its plume and writes its two notes when the fire or the sinking is settled; the yard's afterwards took Prout away already. The King's morning has none.

##### Judgement calls

- **The triage's premise, checked: no ending of the Rat gives the token.** The re-authored Rat dropped the prototype's `token-handed` fact, its `token-given` route and the endings' `create` of the token. Instead `c47327e` put the token round Hobb's neck in the Rat's place, "so the world carries it when he gives it up": in play it's the stage manager's give when Hobb hands it over, which now goes on with the player. So "the Rat ended on hobb-talks giving the token" is tested both ways a game can give it: the stage manager's give and then hobb-talks, and the GM loop's thorough end.
- **The token is the Heron discovery's carried thing** (the Rat's `the-heron`, `carry.things: ["heron-token"]`), rather than the old fact, route and ending give brought back. The discovery's description already has Hobb carrying "the way in on a string round his neck", and a discovery's things go on with whoever holds them while it's owed (missed, the token goes into the Heron round Hobb's neck, as his card there has it). A thorough end takes them. Bringing back the old route would make the handover an authored moment for the co-GM to judge, which the re-authoring chose not to have. It's a one-line content change; the user's working copy of the Rat only moves nodes, so the two merge.
- **Caught in the act's true book follows its words** (`a538739`). Its description and notebook line send the book with Sallow as evidence ("The true book goes with him as evidence (Scrope gives it up once the Master is taken), unless it burned"), and its effect gave it to the player whatever had happened; the old scene gave it only to a player with it in hand. The effect follows the words now, with the old scene's exception: a player with the book in hand keeps it, and otherwise it goes off the Heron with Sallow ("evidence: gone with Master Sallow to the watch-house") and the notebook line is written. The alternative, the words brought round to the effect, would hand the King's morning the same proof after both good endings, where the King's prep has one for each: the book in hand after proof-taken, and Sallow in a cell on Kell's sworn arrest after caught-in-the-act. It's the user's to revisit if the book should go to the player either way.
- **A thorough end takes the things of the discoveries it reaches**, not of those play reached before it: a scene partly played and then skipped keeps what play did with them. A thing not in the scene's world is left, and the thorough line says so ("taken: heron-token; left: ledger-page (nothing here is ledger-page)"). It's what a thorough player would end with.
- **A scene file's operations name things exactly.** The gate's loose matching (a name or most of its words) is for the stage manager's model, which describes things. A scene file names them by id, and the campaign check holds it to ids that exist, so an id the world hasn't got means the thing isn't there (the Rat's page after a played yard that kept it). Such an operation isn't done, rather than handing over whatever is named like it. The stage manager's own gate is unchanged, and the player is kept from both.
- **The order and the log:** the world's operations apply in the effects' order among the story's, the place read once and written once. The end's log line gains a "not done:" part, when there's any, after "recorded:" ("not done: give ledger-page (nothing here is ledger-page)").
- **Left as it was:** the cross-place move still leaves behind what a person held that isn't owed (v3-design §3.7), and the world has no "worn" to tell a token round a neck from stones in a fist. So when the Rat's Heron discovery was reached but the token never asked for, Hobb walks into the Heron without it (it lies on the Rat's floor where he sat), and Cob comes down to the Rat without Prout's boots unless the motive is owed. The page keeps its name from the yard ("a folded page sewn into the left boot's lining") in the player's hands. story-design.md's closeScene ("recorded, not applied") is left as written, as the design docs are (M1); scene-format.md's play semantics say what effects do now.

##### Tests

- The world bridge: a map test walks out of the yard, picks up the pennies dropped there and walks into the Rat: everything she carried is in her keeping there, nothing of hers is left in the yard, and the sheet's pockets read the Rat's world. It failed before the fix.
- The endings: the story tests hold the tests' clerk's endings to their world. The page is in the player's hands from case-made and open-case once it's read, and still in the boot otherwise; Cob and Moll are off the yard after wrong-man, each with where they went. The page goes on into the Rat and the true book from proof-taken into the King's morning; the Rat's give of a page that isn't there isn't done, with nothing in its place; Hobb's token, handed over by the stage manager in the Rat, is in the player's keeping at the Heron, with Hobb on its hoist.
- The thorough end: on the memory host a Rat ended thorough after a yard that kept the page takes the token and leaves the page, and the Rat's own give of it isn't done. On the local host, through the map: the campaign's clerk ended on its good ending with the page the stage manager handed over takes it into the Rat, whose opening is told the player carries it (not handed over, it goes on in Cob's boot). And the yard, the Rat and the Heron ended thorough, as the loop ends them, leave the Heron's GM told the player carries the page and the token, and the King's morning with the page, the token, the true book, the purse and the gear in the player's keeping.
- The boundary: crossing from the Rat with the token in the player's keeping, the hand-off's and the verdict's input say they carry it.
- Caught in the act, both ways: the book gone as evidence with its note and the notebook line, and not in the King's morning; in the player's hands (book-in-hand), kept and carried into it.

#### Merged, and what was checked

The three branches went into M2's branch in turn, each with a merge commit (`e2cd316`, `19cf005`, `ccdf972`). The co-GM's merged with nothing to resolve. The mouthpiece's conflicted in ai/gm/two-part.ts's source header (both jobs' sentences kept, the mouthpiece's first since it goes on from the speakers paragraph), in two-part.test.ts's imports (both sides' names) and in this section (one introduction for the round, each job's part under its own heading, the co-GM's judgement calls, checks and open items a level down as the others' are, and the section moved here from the end of the two-part engine's part, after "Before the QA round", where the round comes in time). The rest of two-part.ts merged on its own: the co-GM's input, tools, limits and refused moves beside the mouthpiece's rules, per-turn note, name pass and closing, with the moved directive the co-GM's ("don't play it again") and the closing directive the mouthpiece's (the last reply's lines). The story items' conflicted only here, both sides adding at the log's end. The Rat and the Heron have both jobs' changes (the limits and the divided ways out; the carried token and caught-in-the-act's book), and the format doc, the loop's README and its skill merged on their own. Nothing the merge put together failed, so nothing was fixed after it.

- **The battery, at `ccdf972`:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1023 of 1023 (the 992 before, and the three jobs' 11, 11 and 9); `npm run screens` PASS, 685 checks; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings) … no problems", and `validate_kmscene.py` 7 scenes, 0 problems; `npm run e2e -- --port 3610 --ai-port 9110 --root runs/e2e-merge` (namespace mode) PASS, every step in 387.5 s (344 checks; step 19's longest gap 1365 ms of the 5000 allowed); `npm run arc -- --port 3620 --ai-port 9120 --root runs/arc-merge` PASS, 15 of 16 checks on (12, V12's, off); `npm run build:web` and `npm run web:check` PASS, 80 checks; `npm run build:site` built (28 files).
- **The loop on the fake** (`stack up --fake --root runs/loop-merge --port 3630 --ai-port 9130`, round `smoke-merge-1018`): the clerk and the Rat, three lines each, and both review packs built. The Rat was reached through the yard ended thorough and its boundary, the player carrying the page (by its yard name), the purse and the gear. The fake's replies are the yard's golden ones wherever a scene is, so in the Rat its girl is named (she isn't in the Rat's cast, so the name pass doesn't know her there), and an untagged line after a thrown stone reads "Someone", as it did before the fixes.
- **One game on real models** (`KM_AI_DAILY_USD=0.3`, round `real-merge-1018`, the investigator in the clerk, six lines, played as a careful player would): $0.031 in all (the six replies $0.006, the co-GM $0.008, the memory reviews $0.011), the first words in 0.9 to 1.5 s and each turn done in 7.0 to 12.7 s. Kell gave the porters' morning; a close look at the heels was a Perception check (21 against 12: dragged); the boy, asked gently, named himself; the girl, promised nobody would take anything off him, admitted she was paid and gave the paid story; asked who paid her, she described the gentleman with the silver-knobbed cane and a big man with a wrapped hand. No name the player hadn't heard reached them, and the model wrote none in its narration (the girl was "the girl in the shawl" to the end, the boy until he named himself); nothing hidden was named (the page, the loose board, the crown in the fist that stayed shut); the per-turn note had what's hidden, the girl's name to keep and the player's aside, and nobody answered the aside. The co-GM set everyone's roles each turn and moved nothing, rightly: no discovery's revelation had landed whole (dragged heels without the blow and the cord, a big man with a wrapped hand without a name), and the dead-cart was six exchanges into its twelve.

What the one game showed that the fixes don't yet hold:
- **The check's setup told what the roll was for:** the narration before the dice already had the heels "torn, grey with grain dust", every outcome had them, and the major success said it again (the repeat `1bdcc3b`'s rule is for, which its probe couldn't measure), then narrated the companion clause ("Kell records each detail without adding a conclusion").
- **Kell broke the girl's story herself** ("It drizzled all night. My watch walked the yard."), before the player had tested it, though her role that turn was to stay out of the exchange.
- **The girl admitted the payment and then recited the paid story,** in that order, on the promise for the boy that's her lever.

### The old pipeline's salvage

After QA round qa1-1018 the user decided what to keep of the old GM pipeline's scene steps: its memory, kept as the GM's notes on each person and read by the two-part co-GM; talks, moved onto the two-part GM; and the spell-and-blow handling (the decider's mechanic question, the rules' act resolution, the reactions, and the threat a reply leaves hanging) parked for combat. Two jobs did the first two, each on a branch of its own cut from this one at `395b73e`, merged in that order (below, "Merged"); everything else of the old scene steps was then deleted (below, "The old scene steps, deleted"). Each part is its own heading below.

#### Memory as the GM's notes (`claude/v2-salvage-memory`)

The memory reviews ran after every turn and wrote a first-person memory per moment beside each person's lore (`Cast/<Name>/memories/index.md` and a note each), and the boundary added how a scene ended for everyone there and the time between. The two-part GM read none of it, so Kell at the Rat didn't remember the yard. The user's framing: "Maybe memory is more like GM notes for the character rather than personal memories." So now:
- `006b439` **One living note per person per game** ([ai/docs/notes.ts](../ai/docs/notes.ts)): `Cast/<Name>/notes.md` beside their lore entry in the game's copy (`characters/<id>/notes.md` for someone with none), the GM's (visibility gm), in the third person and in five parts, each so many lines at most: how they stand with the player now and why (1, set), what they've seen the player do (5), what they've learned (6), the promises made by them or to them (4), and what they did (8). An update (`note`) sets the standing, rewrites the line its `replaces` names (its words, or enough of them to find just that line), takes one out or adds one; a line the part has already is kept, and a full part gives up its oldest line, so the note never grows past a page. `gmNotes(game, id)` is the note as a GM part reads it, or undefined.
- `1447dc2` **The reviews write it.** The attention filter's categories are brought to the note's parts, and anything flagged runs the notes review: one structured call ([memory-review.md](../ai/prompts/memory-review.md), retuned) answering with updates that each rewrite, take out or add a line. The world-facts agent keeps its tool loop for the lore.
- `0292208` **The boundary writes what a scene's end says of each person:** the ending's authored attitude, memory and promise effects as play made them hold, that the others there were there when it ended, and the time between.
- `03f4d6d` **A talk reads and writes it**, and so does the old pipeline's GM a talk plays on: the talk's plan is told the notes for how things stand, a confidence told is a line under what they did, disclosure starts each person's scope with their notes, and the GM's memory block shows them where the memory index was.
- `c4b0862` **The co-GM reads it:** its input has "# Notes on the people here (what's happened with them in play)" above the current roles, each person on stage who has notes under their name and id ([ai/gm/two-part.ts](../ai/gm/two-part.ts) `notesOnPeople`).
- `b32e3eb` **What the probe on real models showed** (below): the review's token cap, the prompt tightened against noting too much, and the time between for the people of the scene that ended only.
- `d3f2711` **`npm run status -- --game <n> --notes`** prints the notes on each person (`--memories`, its old name, still works).

##### Judgement calls

- **Where it lives:** beside the lore entry, as a file of its own (`notes.md`) rather than the memories folder reshaped: one doc a person, named for what it is. Its title says "notes from play", since each person's authored `index.md` already links `gm.md` as "GM notes" (the truth about them). It's read from the game's copy only, never through readDoc's fall-back to the authored lore, so an authored doc at that path is never anyone's notes; and the world-facts agent's `edit_doc` and `create_doc` refuse a notes path.
- **A full part gives up its oldest line** rather than refusing the line: the review is one call with nobody to answer an error, and the boundary and a talk write lines they can't merge. The review is told each part's limit and to merge or drop when one's full; the oldest giving way is the backstop, and its record says so ("gave up …"). Two of the parts hold more than the others (learned 6, did 8) since every boundary adds to what they did. A line is cut at a word past 400 characters (300 cut a lead mid-sentence).
- **The notes review is one structured call, not a tool loop:** it needs no lore (the moment and everyone's notes are its input), one round costs less than a tool loop's two ($0.0002 to $0.0011 a review on the probe), and each update is written at once through `note`, which reads the note again first, so writers one after another keep each other's lines. Its cap is 8000 tokens: Luna's reasoning counts against it, and at 2000 the probe's coin-for-Cob review ran out.
- **The categories, brought to the note's parts:** `promise` (V1's deferred and general commitments), `standing` and `learned` (V1's change in relationship or knowledge, in two; learned takes `witnessed`'s words for a companion and whoever stood watch), `seen` (V1's exchange, with the rest of what the player does in front of someone), and a new `did` (what someone does on their own account that later scenes should hold them to: an admission said to the player alone was caught by no category before); V1's `improvised_detail` and `other_world_update` as they were. V1's `immediate_commitment` went: what's to be done now is done in the next reply or not at all, and a note keeps what lasts. Seven questions where there were eight, still one Jev call.
- **The notes review runs on any flag**, a world fact's too, since an improvised claim or a lasting change has witnesses; the world-facts agent runs after it on a world fact, as before, and lost `save_memory` and `list_memories` (a belief or a claim heard is the notes review's now).
- **Simplified away, as the note's shape made them redundant:** the four per-category memory prompts (review/commitment, relationship, exchange and witnessed), folded into memory-review.md (who promised what to whom and whether it's kept, the feeling it leaves, a gift as a debt or a kindness, the witness's own words); the relationship review's edit of a person's `knowledge/<Name>.md` (the two-part GM reads the scene's cards, not those docs, and the note's standing and learned carry a changed view); and the prototype's lite memory, which nothing in the game called (ai/docs/remember.ts, memory-scene.md, `saveFact`: one merged review and one memory per person at a scene's end, which one note per person kept current replaces). memory-review.md, the lite memory's prompt for a one-call review, is the notes review's prompt now. The memory functions (`saveMemory`, `memoryIndex`, `memoryLines`, `memoryIndexPath`) and `broadcastEnding` are gone.
- **The ending's effects are read on the AI side.** An ending's `attitude`, `remember` and `promise` effects were only recorded (the simulation writes an attitude row nothing on the GM's side reads), so their author's words never reached the GM. The boundary now evaluates their `if` and `unless` facts as the simulation's holdsIn does and writes them into the notes: an attitude is the person's standing ("Friendly: The King's investigator made Prout's case properly."), a memory a line of what they did ("The Hanged Clerk, as it ended: …"), a promise a line in each side's notes. The ending's standing replaces whatever standing the reviews wrote: it's the author's word on how the scene left them.
- **V1's milestone broadcast is a line,** "Was there when The Hanged Clerk ended: Murder by persons unknown.", for everyone there without a memory of it from the ending, rather than the ending's whole outcome in everyone's notes: the outcome is the story's, and the hand-off carries it to every later scene's prep.
- **The time between is for the people of the scene that ended:** the probe's Rat had Hobb's notes saying Kell followed him from the wagons and that two men were asking after him, because the lead and an adjustment named him. Someone the next scene brings on didn't live the time between, and what the adjustments say of them is that scene's prep already. The test of Nan getting an adjustment's line is turned round accordingly.
- **Written once for a boundary** by the notes themselves: a line they have already is kept as it is, so a boundary run again after a crash adds nothing (the title checks on the memory index went).
- **Talks are kept to a small seam,** since another job is moving them onto the two-part GM: `gmNotes(game, id)` to read and `note` to write. The plan's first-person `memory` field (how they'd remember telling it) is left for that job, unread now: the told line is the note's own words, "Told Wren about the door at the Drowned Rat, in a word between scenes (…)".
- **The old pipeline's memory block keeps the prototype's words,** with the notes where the memory index was, so the parity test's goldens (recorded with nobody remembering anything) hold byte for byte; it goes when the talks leave the old pipeline.
- **The co-GM's notes go above the current roles,** for each person on stage only, with a line to let a role follow from them where they bear on the moment and never recite them; its tools section's "what a character remembers … can give them a better reason" now points at them.
- **The mouthpiece reads no notes, and its cards carry no "So far:" line** (probed below): the scenes before reach it as the prep's hand-offs, which on the probe already had the promise, the coin and the marks; with a So far line it brought the promise up unasked more often, but recited the line's own words as Kell's; and the cards are in its instructions, which a review changing someone's standing mid-scene would change on that turn, spoiling the prompt cache for about 97,000 characters. The co-GM's roles carry the notes into what the mouthpiece plays from the next reply on.
- **The e2e's checks of memory** say the notes now: step 22 (Kell's notes after the review), step 31 (her standing and her memory of how the yard ended), step 39 (the talk's greeting told her notes), and a new check at step 33: the Heron's co-GM given her notes from the yard's and the Rat's endings.

##### The probes

- **On the fake** (`npm run loop -- stack up --fake --root runs/loop-notes --port 3770 --ai-port 9170`; round `notes-fake`, the Rat): `start` took the story through the yard ended thorough on murder-unknown, and Kell's notes were there: her standing, "Friendly: The King's investigator made Prout's case properly.", and "The Hanged Clerk, as it ended: The King's investigator proved Prout was murdered, and that Big Hobb Mallory was at the granary."; Cob's and Moll's that they were there when it ended. A line at the Rat, and the co-GM's input (the turn's settle in the call log) had "# Notes on the people here (what's happened with them in play)" with Kell's and Cob's notes, above the roles; the fake's notes review then added its line to Kell's notes.
- **On real models** (`KM_AI_DAILY_USD=0.2`, `runs/loop-notes-real` on 3780 and 9180, `npm run play` with the ready-made rogue, Wren Ashdown; the clerk ended thorough as the GM loop ends a scene, by a script in kingmaker-scratch/v2/salvage-memory, since `play end` has no thorough). The first game's notes showed what `b32e3eb` fixed: the coin-for-Cob review ran out of tokens, one promise was noted five times over, a look at the body was noted as something seen, and the review running beside the one before noted that moment again. The second game, after the fixes: four lines in the clerk (a promise to Kell that the children wouldn't be blamed; a silver coin pressed into Cob's hand; a look at the neck, Perception 11 against 12, narrowly; the strangling named to Kell, who asked for evidence). Kell's notes after them: standing "cautiously receptive to Wren, who promised the children would not be blamed, but expects actions to prove her word"; seen the coin; learned the claim of strangling she couldn't see from below; the promise, untested. Cob's and Moll's had the coin and the claim; the look at the body was noted for nobody. Ended on murder-unknown, her standing became the ending's and her notes gained the ending's line and two of the time between. At the Rat, one line, "(quietly, to Kell) Before we start here, Sergeant: what did you make of me back in the yard this morning?", and her reply reflected the yard: "As for the yard: you saw what I couldn't swear to from the ground, and you were right to make me look again. You didn't ask me to write more than I could stand behind." That came through the prep's hand-off, since the mouthpiece reads no notes. The co-GM's input had the notes on all four people there, and its role for Kell reflected them: "Her respect for Wren is strengthened: they insisted on evidence she could stand behind and proved right. Still wary of the room and determined not to overstate anything." The notes review then rewrote her standing from the reply.
- **The notes review replayed** on both games' recorded requests with the tightened prompt (kingmaker-scratch/v2/salvage-memory, replay-notes.mts; replay-1.txt and replay-2.txt): the look at the body, no update in 4 tries of 4; the coin, a line under what each of the three saw in every try, and in two of the four more besides (a standing for each, Cob's taking it: 7 and 10 updates in all); the Rat's line, Kell's standing rewritten (and once a line of what she'd seen in the yard), and nothing for the question or her "I'm lent", where the game's review (before the last tightening) had noted both.
- **"So far:" on the mouthpiece's cards** (so-far.mts, so-far-1.txt): the Rat's first-line call replayed as the game sent it and with a line on each card from the notes (the standing and the promises: Kell's and Cob's), three tries each. The game's line: both variants answered from the yard, but with So far all three replies opened on the standing's own words ("You asked me to look again. You didn't ask me to swear to what I couldn't see."), which the review had written from the original reply. A second line, "Those two kids from the yard this morning. Do you reckon they'll keep out of trouble?": Kell brought up the promise in 2 of 3 with So far and 0 of 3 without, where without it she spoke of Cob's crown not buying his word. Not kept, for the reasons above.
- **The cost:** $0.059 by the stack's ledger for the two games (with their reviews and boundaries), and $0.021 for the replays and the So far probe outside it: $0.080 in all.

##### Checked

- `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1028 of 1028 (the 1023 before, and the note's four, the review rewriting a line rather than adding one, the co-GM's notes; the broadcast's test went with it); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings) … no problems".
- The tests named in the task: the note's update rewriting rather than appending (notes.test.ts, and the remember endpoint's second review), the co-GM's input's notes section, and a person with no notes (gmNotes undefined, no section when nobody on stage has any, Cob left out when Kell has some).
- `npm run e2e -- --port 3790 --ai-port 9190 --root runs/e2e-notes` (namespace mode; the QA round's stack held 3330 and 8817): PASS, every step in 408.7 s (346 checks): step 22's review noted Kell's line ("note kell seen: added …") into her notes, the GM's, with no memories beside them; step 31's notes on her after the yard had the ending's standing and memory; step 33's Heron co-GM was given them, with that she was there when the Rat ended; step 39's talk greeting was told them; step 19's longest gap in A's stream 1165 ms of the 5000 allowed.

##### Open

- **Reviews of consecutive turns run at once,** and the later may not see the earlier's lines: it's told to note only its own moment, but a paraphrase of a line already there can still go in beside it. Two processes writing one game's notes could lose a line (the AI service is one process today).
- **The lead is in the second person** ("Kell tells you…") inside a note in the third.
- **What they did fills up over a long story** (eight lines, three or so a boundary): by the King's morning the yard's ending has given way, which the hand-offs still carry.
- **The talk plan's first-person `memory` field** is unread, for the talks job to drop or use. *Dropped with the old talk's dead code (below, "The old scene steps, deleted").*
- **Someone invented in play** (no cast id) has no notes.

#### Talks with Kell, on two-part (`claude/v2-salvage-talks`)

A word with whoever walks with the player was the last thing the old pipeline's turn played: talk.sift and talk.plan choosing what the companion might confide, then decide, the old reply and the old settle, with staged secrets (guard stages), the suggested lines and the talk record. A talk plays on the two-part steps now, as a scene does, whatever the campaign says, so nothing in the game asks decide, consider, repair, the old reply or the old settle; the opening still chooses, the record still keeps what's been told, and the template, its ids kept, is a two-part scene. The commits:
- `9f68b0a` **The template:** the start `talk` has the companion's opening role (a `cast` note for `{companion}`, the co-GM's note -1); `told` is a discovery whose revelation and paths each talk's prep fills, its way in (`confided`) taken once the revelation has landed; `goodbye` stays the one ending, its way in from the start. A scene patch fills a discovery for this play of the scene (`fill`) or drops a node with its routes (`drop`), in effectiveScene, so both sides see the same talk; talkSceneDocument fills the template's keys too; `isFarewell` and `GOODBYE`; the campaign check's talk rules and scene-format.md as they are now.
- `ad7a5cd` **The AI service:** with `twoPart` on a talk's requests, the greeting is the mouthpiece's (`two-part.opening`, from the opening role and `talkOpening`) beside the sift and the plan; `prepareTalk` fills `told` (its revelation, and two paths: how it shows, free when the talk comes near it, and the heart of it with the first of it, earned as readily as the plan says) and puts the confide on the companion's card as the scenes' cards keep a secret (`Shows: … Opens: … First: '…'`, then the thing itself); with nothing to confide, or a prep that fails, `told` is dropped. A talk's reply has no dice (`TALK_FORMAT`, a schema whose check is null) and its closing never waits; the companion's lore entry goes with the people and what they remember, each memory whole, in the per-turn note and the co-GM's input (`talkLore`, `talkMemories`); the settle is the co-GM beside the talk's lines, and once the co-GM has taken the way into `told` the companion's memory of telling it is written, once. The fake co-GM walks a talk only where `coGm.endings` says; the canned GM's talk keeps nothing back.
- `9691429` **The simulation:** `playsTwoPart` is true for every talk; `openTalk` gives the companion their opening role and asks the two-part GM; the goodbye said in so many words is taken as the turn starts (the reply to it is the closing, no settle after); the talk's settle (`talkSettled`) takes the co-GM's moves and roles on the rows with no world, its move into `told` putting the confide on the record at the talk's end, its move into `goodbye` (a goodbye in the player's own words) ending the talk there; the talk's own lines, not the suggest job's.
- `ad255f3` **The e2e and the docs:** step 39 on the two-part talk, with a second talk's goodbye; the two-part design's As built, the AI README, the README, ARCHITECTURE.md and the dev host's README and fake.json. `4524a56` `npm run status` shows a talk's discovery patches (the probe found it showing "undefined keeps back").

##### Judgement calls

- **The sift and the plan stay** (nothing simpler): the two-part GM plays what it's given, but doesn't choose what a companion opens up about across talks, keep out what they've already told anywhere (in a scene as well as a talk), or hold the deepest back until the third talk; the two calls do, and the prototype found a model choosing on its own couldn't be trusted to keep either. What the two-part GM made redundant is the staging: the plan's `ready` (the guard stage a talk started from) and its `shows` and `opens` now fill the discovery's paths and the card's tell, and the co-GM stages it through the companion's role, the next piece only. The plan gains `first` (the first of it they'd say), as the scenes' cards have a "First:" for the co-GM to stage with, and its `ready` reads how near an earlier talk came, in that talk's words.
- **The discovery is filled by a patch, not a template written per talk.** The talk's scene is built from the template on both sides (the simulation's def, the AI service's from lore), and the prep only exists once the open has run; a patch is what both already read through effectiveScene, so `takeRoute` on the rows and on the co-GM's copy agree. `fill` replaces a discovery's revelation and paths (the latest word wins); `drop` takes a node out with every route into and out of it.
- **Nothing to confide drops the discovery** rather than leaving the template's words in place: a co-GM whose targets say "the one thing they're keeping back" steers the companion's role towards confiding something, invented if need be. The old talk had no way into `told` without a confide, which is the same thing.
- **Staged secrets are gone from a talk, and the record keeps what's told:** the co-GM's role stages it, as the scenes' roles stage their secrets, so nothing judges a guard stage after the reply and nothing writes the record's `stages` any more. A record from before still counts: its stage for the note makes the plan's readiness readier (`readiness`), and `toldIt` reads an old talk's route or settled guard beside the discovery reached.
- **No dice in a talk:** the old talk had none (its approach: no considered rolls, no freeform checks), and what a companion tells, and when, is a matter of how the player treats them, never a roll. The reply format says `check` is always null, the schema holds it to that, and a check the model writes anyway is dropped; the confide's paths have no checks.
- **The goodbye said in so many words is the simulation's to see** (`isFarewell`: the talk's last choice, or the same words typed, give or take case, quotes and stops), as the decider's attempt judged it before the reply: the way into `goodbye` is taken as the turn starts, so the reply is the closing (`two-part.closing`, which never waits for a talk) and the talk ends with it. On the fake it now closes the talk, where the fake decider couldn't tell a goodbye from any other line. A goodbye in the player's own words is the co-GM's to see, after the reply that answered it, and the talk ends at that settle: the reply was the farewell, and a closing reply would need the player to speak again after saying goodbye.
- **The companion's lore and memories are the talk's own block, not disclosure.** The old talk's GM had the docs service's memory (their lore entry, their memory index, and what disclosure opened of it on each turn, in decide); a two-part talk runs no disclosure, so `talkLore` puts their lore entry with the people (headings a level down, without the "Read when relevant" links nothing would open) and `talkMemories` puts every memory in the per-turn note and the co-GM's input by its title and when it was, the latest 16 whole. The memory job (`claude/v2-salvage-memory`) may give the two-part GM memories for every scene; where it does, the talk's block is the companion's part of it, to fold into theirs when the two meet. *Changed at the merge: the memories are the GM's notes on them now, `talkNotes` in the per-turn note, and the co-GM has them among the notes on the people here (below, "Merged").* A talk's withheld note (the deepest, before its time) was disclosure's to keep out: it's still recorded, and still named in the facts as not this talk's, but nothing would have opened it.
- **The fake co-GM walks a talk only where `coGm.endings` says** (`told` among them, so the recipe can name a discovery): its first good ending is `goodbye`, so walking to it would end every fake talk on its first line. A talk is known to it by its start, the template's `[talk]`.
- **A talk's calls are the two-part GM's** (`two-part.opening`, `.reply`, `.closing`, `.co-gm`) beside its own (`talk.sift`, `talk.plan`, `talk.suggest`), so the call log and the ledger read as a scene's do; the key's scene says it's a talk.
- **The old pipeline's talk paths stay, unreached,** as asked: the AI service still answers a talk request without `twoPart` the old way, and the simulation's old commits keep their talk branches. What that leaves unreferenced is listed below for the cleanup.
- **Walking on is refused while a turn is in flight, as it was**, and the settle is longer now: the co-GM takes 3 to 8 s on real models (3.1 to 3.6 s in the probe below, 4.6 to 7.3 s in M2's live game in the yard), where the old after-reply read took 1 to 2 s. The e2e waits for the lines before walking on, as before.

##### Tests

- **Rewritten for the two-part talk, keeping what each checked for:** the town's AI tests (ai/service/endpoints/town.test.ts: the open's greeting, now the mouthpiece's with her role, the facts, her lore and memories and no dice, beside the sift and the plan, the card and the filled discovery, the deepest withheld; the third talk; the settle's co-GM beside the lines, with no world and nothing remembered until it's told; the memory written once when the co-GM has it out; the canned GM's talk), the simulation's (simulation/src/map/acts.test.ts: the talk opening with her role and its prep, a line on the two-part steps with the co-GM's role and the talk's own lines, the lines kept less the one said, walking on with the Rat untouched and the record counted), the talk's prep (ai/gm/talk.test.ts), the template's filling and the campaign check's talk rules (shared/scene/talk.test.ts, hosts/dev/campaign.test.ts), and the e2e's step 39.
- **New:** the goodbye said closing a talk with its reply and no settle after, what she confided on her record, and a goodbye in the player's own words ending a talk at its settle; an act at Kell walking with you opening a talk whose first turn the rules resolve with no decide; `isFarewell`; the talk's routes with its discovery filled or dropped (`fill`, `drop`); `toldIt` by the discovery reached; `talkLore` and `talkMemories`; a talk on the two-part GM's AI side (no dice, a check written anyway dropped, the lore and memories in both parts, the fake co-GM walking a talk only where its recipe says); the campaign check's new rules; `npm run status`'s talk patches.

##### The probe

On real models (`openai/gpt-6-luna`), a dev stack of its own (`KM_AI_DAILY_USD=0.14 npm run dev -- --port 3767 --ai-port 9267 --root runs/dev-talks --fresh`), played with `npm run play`: the ready-made ranger walked into the yard, said two lines there (to Kell, who said she'd met Prout; a look at the body, a Perception check passed), and ended it as the owner on murder by persons unknown; then, back on the map, a talk with Kell of four lines and the goodbye. The talk's transcript, calls and the ledger are in `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/v2/talk-probe`.
- **The opening:** the sift held back her deepest (her note on Wendell Prout, for a later talk); the plan chose why she keeps letting Cob go (her note on Cob), ready when asked, first "He's been up twice.", and found what she'd told in the yard ("She knew Prout by sight; he kept the Granary Roll; She had met Prout"); the discovery filled with it, the card with the tell. The greeting, from her opening role: "Sergeant Kell stands beneath the granary eaves, sharpening her pencil with a clasp knife while the town carries on around you. She looks up, still as a post, and closes the knife. 'Investigator.'" First words in 1.1 s, the turn done in 4.2 s (the plan, 3.9 s, beside the greeting's 1.9 s).
- **Line 1,** small talk about her notes: "No. I used to keep them in my head. … The head is less reliable under questioning." The co-GM's role for her held it back: "Keep her attention to Cob's oversized boots for when the talk turns there, but do not reveal why she has let him go unless Wren asks."
- **Line 2,** "Do you know Cob?": the first of it and the heart, as readily as asked: "He's been up twice. … Pilfering. I let him go both times. The Mending House is not improved by adding hungry children to it." The co-GM took the way into told after it ("Kell told Wren she…"), and her role kept the rest for a follow-up (a good lad with boots that fit and a hot dinner, if asked); her memory of telling it was written once ("Told Wren about why she keeps letting Cob go": "I told Wren why I let Cob go: I don't think the Mending House is improved by adding hungry children to it. I didn't say I knew what was in those boots.").
- **Line 3,** the follow-up: the rest of it, "A boy who might turn out decent, with boots that fit and a hot dinner. … The shoes make me less certain what he knows. They don't make him guilty."; her role moved past it ("don't repeat it").
- **Line 4,** pressed on Prout, the deepest held back: "There is. … Another time, investigator."; her role held that line for the rest of the talk.
- **The goodbye** ("I'll leave you to it.", say_goodbye) closed the talk with her reply, "Kell gives a small nod and opens her notebook again. 'Noted.' She falls into step beside you…", then "Scene ends: Goodbye for now", in 1.4 s, with no settle after. Her record: count 1, told why she keeps letting Cob go (her Cob note).
- **Latency, the turn rows:** first words 0.8 to 0.9 s on each line, each turn done (the co-GM's settle in) in 4.9 to 5.4 s; the reply 1.2 to 1.7 s, then the co-GM 3.1 to 3.6 s in all (two rounds: its calls, then "done") beside the lines' 1.4 s.
- **Cost:** $0.0224 for the talk in 55 calls, about half of it the memory reviews running after each line (the opening $0.0025 with the sift and the plan; each line's reply $0.0005 to $0.0012, the co-GM's $0.0006 to $0.0014, the lines' $0.0001 to $0.0002; the closing $0.0006); $0.0436 for the whole probe, the yard's opening, two lines and boundary included.
- **What it showed beyond the talk's own working,** not changed here: talk.suggest's lines (told the topic, to "lead towards it without naming it") named it after line 1, before the player could know it ("You've let Cob slip away before. Why keep giving him chances?"); and one reply left its second line untagged ("nobody can tell" on the screen).

##### Open

- **The memory job and the talk's memories.** `talkMemories` reads the memory reviews' memories (each companion's `memories/index.md`); the memory job (`claude/v2-salvage-memory`) may give the two-part GM what people remember in every scene, by another shape (its scratch has the GM's notes and a "So far" line on each card). When the two meet, the talk's block should become the companion's part of theirs, or go where the cards carry it. *Done at the merge (below, "Merged").*
- **talk.suggest names the topic** it's told to lead towards (the probe, above): its instruction, unchanged here, might say the line asks after what showed, never names it.
- **Walking on waits for the settle,** as it did, and the settle is the co-GM's now (3 to 4 s in the probe, the turn done about 5 s after the line).
- **M3's rules** (shared/scene/rules.ts on its branch) need the talk template's rules as the campaign check has them now (the start's role, the discovery `told`, a way into `goodbye` from the start), and the plugin's editor shows `told` as a discovery whose words each talk fills. *Done at M3's second restack merge (under M3, "On M2's QA fixes, the salvage and the cleanup").*

#### Merged

Both branches were cut from `395b73e` and merged into M2 in turn, each as a merge commit: the memory job's (`claude/v2-salvage-memory`) first, which went in with nothing to resolve, then the talks' (`claude/v2-salvage-talks`). The talks' job wrote its two-part talk against the memories the memory job replaced (`talkMemories` read each companion's `memories/index.md`, and `rememberTold` saved a memory), so the merge is where the talk was moved onto the GM's notes. Nine files conflicted, and both sides are kept in each:
- **ai/gm/talk.ts:** the talk reads and writes the GM's notes. `rememberTold` is the memory job's (a line under what they did, "Told Wren about the door at the Drowned Rat, in a word between scenes (…)", written through `note`, which keeps a line it has already, so it's written once), and the talks' settle calls it as before when the co-GM's move into `told` comes or the discovery's reached. `talkMemories` became `talkNotes`: the GM's notes on the companion (`gmNotes`) under "## The GM's notes on <name> from play (what's happened with them)", with the old block's line to let them show and never recite them, "(nothing yet)" when play hasn't given them any. The plan reads the notes for how things stand (the memory job's), and the source header and the talk's description have both jobs' sentences.
- **ai/gm/two-part.ts:** the co-GM's input keeps the memory job's notes on the people here (`notesOnPeople`), which in a talk is the companion's notes, so the talks' separate block in the co-GM's input is gone; the per-turn note has `talkNotes` where it had `talkMemories`; the source header has both jobs' sentences, and `notesOnPeople` says a talk's mouthpiece has the companion's too.
- **The tests** (ai/gm/talk.test.ts, two-part.test.ts, ai/service/endpoints/town.test.ts): the talks' cases, on the notes: the opening's greeting told her notes (a line under what she's seen), the settle's co-GM given them among the notes on the people here, nothing under what she did until it's told and one line when it is, written once however often it's found told; the staged secrets' absence kept beside the memory job's notes check.
- **The e2e's step 39** (hosts/e2e/town.ts): the talks' checks of the mouthpiece's greeting from her opening role, and the memory job's check of her notes in it (the memory review's line and what the yard's ending says of her) under the talk's heading.
- **The docs:** README.md (the memory job's notes and `--notes`, the talks' line that nothing plays the old pipeline but the tests' campaign), ai/README.md (the talks' rows for the reply and the settle with the told line in the notes, the fake's options with both, the talks' "Left out still" with the memory job's lite memory gone), hosts/dev/README.md and fake.json's about (the memory job's notes review, the talks' two-part talk on the fake with the told line in her notes), two-part-design.md (the talk's notes in its per-turn note; the mouthpiece reads none in a scene), and this log (one introduction, each job's part whole, the notes where the talks' part said memories marked as changed here).

##### Judgement calls

- **A talk's mouthpiece reads the companion's notes, a scene's reads none.** The memory job kept notes out of the mouthpiece in a scene (the prep's hand-offs carry the scenes before; a "So far:" line was recited; the cards are in its instructions, where a changed note spoils the prompt cache), and the talks' job put what the companion remembers in the per-turn note, as the old talk's GM had their memory index, since a talk is all about the companion and how things stand with them. The per-turn note is the input, not the instructions, so the cache holds; the block's line says not to recite them; and both jobs' e2e checks hold the greeting to having them. The co-GM reads them once, among the notes on the people here.
- **`talkNotes` shows "(nothing yet)"** when the companion has no notes, as the talks' block did for no memories, so a first talk is told there's nothing behind it rather than nothing being said.
