The e2e and the rest of the battery ran after the deletions (below).

#### The old scene steps, deleted

With memory kept as the GM's notes and talks on the two-part GM, nothing in the game played the old pipeline's scene steps: only campaign.json's `gm: "pipeline"`, which only the tests' packs set, reached decide, consider, repair, the old reply and the old settle. They're deleted here, a part a commit, from the talks job's list above ("What the cleanup can delete"), each checked again by its callers; the spell-and-blow handling the user parked for combat stays (below, "Kept, parked for combat"). The line counts are each commit's own, the docs it changed included and this log left out.
- **The old talk's dead code** (−339 +95 in 24 files): the old talk opening (`gm.talk.opening`) and settle (`talkSettle`, `afterReply`'s `staging`), so a talk is the two-part GM's on the AI side whatever its request says (an open with `talk`, a settle in a scene with a companion); the simulation's `afterTalkSettle` and the reply commit's tests for a talk; staged secrets whole: the guard questions after a reply (`guardHolders`, `guard_<id>`, `readAfter`'s `guard`), the GM's note of how far people have opened up (`GUARD_NOTE`, `guardNote`), `guardAfter`, the prep's starting stage (`TalkPrep.guard`, `prepared`'s), `After.guard`, `TurnView.guard`, and `GmState.guard` with the gm_state `guard` column and its `Guard` type (a schema change: the bindings regenerated); a talk's decision's settings (`talkSettings`); the talk rule in `sceneOf` (a talk's ways into its ending as anytime attempts, its way into told left out of the transitions), which only the old pipeline read; and the plan's first-person `memory` field (`Confide.memory`, a field of the gm_state `Confide` type), which nothing read once the told line became the GM's notes' own words. `toldIt` takes no companion now (it was for their guard). A talk record's `stages` stay, read for records from before (`readiness`), and `endTalk` still drops a note's stage once it's told; it writes none. Tests gone with it: the talk's route and decision (talk settings, old route), staged secrets, "as a Scene, a talk is what the template was", and the talk halves of two more (the anytime goodbye in document.test.ts and talk.test.ts).
- **The campaign switch, and the tests that played the old pipeline** (−1049 +342 in 32 files): `CampaignFile.gm` and `playsTwoPart` are gone, so every scene opens with the two-part GM's roles, starts its turns with twoPartStart (`startPlan` has no old branch, and a turn's first phase is always `replying`, so `firstPhase` went) and queues no co-GM review after them (`afterTurn` queues the memory review alone, and takes only the turn's ids); the simulation's test pack (`caerwynPack`: `twoPart` gone from its options) and the local host's tests play the two-part GM on the tests' own hanged clerk. Of the 69 tests the switch failed, 37 moved onto the two-part GM, keeping what each tested that's still there: the job loop's twelve (jobs.test.ts: the reaper, a status that won't mend (now a settle that fails, the reply committed before), a commit that throws (a check with no outcomes to write), a late answer dropped, a lost dispatch, ten minutes failing, the budget, the jobs beside a turn, `dispatch_waiting`) and real-play's two, on the reply; a line's plumbing (say.test.ts: said at once, replied with its check rolled from the game's dice with the sheet's modifier, settled, each step's request carrying what the commit before it did, a line waiting while one settles, the same seed rolling the same dice); the refusals while a turn replies or a line waits already; a stream post dropped while the turn settles; `end_scene` while the reply's in flight; the map's ending (map.test.ts's spectator, walking out after it and carrying into the Rat, now ended by the co-GM's `make-case` and the closing); the map's act as turn 1 (acts.test.ts: resolved by the rules as it starts, its mechanic on the turn and its directive the reply's); the first check's dice for the prototype's seed (rules.test.ts, the seed 4242 written in, since the golden recording it was read from goes with the parity test); the lines (a closing that waits asks for them, one that closes doesn't; a set for a scene that's over is dropped); the memory reviews (the closing's turn remembered); the world bridge (world.test.ts: the map's act resolving a spell at a thing as its turn starts, a blow and a hold left hanging landing as the next turn starts, a Shield raised against the blow, fire burning on, the rules' state kept turn to turn, the act queued as the map's menu queues one); and the local host's line, dice and story (the Rat's two-part opening told the story so far in its prep). 32 went, having tested only the old pipeline: consider.test.ts (5) and endings.test.ts (16) whole (considered rolls; the endings the decider found, held, wound down, answered out of character, the same push again, the improvised ending, walking out), the co-GM's reviews merged at a turn's start (2), the local host's endings as the prototype played them (7), and the world bridge's fights that undid the turn's ending or skipped the ruling (2). The reactions to violence, parked for combat, are tested where the parked code is reachable now: `reactionsTakeEffect` (exported for it) on an act the rules resolved, the fight it starts leaving the first fighter's blow, which the next turn's start lands. The docs that named the switch say it's gone.
- **The old reply and settle paths** (−1297 +213 in 43 files, and the GM steps' golden recordings, −24233 lines of JSON in 16 files): the reply endpoint is the two-part GM's alone (its directives rebuilt from the decide commit's decision went, with `directivesFor`, `replyOperation` and every word only it said: the held ending, the out-of-character hold, the dice decided, the stuck loop, the earned payoff, the bill, the limit's "no, but", the plain check, the last chance before a close), and so is the settle (the director's read after the reply went: `afterQuestions`, `readAfter`, the status, ending, landed, left, outcome, ask and own-exit questions); in the simulation, the reply and settle commits are the two-part GM's (`afterReply`, `afterSettle` and their parts, the wind-down's old close, walking out of the place) and the bookkeeping that applied the read (`moveOnAfterReply`, `endingReached`, `whyItWaits`, `arrivals`, `reachEnding`, `movedOn`). With them went the `twoPart` markers on the reply and settle requests and the say plan's flag, `ReplyRequest.decision`, `After` and `SettleResponse.after`, `Outcome.wasStuck`, and the bookkeeping only they kept: `GmState.endingLead`, `sceneStatus` and `repeated` with their gm_state columns and the `EndingLead` type (a schema change: the bindings regenerated), `ReplyResponse.gm.repeated` and `SceneStatus`; the suggested lines' way out no longer reads an ending lead. Tests: the reply's backstory, the settle's operations by game key, the streamed speakers, the history's names and the story read by the lines alone moved onto the two-part reply and settle; the old reply's directives and wind-down, the settle's read and its closed-scene case, the last chance before a close (crossing.test.ts), the after-reply reads (director.test.ts), the ruled turn's and the plain check's replies and the left-the-place read (consider.test.ts) and the act's and reactions' directives in the old reply (world.test.ts) went.
- **The decide endpoint's route and presence questions** (−1357 +345 in 60 files): `POST /v1/turns/decide` and its step are gone (the endpoint, its route, its request check, its canned and scripted answers, `DecideRequest` and `DecideResponse`, its `STEP_ENDPOINT` and `STEP_TIMEOUT_MS` entries, its key's step and its fault's name), and with it the turn's one decision before the reply: `beforeQuestions` and `readBefore` with every question only they asked (the route's attempt and its means, the cue and its counted condition, the roll call, the same push again, a plan off the graph, earned, daft and the bill, an answer to an ending on the brink, a limit) and their confidence bars, `withMechanic`, `rulingOf` (the forced act's read), `outOfCharacter` (the wind-down's one question) and `sameAgainDirective`; in the simulation, the decide commit (`afterDecide`, `decideOutcome`, `windDownOutcome`, `routeMove`, its `takeRoute`, `diceLine`, `breakOut`), the bookkeeping only it read (`routeOf`, `wantsReview`), the say plan's `decide` step and `then`, `Outcome.move`, and the turn's `deciding` phase, in the client too (its phase, the spinner's and the deed's tests of it, and the demo's canned turn, which goes from queued to replying as a two-part turn does). What decide asked of the rules stays, parked for combat: the mechanic question and the act's details call as `mechanicQuestion`, `readMechanic` and `mechanicOf` (gm/director.ts), what they offer from the sheet and the world as `rulesOptions` (gm/mechanics.ts, moved from the endpoint), and the route an act would hold back (`alsoQuestion`) and the act that's only the means to something risky (`meansQuestion`) as extra questions that ride along in the details call; the reactions step's commit goes on to the two-part reply, and `takeAct` takes no move. The map's act was resolved without decide already (twoPartStart). Tests: 21 went, having tested only decide (16 of director.test.ts's: the route's act, a sure route, the means among the social skills, two routes sharing a read, the same push again twice, the cues four times, the bill, the brink, a route's roll held behind the rules' act three times, the considered roll the rules settle; and decide's read of a roll to rule on in consider.test.ts, its route, memories and wind-down in pipeline.test.ts, its forced act in town.test.ts and a route held behind a blow in world.test.ts); 2 are new, on the parked `mechanicOf` (a line's act with its details, a rest with none, an unsure read; the extra questions in the details call); world.test.ts's cast at the hoist rope moved onto `mechanicOf` and `rulesOptions` with the director's decider on the stand-in; the server's tests use the settle where they used decide as any think, and the protocol's, the job loop's and the QA loop's tests a live step's key. `Roll` moved to dice-row.ts, its one reader), the say plan's consider step and what it carried (`decision`, `pendingExit`, `ruling`, `verdict`, `route`, `withheld`), the say plan's `onStage` (left over from the old settle's `whyItWaits`), `Outcome`'s `taken`, `held`, `pending`, `closes`, `ruled` and `freeform`, and the bookkeeping only they kept, `GmState.rulings` and `pendingExit` (the ending held for the player's answer) with their gm_state columns and the `RulingRow` and `PendingExit` types (a schema change: the bindings regenerated), so the stage manager's settled facts are the scene's facts alone and the suggested lines' way out fits on a closing or after four lines; The prompts and AI READMEs say so, and the story-so-far test no longer reads the actor's and the narrator's words.

In all, from the talks' merge to here: −31495 +1259 in 173 files, of which −24233 is the GM steps' golden recordings (−7261 +1255 in 157 files without them), the log left out. npm test went from 1036 tests to 952 and the screens from 685 checks to 677: of the tests that went, each tested only what went with it; the rest moved onto the two-part GM or the parked code (above, commit by commit).

##### Kept, parked for combat

The user's call after QA round qa1-1018: the spell-and-blow handling stays for the combat work. Nothing in a scene of the story or a talk reaches it now (the two-part GM narrates a spell or a blow said in words; only the map's act, its mechanic chosen already, is resolved by the rules, as its turn starts), so where it's reachable only from tests, ai/README.md's "Parked for combat" says what it is and where its tests are:
- **What the rules resolve of a line:** the mechanic question and the act's details call, as the old decide asked them, now `mechanicQuestion`, `readMechanic` and `mechanicOf` (ai/gm/director.ts), with `rulesOptions` (moved from the decide endpoint), `mechanicDetails`, `jumpDetails`, `alsoQuestion`, `meansQuestion`, `aimable` and `namedThing` (ai/gm/mechanics.ts). Tested in director.test.ts (the question then the details, a rest with none, an unsure read, the extra questions riding along, a spell's aim) and the AI service's world.test.ts (a cast at the hoist rope, the world in the details call).
- **The threat a reply leaves hanging:** `threatQuestions` and `readThreat` (ai/gm/director.ts), from the old settle's read; shared/rules/threats.ts still lands one as a turn starts (the rules first, live every turn), and `gm.threat`, `held` and `brawl` stay, though nothing sets a threat now.
- **The reactions to the player's violence:** `POST /v1/turns/reactions` (ai/service/endpoints/reactions.ts), `decideReactions`, `reactionDirective`, `witnessesOf`, `nameIn` (ai/gm/mechanics.ts), the simulation's reactions step (`afterReactions`, which goes on to the two-part reply, and `reactionsTakeEffect`, exported for its test) with `SayPlan.act`, and shared/rules/scene-acts.ts `react` and `violence`. `Outcome.brawl` is still set when a fight breaks out, though no reply reads it until combat does.
- **The rules' act itself** (say-commits.ts `takeAct`, shared/rules/acts.ts and scene-acts.ts) is live: the map's act goes through it.
- **fight.md**, for a line typed in a fight (V13).

##### Judgement calls

- **"The decide endpoint's route and presence questions."** Decide asked no presence question of its own: who's here was only its decision's state (`characters_present`). The boundary's `director.presence` (gm/presence.ts `settlePresence`, who's in the next scene) is the story's, and stays.
- **Bookkeeping went with the steps that kept it,** columns and all, rather than staying empty: `rulings` and `pendingExit` with considered rolls, the old co-GM's `canon`, `notes`, `notesTurn`, `whispers`, `lastRepairTurn`, `offGraphTurn` and `oocTurns` with its repair, `opened` and `withheld` with disclosure, the `co_gm_result` table with the repair. Each is a schema change (the bindings regenerated each time); a dev database is wiped on an incompatible publish, and nothing outside the tests' stacks holds one. The move registry's append-only rule gives way for the repair move for the same reason. `patches` stays: the boundary and a talk write them; so does `ScenePatch`'s whole shape, the format's.
- **The client's phases went with the simulation's:** `deciding` with decide and `considering` (with `CONSIDERING`, the prototype's line for it) with considered rolls, since the turn row can't have them; the demo's canned turn goes from queued to replying, as a two-part turn does.
- **The old opening went with disclosure,** the only thing that ran it: nothing has sent an open without `twoPart` since the campaign switch, so the flag went too and every opening is the two-part GM's.
- **Leftovers folded in where they belonged:** the say plan's `onStage` (the old settle's `whyItWaits`) with considered rolls, `breakOut` cut to the brawl flag with decide (it only undid an ending decide's move had reached), the model client's `finalAnswer` and the fake's `stall` with the ruling (their only user), and the graph's and directives' helpers whose last caller went, in a commit of their own at the end.
- **Tests:** a test that played the old pipeline went when what it tested went; one that tested something still there moved onto the two-part GM or the parked code (the server's tests use the settle as any think; the opening's on the mouthpiece; the cast at the hoist rope on `mechanicOf`). The story test of an opening after a boundary went rather than moved: it played the prototype's Rat, which only the old GM could open; the local host's story test has the two-part opening after a boundary.
- **The request check** (ai/service/requests.ts `GM_LISTS`) named the bookkeeping's lists by hand, so dropping a column there is two changes: the type check can't see it, and the tests caught it (every turn a 400) when the old co-GM's went.

##### Open

- **The spinner's lines** (client/busy.ts `GM_BUSY`) show only while a turn is queued behind another: a two-part turn starts replying, and a reply with no words yet says "The GM is speaking…". So since M2 the player rarely sees them; nothing here changed it.
- **Dead code older than this cleanup** turned up in the same pass and was left: `beatOrExit` (shared/scene/types.ts), `entryAt` (simulation/src/transcript.ts), and the markup stats (`sumStats`, `emptyStats`: ai/gm/markup.ts, the notes on marks that went in M2).

##### Checked

After every deletion, on ports of their own (never 3330 or 8817): `npm run check`, the type check clean, npm test 952 of 952 and the screens 677 of 677; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn`, "6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems"; `npm run e2e` (3410 and 8910), every step passing in 409.1 s, 358 checks; `npm run arc` (3420 and 8920), 15 of 16, the fight's own check off until V12 as before; `npm run build:web` then `npm run web:check`, 80 checks passing; `npm run build:site`, the pitch, the roadmap and the game, 28 files; and a fake loop round of two games (`loop stack up --fake` on 3430 and 8930, the hanged clerk and the Rat, four turns each at most): both started, three lines said in the clerk and two at the Rat, their review packs and report made, 37 and 36 calls with no errors and none to decide, consider or repair (the keys' steps: pick, open, reply, settle, remember, and the boundary on the way to the Rat).
### Fixes from QA round qa2-1018

QA round qa2-1018, the confirmation round after round 1's fixes, played eight games of M2's scenes on the two-part GM (build `395b73e`); its folder (`runs/loop/qa2-1018` in the `claude/v2-scenes` worktree) has the games, the call logs, and the triage's verdicts with the why of each finding in its triage.json. What it found went to fix jobs, each cut from M2's branch at `395b73e`, and each job's part is its own heading below. They were merged one at a time after the old pipeline's salvage and the old scene steps' deletion (above), so each went onto the cleaned-up code; what the merges changed is under "Merged, and what was checked" at the end.

#### The co-GM (`claude/v2-fix2-cogm`)

What failed: **H5** (g08, the Heron, severity 5): at turn 16 Kell said the words of arrest over the open true book ("Master Josiah Sallow, I arrest you for theft from the Crown"), Sallow snatching for the ledger with one Crake between them and the other's hand in his coat. The co-GM saw it ("while Sallow remains defiant and the Crakes are not yet dealt with") and rang the second bell anyway, because it was overdue and round 1's fix had told it so and written "whatever is happening" into the cue: the reply to the player's Grease spell closed the Heron on "The barge sails", mid-arrest. **g01/1** (the clerk, severity 3, round 1's g02/9 again): the co-GM took the required Hobb lead on Cob's description of the big man ("like a door, with a bandage on his right hand"), so Big Hobb Mallory and the Drowned Rat were first said in Kell's closing line; her card says she names him as soon as the description reaches her. And g08's turn 15 took the true book on a reason that denied it ("The ledger has not been read…"), which round 1's refusal didn't catch.

What changed:
- **Endings first, and a cue into a bad ending waits on the good and mixed ones** (`6fe7f83`). The tools section reads the open routes into endings before the rest: one whose condition has happened, as written, is taken in that settle, ahead of any cue, and one about to happen (the arrest made but not yet secured, a hand on the cane but no blade out) isn't yet. A cue into a bad ending is brought about only when no good or mixed ending's condition has happened and the player isn't in the middle of bringing one about; its overdue line says so ("Overdue: bring it about now, unless a good or mixed ending's condition has happened, which is taken instead, or the player is in the middle of bringing one about, which comes first"), and so does a move's "still overdue". Its first move in a turn while a good or mixed ending is open is held, and those endings' conditions are put to the co-GM (`heldText`): take one instead, leave the cue while the moment plays out, or move it again, when it's taken. A cue two replies from its limit is due soon, and can be felt coming once in someone's role (the tide, which g08 never felt). The Heron's second bell rings "whatever else is happening; only the Master being caught at it right then comes first". scene-format.md and the format's type say what a limit means now.
- **A discovery only once its heart has landed** (`4c0ffef`). The tools section: a discovery's details and examples needn't all come out, but its heart must: what it says happened, and when it points to someone the player must go and find, who they are by name and where to find them; someone described but not named is part of it, and whoever holds the rest as their card says (a sergeant who names a man as soon as his description reaches her) is given it in their role for the next reply, unasked where the card says so. `unsaidIn` finds the people a revelation names from the story's other scenes whose names nobody has said anywhere the player has been told: the co-GM's targets say so ("Nobody has said Big Hobb Mallory's name in the story yet."), and its first move into such a discovery in a turn is held (`unsaidText`), to be taken again only if the name is a detail of it. The settle gives the co-GM's view the campaign's story from the first scene on, and the fake co-GM takes a held move again once.
- **More of the ways a reason denies itself** (`729ed2d`): "has not been read, explained, named or identified", "no one, nobody, no witness or neither … has yet named, identified, sworn, said, told…", "has not occurred".
- **The probe tries turns the round's co-GM never saw open** (`7a27c12`): `--reply` asks the turn's reply again first and the co-GM about it, `--drop` leaves the round's moves on a route out of the rebuilt bookkeeping, `--reply-text` tries the co-GM on a reply of your own, `--band` sets a check's outcome, and `--role` shows the role each try set for someone.

##### Judgement calls

- **H5's premise, checked: the arrest at turn 16 hadn't met "Caught in the act".** Its route's condition, the author's since the re-authoring, says Sallow must be held and his Crakes stood down or dealt with, and "The words of arrest alone, with Sallow defiant and his Crakes still beside him, aren't this: that is the moment he draws"; the co-GM read it so at the time. So "endings first" alone wouldn't have saved g08: what does is the bad cue waiting while the player is in the middle of bringing a good or mixed ending about. The condition is kept: the arrest plays out, into the arrest secured or the fight (whose stand-in ends with Sallow surrendering to Kell, and the scene picking up for the arrest). Taking it at the words, as the triage's premise had it, would mean rewriting the author's condition.
- **The Heron's "whatever is happening" was round 1's** (`806f426`), not the author's, and it's what the co-GM quoted as it rang the bell; it's narrowed to the Master being caught at it. In-the-act's and the fight's words are untouched.
- **A hold, not a refusal, for both:** the co-GM keeps the last word (a second move is taken), at the cost of a tool round on the turns it fires. A refusal on a name nobody has said would stall the fake's walk (its replies never say Hobb) and any game where a name comes out in a way the matcher misses; a bad cue refused outright could never ring, round 1's H1 at the Heron.
- **The bad cue's hold fires whenever a good or mixed ending is open, overdue or not.** At the Heron's midnight the fight's routes are always open, so it fires every time the bell is rung: one round, once a scene. **"In the middle of" has no cap in code:** the Heron's moments under way resolve within a turn or two (into the arrest or the fight), and a cap would bring H5 back at the cap. Watch for a bell that never rings.
- **Who a revelation names is for leads only:** people of the story's other scenes (not this scene's cast, so the Heron's Scrope, whose name the player may never hear, doesn't hold back the true book), never said in the story's hooks, lines, hand-offs or chapters. Of the campaign's revelations it finds Hobb in the clerk's and the wharves' leads and Sallow in the clerk's motive and the Rat's master and Prout's page; the hold's words let the co-GM take it again where the name is a detail (the motive's 'J. Sallow' on a page), and the scenes are the campaign's (a vocabulary stub isn't), the talk template's {name} no one.
- **The note is in the targets, not on the open route:** on the route it made the co-GM call move_scene to say the name hadn't been said (3 of 4, then 2 of 6, each reason denying itself). In the targets it's for the roles.
- **The co-GM's view has the story from the first scene; the other thinks' views are as they were.** A turn view carries the campaign's story only once there's a story so far (so a first scene's thinks never read the vocabulary), so the clerk's co-GM never had it; only the co-GM's copy gets it, and it reads only the installed scenes' casts.
- **Due soon is for every cue,** two replies before its limit, a sign once, never the moment. It's weak (below) and harmless (no early bell in its turns).
- **The refusal's patterns** are kept to what both rounds' moves and the probes said, verified on the 99 moves: they refuse round 2's true book and nothing round 1's didn't.
- **The probe's reply asked again is the mouthpiece's logged request with its per-turn note rewritten in place** (the routes the rebuilt bookkeeping has open, a closing's directive out), not rebuilt: the world, the hidden things and the names are as the round logged them. A check is rolled on a bare d20 (the log hasn't the player's modifiers), and the name pass isn't run on the new reply (the co-GM reads it with the speakers' tags out).
- **Kept from round 1:** the reason-contradiction refusal and the counters (the turns, each cue's count and overdue), re-probed below.

##### The probe

The round's turns, and round 1's H1 to H3, replayed on the real model (openai/gpt-6-luna) with `npm run loop -- probe` (logs and the written replies in `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/v2/cogm2-probe`; $0.268 in all, 23 runs, every co-GM try 5 to 24 s). "Before" is this branch's base, the round's code; for round 1's turns, round 1's own figures, taken on the fixes this base has.

| Turn | What should happen | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| qa2 g08 t16 (the words of arrest, Sallow and the Crakes not held) | no barge | barge 1 of 3 (the round: 1 of 1) | barge 0 of 6: no move 4, the fight 2 |
| qa2 g08 t17, reply asked again (the Grease), the round's bell left out | no barge | (the round closed on it) | barge 0 of 6: no move 4, the fight 2 |
| qa2 g08 t17, written reply: Sallow in irons, the Crakes' hands up | caught in the act, the bell overdue | | 6 of 6 |
| qa2 g08 t17, written reply: Sallow away through the land door | the barge | | 4 of 6 (2 waited a turn) |
| qa2 g08 t16, the hold alone (the old words and condition) | no barge | | barge 0 of 6: the bell moved in 2, both held, neither moved again |
| qa2 g08 t13 to t14 (due soon) | a sign of the tide, no bell | | a sign in 1 of 6 (Hep "aware the ebb will not wait"); bell 0 of 6 |
| qa2 g01 t16 (Cob's description, no name) | no Hobb | Hobb 2 of 3 | 0 of 6 (one held, not moved again); Kell told to name him unasked in 2 |
| qa1 g02 t16 to t18, the round's Hobb move left out | no Hobb | (the round: Hobb at t16) | 0 of 9 |
| qa1 g08 t15 to t17 (H1, the Rat's bell) | the bell | (round 1's fix: 18 of 18) | 9 of 9 |
| qa1 g06 t15 (H2) | the bell, not "Hobb goes to work" | 3 of 3, 0 of 3 | 3 of 3, 0 of 3 |
| qa1 g06 t16 (H2) | Hobb talks | 3 of 3 | 3 of 3 |
| qa1 g02 t18 (H3) | no signed murder | 3 of 3 | 3 of 3 |

On the way: the rules alone held the Hobb lead in 3 of 4 (the fourth on a reason denying itself, which the wider patterns refuse); the first "endings first" took the fight at t16 in 6 of 6 (Sallow reaching for the ledger read as blades out), and "one about to happen hasn't yet" brought it to 2 of 6.

##### Checked

##### Open

- **The Heron's caught-in-the-act condition and its closing disagree:** the condition says the words of arrest with Sallow defiant aren't it, and the closing asks for "Kell's proper words, Sallow's courtesy curdling into threats". In the round's own turns Sallow was never held in any reply (t16, t17 asked again six times), so the probe never reached caught in the act there; the written reply shows it's taken once he is. The author's to decide.

#### The mouthpiece (`claude/v2-fix2-mouth`)

The mouthpiece's share was one hard failure and four patterns, three of them things round 1's fixes had missed or broken. **H4** (g06, the clerk, severity 4): the player made exactly the promise Cob's card and his path say open him with no dice; round 1's "call a check at a turning point" had the mouthpiece roll Persuasion anyway, and the failure had Cob bolt through the gate with the scene's required lead. **Setup and outcome disagreed** (g01/5, g06/2, g06/5, g06/9): the narration before a check showed what the roll was for, or who noticed, and the outcomes repeated or contradicted it. **Round 1's speaker fix had a bug** (g04/6, g04/7, g03/2, g07/5): the player's words quoted inside a sentence came out as their line, mid-sentence, and some lines were "Someone" or the wrong person's. And: the Crakes named unheard (g08/4), people reciting their own terms (g01/2, g04/3), Kell drawing the player's conclusions, answering for them, naming what the case still needed and overriding their word (five findings), closings that walked on into the next scene, moved the player or restaged the last reply (four), and a sore side the player never had (g05/4). The user's two prompts are untouched; everything is in code and what's appended.

What changed:
- `e57f1d2` **Who says a line** ([speakers.ts](../ai/gm/speakers.ts), [markup.ts](../ai/gm/markup.ts)): a quoted fragment inside a narrated sentence is nobody's line, even the player's own words echoed (withSpeakers' `player` is gone); what someone's called by a word anyone could be is its last two words ("the big man"), so Hobb's "Not tonight, Arlo." is his after "The big man’s shoulders tighten." and after "the big man says"; a line whose speech tag names someone nobody here is known as ("a voice asks") is theirs, not the lead-in's subject's (strangerTagged); a sentence naming nobody who might speak goes on about whoever its pronoun is (the mastiff at his boots leaves the watchman's line his). The review pack's "Name: " before a line said mid-sentence (g04's turns 14 and 15, rightly credited) is the pack's convention, and the reviewer's brief now says so.
- `93f17e9` **A group's named members in the name pass** ([unheard.ts](../ai/gm/unheard.ts)): each member with a capitalised name in theirs is unheard until it's heard, called it swapped for what they are ("the talking bruiser", "the silent bruiser"), swapped where it stands in the narration ("the silent Crake", "the Crakes", "One Crake’s boots", "Crake" alone), here while their leader is. The screen knows them by it, and a name that's what someone is ("the silent Crake") is no longer told by its describing word ("silent").
- `7c9f0a7` **The loop's probe asks the mouthpiece again** (`npm run loop -- probe <round> <game> <turns> --mouthpiece`, [qa/loop/probe.ts](../qa/loop/probe.ts)): before is the round's request; after has this checkout's rules and the per-turn note's parts made from the cards, the cast and the bookkeeping, on a view rebuilt from the round, with the stage, routes, established details, world, player's turn and directives the round's (a closing's directive made again). With nothing changed it reproduces the round's request byte for byte.
- `3d4161a` **Checks** ([two-part.ts](../ai/gm/two-part.ts) FORMAT, `holdersNote`, `inReach`): what the prep says for certain wins over the dice (no dice, a promise that answers someone's fear, what coin never buys); a check is for what the player has just tried, never for what the reply is still waiting for, nor what's already in view; the setup stops at the attempt and each outcome says only what the roll decided; no failure sends whoever holds a required discovery out of the scene. The per-turn note lists who here holds each required discovery not reached, and before the outcomes are kept a failure that sends one of them off is the other failure instead.
- `706f8cd` **Terms, the companion, the player character, the scene's ground, closings** (two-part.ts, [brief.ts](../ai/gm/brief.ts) `playerNow`): nobody states their own terms or says their card's words for what opens them, and the note's kept-back secrets are the GM's knowledge; the companion's five points, and the co-GM told to write her role with what she holds back every turn (`companionRoles`); no condition for the player character that play or the rules haven't given, and their state in the note only a quarter down or more; a reply follows the player only to the edge of the scene's place; a closing told what its last reply did and said first, then playing only what that reply didn't of the ending's words, where the player is.

##### Judgement calls

- **The speaker fix is in the markup's crediting, not the pack.** The turn-7 garble was a real crediting bug (the echo crediting gave the player a line mid-sentence, coloured on screen); turns 14 and 15 were lines rightly credited after a speech verb ("the landlady says, “…”"), and the pack's "Name: " convention puts the name before every line, so the reviewer's brief says a label mid-sentence is never a problem in itself. Changing the pack's format would touch review-check's quote matching and the report's marks for one cosmetic case.
- **"The big man" is the two words, not "man".** A head word anyone could be (man, woman) was dropped, and the two-word head ("big man") is Hobb's alone in every scene; "the biggest man", as the Rat's opening has it, still isn't, rather than stemming adjectives.
- **A stranger's tag makes the line nobody's we know.** "…,” a porter says" after a sentence about Arlo was Arlo's; now it's nobody's ("Someone" in the pack, the default colour on screen): a wrong colour reads worse than none.
- **Members are swapped word by word, not as names.** Their names ("the talking Crake") are descriptions with a name inside, so the pass swaps the name ("Crake") for what they are where it stands, keeping the words around it, rather than the whole phrase; what they are comes from their look ("An enormous river-bruiser…"), else a brother's who shares the name (the silent one's look says only "His identical brother"), else "man". No `called` was added to the format's members, as round 1 added none to the place's creatures.
- **The no-dice rule names its own example.** The clerk's coin-for-a-look is in the format's words ("coin offered for a look at Cob's boot gets haggling, never the look"), as round 1's meaning rule named its own lines, being the plainest case of what the prep says never works. It didn't clearly beat the general words in the probe: coin bought the look 2 times in 3 before, once in 3 on the general words, and once or twice in 3 with the example.
- **A failure that sends a holder away is swapped, not refused.** The mouthpiece's narration has already streamed when the check comes, so asking again would show a reply being taken back, and a second call costs the turn seconds; swapping the failure for the other one, when that keeps the holder, is deterministic and free, and leaves the dice's failure a failure. The words that count as leaving are physical departures (bolting, fleeing, off through the gate, over the wall), not stepping back, a figure of speech ("their confidence has vanished", "too low to make out") or a departure said not to happen ("they do not bolt", "neither leaves the yard"): on the round's twelve checks it changes only g06's turn 18, and on the probes' 35 none. When both failures send them off, they stand.
- **The holders list names the companion too.** Kell holds the clerk's Hobb lead (she names him from a description), so she's listed; the list says it's so nothing closes the lead off, not a reason to hand it over.
- **The player's state is a quarter down or more.** "HP 28/31" on a fresh dwarf is the engine's quirk (toughness raises the sheet's maximum, which healing never reaches: shared/rules/actions.ts), and playerNow read it as hurt and said "Let it show"; a scratch isn't worth showing either way. playerNow is shared with the old pipeline (talks), whose golden recordings have no player hurt, so its parity tests are unchanged; the engine's quirk itself is the rules', and left.
- **The companion's restraint is coordinated through the co-GM's role, in what's ours.** `companionRoles` goes before the co-GM's tools section (CO_GM_TOOLS is untouched, for the co-GM job's changes to merge clean), and asks for the companion's role every turn to say what she holds back. The probe showed why: with the round's own co-GM note, whose role told Kell to "state plainly that the cord mark and blow show Prout was struck and strangled" and "ask what lead identifies who was there", the mouthpiece said it 3 times in 3 whatever its own rules said; with the roles this branch's co-GM wrote, 0 in 3.
- **What took the scene to its ending isn't in the closing.** Telling the clerk's closing that its ending came when "Kell signs Prout in as a murder" (the route's condition, from the path) didn't stop Kell telling Tull again (3 in 3), so it was taken out again rather than kept unproven.
- **The extra line on terms a role names was taken out too.** "Nobody says what would earn it, even where their role names it" left Dilly reciting 3 times in 3; her terms come from the role the co-GM wrote ("keep her own knowledge back unless Maren sits in and plays fairly") and her card, and the lever is the co-GM's (below).

#### The scenes (`claude/v2-fix2-content`)

The scenes' side of the round's findings: what the prep said, or left unsaid, that let the GM go wrong. The co-GM's code-side guard (an overdue cue never pre-empting a good ending whose condition has happened) and the mouthpiece's rule that reads "No dice:" are the other jobs'.

What failed:
- **H5, the Heron's other half** (g08): Kell had just said the words of arrest over the open true book, the second bell's outer limit (eight exchanges after the Master, round 1's `806f426`) was overdue, its condition said "the second bell rings, whatever is happening", and the co-GM closed the scene on the bad "The barge sails" mid-arrest; and nothing all scene had warned of the tide. Replayed on the round's own request at turn 16, the co-GM took the barge sailing 2 times in 4.
- **Clocks that never move:** Hep's "Forty to the hatch!" five replies running in g08 (her voice, her role palette and the floor's pressure all said forty), and g05's corporal, who "waits by the cart, order in hand" from turn 13 to 19, his role the same every turn.
- **H4, a no-dice path rolled** (g06): the player swore the very promise Cob's card says holds the left boot out; the boot's path had a check (Persuasion DC 13) for a polite ask, the mouthpiece called it on the promise, and the failure sent the children off with the scene's required lead.
- **Arlo's count restaged** (g04/1): the mouthpiece had Arlo call "Third challenge, Hobb" in its second reply, before the cue, "Two refusals already" in its third, and the cue staged the third challenge again in its sixth; the chair's forfeit landed neither time.

What changed:
- `3ba71c8` **The Heron's second bell gives way, and both its bells are felt.** Its condition: not needed once the player has stopped the loading (the gang's sacks down for good, the hoist out of action, the barge held at her moorings), has put the case to the Master's face in front of Kell (the arrest coming, or said), is fighting him and the Crakes, or has the true book in hand; then the bell can ring and the barge go in the background, but what's under way plays on to its own end, each named by its route ([in-the-act], [cornered], [overpowered], [away-with-book]); its outer limit holds "unless one of those is under way". The barge sailing's truth says the loading was never stopped and the Master never taken (if he came, he went home free with his share), and its closing starts where the player is (g08/6's close had moved them onto the wharf). The clock says both bells come a step nearer every exchange, never the same sign twice, and that the barge ends the night only if the player hasn't stopped the loading, taken the Master on or got the book; the midnight event's description and pressure show the tide turning from the Master's arrival (the last sacks, the hatches, the bargees singling up, Sallow at his watch); the midnight bell's cue says Hep's count falls before it. Hep's voice, role palette, whyHere, knowledge, the start's and the floor's notes on her, the floor's pressure and the two paths through her count say the number she shouts is the count as it stands, a few sacks fewer every time.
- `f08a050` **A worse ending gives way to a better one, by name.** Every route into a bad or mixed ending that could be true at the same moment as a good one's says so: the clerk's suicide under protest (not while the case is still being made or found; made, it's [signed-murder]) and last resort (going after the Hobb lead isn't walking away: the cart comes and Kell signs on what they have); the Rat's Manners after Hobb has told the heart of it ([let-him-go], [their-man-inside]), as its ways out already said; the Heron's giving up ([away-with-book] with the book in hand), overpowered and attack once the Master is held ([in-the-act]), and fire or a sinking after the book was out or the Master held; the King's putting it off once the player has given him what moves him ([king-acts]: he decides on the stairs) and a scene made after his word. The two-part test of the co-GM's lines into endings reads the clerk's last resort after its bracketed last sentence.
- `81dc64d` **The other clocks move.** The clerk's morning is felt moving on, a step nearer noon every few exchanges (the clock, the start after Kell's first glance at the sky, and her role palette glancing more often as noon nears), and at the cart Tull's patience runs down a step every exchange, never the same step twice (the cart's pressure and his note at it: the order read, the ladder up, Prout lowered, his pie, the Sarge asked for the last time). The Rat's eleventh bell is felt coming (its clock, start and cue: the night men drinking up, the tenth bell, Hobb's pennies going down and his eyes on the door). The King's Marshal calls about two minutes fewer an exchange, never a number twice.
- `a6655ab` **Arlo's challenges are counted once.** The Rat's start has a paragraph of its own, the one place the count is said: Hobb has refused Arlo twice tonight already; the next challenge is the third and an event of its own, so until it comes Arlo wagers, needles, recites the custom and grins at the hand but doesn't put his elbow on Hobb's table; it comes once; and the third refusal costs Hobb the chair there and then, Nan says so, nobody counts again and there's no fourth. Arlo's first suggested role says the third challenge hasn't been made, and the event ends that it happens once.
- `b117114` (and `d30ec2b`, which mends one path's wording) **"No dice:" up front.** Every path with no check begins "No dice:" with the author's words after it, and every path with a check whose other approaches need none puts those first and the roll after it (Cob's boot, the body through Kell, Moll's story against the drizzle, the hand at the arm-wrestling, the room, the ways to Cob's page, Saul's door and dog, Scrope's book, the look over his shoulder, Hep's gang, a bluff with the right paper). Each card's "Opens:" says "with no dice", and so do Cob's held-out boot, Saul's dog and sergeant and Sallow's corrected record.
- `57a261e` **The docs.** scene-format.md and the comments of shared/scene/document.ts, which mirror it: "No dice:" up front in a path's earn; a route into a bad or mixed ending names the good route it gives way to, and a cue into an ending says what has it no longer needed, which play reads as an overdue cue not brought about; a clock says how it's felt coming. authoring-scenes.md has the same, and two rules of its own (a worse ending gives way; say once where a count stands). The Heron's truth sheet has Hep's count falling.

##### Judgement calls

- **The bell holds off for the run-up to an arrest, not only the arrest.** "An arrest in progress plays on to its ending" read as the words of arrest alone left g08's turns 14 and 15 open to the bell: it was overdue from turn 14, while the player was setting Prout's page beside the order of sale and turning the clerk in Kell's hearing, and the round's co-GM hadn't taken it there, though its answer on turn 15 named it overdue. So the case put to the Master's face in front of Kell counts as under way, "the arrest coming, or said".
- **Stopping the loading is said in things that can happen** (the gang's sacks down for good, the hoist out of action, the barge held at her moorings), since the Patience sails with whatever's aboard: the clock now says she goes in the background, and the night ends on what the player did, not on her.
- **The limit stays as data, unchanged,** and no `until` was added: what has the bell no longer needed (a case being put, a fight, the book in hand) isn't a beat, and the co-GM's tools already say an overdue cue isn't brought about when its condition says it's no longer needed. scene-format.md's play semantics now say so.
- **The routes named in the bell's bracket stay.** The co-GM took [cornered] at turn 16 before the sword was drawn in 2 of 4 tries; a variant without the names did the same (2 of 4), so they aren't what leads it there, and they are its handles on what the arrest ends in.
- **The barge sailing's truth says the Master went free,** because the co-GM reads what's true at an ending on its routes: it can't be true mid-arrest. Its closing starting where the player is answers g08/6 from the scene's side; the closing rule is the mouthpiece job's.
- **Which worse routes give way.** A test weighs every route into a bad or mixed ending of the campaign: thirteen name the good routes they give way to, and three can't be true with a good one at once (Hobb bolted to his master, the trail lost without the Heron, the Master's sword drawn). The Rat's Manners after the heart of it gives way to [let-him-go], losing the thrown-out ending's bar and Nan's coolness, by the rule (the better ending's moment had come). A fire or a sinking before the book was out or the Master held stays the bad ending even if they get out with the book after: the order decides, and up in smoke's effects already carry a book in hand.
- **How often a clock shows:** every exchange for the short ones (Hep's count before midnight, the tide after it, Tull's patience at the cart, the Marshal's minutes), every few exchanges for the long ones (the clerk's twelve to noon, the Rat's fifteen to the eleventh bell). The signs listed are examples, and "never the same sign twice" is the rule. Tull's note and the cart's pressure keep their first words, which the e2e's check of a newcomer's default role and the prep test read.
- **The Marshal's two minutes an exchange** match the counts round 1 gave the King's cues (fifteen minutes about eight exchanges in, five about twelve), though not as arithmetic: twenty-eight to fifteen in eight is nearer one and a half, fifteen to five in four is two and a half. "About" covers it, and the cues test holds them to 8 and 12.
- **Arlo's count is said in the start's description,** which the mouthpiece has as the stage's direction while the scene is at its start and as prep after it; it's the one place, and the card (Hobb on two), the event (it happens once) and the suggested role (not made yet) agree with it. The cue's condition, the custom in what any local knows and the round-1 tests' line are as they were.
- **"No dice:" on every path with no check, not only the mixed ones,** because the reply format's own rule calls a check on a risky attempt at someone who could go either way whether or not the prep has one, and a kindness or a promise is exactly that. Where a path is free through and through, the author's words follow it as written but for a first letter lower-cased (and a "Free:" or a trailing "no dice" folded in); "Free to …" stays as "No dice: free to …" (free says no coin too). The six paths that are rolls through and through are as they were.
- **A fright beside free approaches is on a roll.** The re-authored clerk's "a real fright works too, and costs", in the same sentence as the promise, would have read as certain under "No dice:"; it's now Intimidation DC 12, as the old clerk's scare-kids route had it, and Hobb's card says his is on a roll, as his loft path's Intimidation DC 15 already did. Saul's "the King's name alone is a harder sell" is left unpriced, in the author's words.
- **Route conditions that already say "no dice" in their own words** (going in as a hand, his mum made safe, their man inside, the King's word, sending for Sallow) are as they were: a route without a skill has no dice by its shape, and the job was the paths and the cards.
- **The cards' first sentences and tells are untouched,** since the per-turn note lists each secret by its first sentence and each tell by the first of its "Shows:"; only their "Opens:" and later sentences changed.
- **The interludes' two paths a scene** have the marker too, though the story skips them, so the test can hold every scene.

Arlo's count at g04's third turn read "Hobb's on two" in every try, before and after, against a transcript whose second reply had already staged the challenge. H4's turns show the marker alone changes little: on the round's requests the mouthpiece rarely called the check either way (its two replies in the round both did); the rule that reads the marker is the mouthpiece job's.

#### Merged, and what was checked

Each job merged into `claude/v2-scenes` in turn, a merge commit each, on top of the salvage and the deletions.

- **The co-GM's.** Seven files conflicted, each where both sides had written beside the same lines, and both are kept: ai/gm/two-part.ts's source header (the salvage's notes and talks, then this job's sentence) and its new functions (`notesOnPeople`, then `unsaidIn`, `unsaidNote` and `unsaidText`); the two-part tests' imports; the fake's co-GM round (a talk walked only where the recipe says, and a held move taken again once); the AI README's fake options and the two-part settle's row (the GM's notes on the people on stage and this job's targets, holds and refusals; the old reply's and settle's rows stay gone); the two-part design's As built (the memory paragraph, then this job's); the probe's view, which has no `memory.opened` since the disclosure went; and this log. What changed on the way: the talk's settle test reads the open route's words as this job wrote them ("its revelation, as written, has landed"), and `unsaidIn` finds nobody in a talk (below). Checked on the merge: the type check clean, npm test 958 of 958 (the cleanup's 952 and the job's six), the campaign check with no problems.

##### Judgement calls

- **A talk's discovery names nobody unsaid.** Since the salvage a talk's co-GM is the scene's co-GM, so the co-GM job's `unsaidIn`, written before talks played on it, read the talk's told discovery ("Twenty years ago Nan Quill put her out through the Drowned Rat's door") and found Nan Quill, of the Rat's cast, whom nobody had said: the targets said so, and Kell's confiding would have been held once for a name. A talk's discovery is what the companion confides of their own past, never a lead to someone to go and find, which is what the job's own judgement call keeps the check to, so a talk (a scene with a `companion`) has none; the talk's settle test checks the note isn't there.
- **What's ours reaches a talk as written.** A talk's mouthpiece reads the same rules after the user's prompt, but for its format: the new parts of what the player sees and hears (nobody states their own terms, the player character's body as the player's, where the scene plays, whose place in a talk is its setting, out in the town between scenes) fit it as they stand, and since Kell walks with the player there, the companion's five points and the co-GM's `companionRoles` reach it too, her role saying what she keeps back, as a talk wants. The check's rules don't (a talk's format has no dice, and a check written anyway is dropped before `inReach` reads it), and nobody holds a required discovery in a talk (its told isn't required), so its note has no holders.
- **The Heron's second bell is the scenes' job's.** The co-GM's job had narrowed round 1's "whatever is happening" to "whatever else is happening; only the Master being caught at it right then comes first", and the scenes' job rewrote the whole condition: not needed once the loading is stopped, the case is put to the Master's face in front of Kell, he and the Crakes are being fought or the true book is in hand, the tide felt before it rings, and its outer limit "unless one of those is under way". That takes in the co-GM's narrowing and more, and names the routes the co-GM's hold puts to it, so it's kept whole; the co-GM job's words for it are gone with the rest of the old condition, and its judgement call above is about them.
- **The talk template's paths have no "No dice:".** The salvage made the template's told a discovery with two paths ("Free, when the talk comes near it…", "The question or the moment that brings it out…") after the scenes' job was cut, so its test, which holds every scene file, failed on them. A talk has no dice at all (its reply format has no check, and a check written anyway is dropped), and each talk's prep writes its paths afresh (ai/gm/talk.ts `confidePaths`), so the marker would mean nothing there; the test passes over a scene with a `companion`, the template, rather than the template taking a marker its talks never would.

##### The smoke on real models

Round `smoke-fix2-1018` on a stack of its own (`loop stack up --root runs/loop-smoke-fix2 --port 3547 --ai-port 9047`, `KM_AI_DAILY_USD=0.3`; archived to `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/loop/smoke-fix2-1018`, with its report), two games played by hand as the thief persona (Nell Quickfingers, a halfling rogue), no reviewers: $0.170 in all, 198 calls (the clerk's game $0.038, the Heron's $0.114 with the two scenes ended on the way, the rest the characters).

- **The clerk, six lines as the thief:** hung back, sidled up to Cob about his boots, then the promise (his name kept out, nothing to the watch or the sergeant's book, the men from the loft kept off him). **H4 holds:** no check on the promise; Cob asked for a proper swear, spat and shook on it, and held the left boot out himself, "Boot stays Cob's"; the page came out of its lining with no roll, and once it was read the co-GM took the motive and Cob gave the thump and the choking from the loft. Kell stayed by the gate, her pencil tapping, and drew nothing for the player.
- **The Heron from its opening to the arrest, ten lines:** Kell left at the land door, in at the river door with Hobb's token as a hand, the re-sacking seen, Prout's page laid by Scrope's hand (his "Wendell's hand. I would know it anywhere."), the true book kept open for the Master, Kell inside behind him to see him initial the page and count his share, then the arrest. Hep's count fell every time she called it (36, 32, 28, 24, 20, 16) and Scrope looked at the land door. The co-GM's move for "Kell saw him" was refused once, its reason saying he hadn't initialled yet, and taken the turn he did. **H5 holds:** the barge never sailed (the second bell never came due: eight exchanges after the Master was turn 13), and once Sallow was held the co-GM took caught in the act, which closed the scene where the player stood, the true book handed over. But the co-GM took [cornered] when Sallow's hand closed on his cane and he said "Crake.", before the blade was out (below), so the reply to the player's words to the Crakes was the fight's closing; the fight's stand-in had Sallow surrender, the scene picked up with Kell holding him, and caught in the act came on the next settle.
- **Card recital:** none. Cob's "Not for looking, not for borrowing, not for bluebottles" refuses rather than names his terms, and nobody at the Heron said what would open them. **Kell:** no conclusions, no examining for the player, no next steps; once at the Heron she answered the forewoman's challenge to the player's promise with her own offer ("I can't promise it. I'll write down what you swear, and speak for you"), within her card but in the player's place. **Speakers:** every spoken line in both games credited to the right person by the name the player knew (Hep's first shout, offstage in the opening, "Someone"); the pages' written words untagged.

##### Left

- **[cornered] before the blade** (the co-GM's and the scenes' jobs' open item, seen again): the endings-first rule's own example ("a hand on the cane but no blade out") was taken as the fight.
- **A cue moved on a reason that denies it:** at the Heron's turn 4 the co-GM moved the midnight cue, only due soon, with "Not yet: the midnight bell has not rung, and Nell is still speaking with Scrope"; a cue's move isn't refused on its reason (round 1's rule, since a cue's move is what brings it about), so the Master came a little early. Due soon may be inviting the call; a cue that isn't overdue moved on a reason like that could be held as the bad cue is.
- **The fight's aftermath names the unheard:** `gm.aftermath` (the old voice's one call, kept for fights) said "Scrope clutches the open true book" though the player had never heard his name; the unheard-name pass runs on the mouthpiece's narration and not on the aftermath's.
- **A stray `”,`** ended the Heron's closing, the model's own inside the JSON string.
- Not played: the clerk's closing restaging Kell's order and Dilly's terms at the Rat (the mouthpiece job's open items), and the Heron's bell overdue in the middle of an arrest, which this game's early arrest never reached.

### Fixes from QA round qa3-1018

QA round qa3-1018, the third round (after round 2's fixes, the old pipeline's salvage and the old scene steps' deletion), played eight games of M2's scenes on the two-part GM (build `08113e1`): the King's morning (tangent), the Heron (schemer, thief), the Rat (absurd, investigator) and the hanged clerk (wizard, barbarian, terse). Its folder (`runs/loop/qa3-1018` in the `claude/v2-scenes` worktree, archived to `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/loop/qa3-1018`) has the games, the call logs, the testers' notes and the triage's verdicts with the why of each finding (its triage.json); the published summary is the round's report artifact. What it found went to fix jobs, each cut from M2's branch at `08113e1`, and each job's part is its own heading below.

#### The co-GM (`claude/v2-fix3-cogm`)

What failed:
- **H8** (g02, the Heron, severity 4): at turn 13 the co-GM took away-with-book ("Out with the true book") with "none" for its reason, the player still in the counting-box with Sallow's hand on his cane and a Crake on the stair; the route's condition is leaving the Heron with the book, nobody holding them, and the closing waved the standoff away.
- **H7** (g05, the clerk, severity 4, the third round running): at turn 17 the co-GM's move into find-hobb was held (nobody had said Big Hobb Mallory), and it took it again in the same settle through the hold's own exception ("If the name is only a detail of it and its heart has landed, take [find-hobb] again"), on the reason "she will identify him as Big Hobb Mallory, champion of the Drowned Rat, in the next reply". She didn't, the ending came at turn 18's settle, and the name was first said in the closing.
- **H6, the co-GM's part** (g07, the clerk): held on find-hobb at turn 17, the hold's text told it "whoever holds the name gives it in the next reply… (write it into their role, unasked where their card says so)", and from turn 17 to 20 it wrote Kell's role "If Grunda asks about the companion…": the player never asked, and the game ran out of turns without the lead.
- **Discoveries not taken** (g08): the player got into the loft at turn 19 and saw the cut page with the entries either side "lost to goblins", countersigned "J. Sallow, for the Royal Purveyor" (the motive's tally-loft path, word for word), and at turn 20 pointed at the scuffs and the page for Kell's record; the co-GM took neither the motive nor the murder, its route saying "its revelation, as written, has landed" and the motive's revelation also saying "sold off" and "Prout had found out". Round 2's bar was too strict.
- **Kell signed before the scene could end** (g05/8): at turn 15 she signed Prout in as a murder in the story, when signed-murder still needed the Hobb lead, so the story ran four turns past its own ending and the closing signed him in again, to a corporal who had gone.
- **Roles that don't move on:** the King asked "What would you have me do?" four times after the player had answered it (g01/3), Kell asked "On what evidence?" again after the answer (g05/3) and said three times she'd "speak for him" (g03/3), Arlo's third challenge was played twice from a role that quoted it (g06/1), and Tull's patience stood still for six replies with "take one practical step, such as checking the time or readying the sheet" written into his role every turn (g07/6, g08/3).

What changed:
- `bbb19ef` **The co-GM's moves.** A move is refused when its reason says nothing of what happened (`saysNothing`: empty, a null answer, or only the route's own words back, its label, id or where it goes), when it says it's still to come (`stillToCome`: "will identify", "can name", "in the next reply", "is about to", "once she names him"), or, for a cue, when it says its moment hasn't come (`deniesMoment`: opening "Not yet" or "No", or calling itself no longer needed; or any reason that denies itself while the cue isn't overdue); and `deniesItself` catches more of the ways a reason says it hasn't happened ("No:" up front, "no one has established", "have not landed", "has yet described", "but … has not"). A lead (a discovery a way into one of the scene's endings requires, beats.ts `endingNeeds`, whose revelation names someone of the story's other scenes) is refused every time while nobody has said the name, whatever its reason, and there's no "take it again" for it (`leadText`; moved again in the same turn, a short answer that it waits for a reply). The refusal leaves the name owed: the settle answer's `owed`, which the settle commit keeps on the path as a step that moves nothing (beats.ts `oweName`, `owedLeads`: `owed:hobb`, from the stage to itself, once). While it's owed and unsaid, the reply endpoint tells the mouthpiece that whoever gives it freely as their path has it (`nameHolders`: a hook path via someone here whose clue has the name, the clerk's Kell) names them now (`owedDirective`: "Sergeant Agnes Kell names him now: Big Hobb Mallory. What's been said of him has reached her, and her path in your prep has it: … So in this reply, after answering the player's turn, she says the name aloud in a line of her own, unasked, and writes it down"), reading the campaign's story only then. The co-GM's targets say a lead isn't reached until its name is said, tell it to move a lead once its person has been described so the name is owed, and, once it is, who gives it; its tools say the same, with that role written with no condition ("she names him now", never "if asked"), and that a move's why says what happened (for a cue, what brings it about now). A discovery that isn't a lead and names someone unsaid (the clerk's motive, 'J. Sallow' on a page) is held once as before. The fake mouthpiece says an owed name when its note says who names them now.
- `a07c697` **A discovery reached by one of its paths.** The co-GM's open routes into a discovery say "the player has its heart, by one of its paths (the revelation): a strong or proof path's clue that has landed with them, as its earn allows, is enough, though it doesn't say all the revelation does; suggestive clues count once the player has drawn the conclusion from them", its prep's discoveries say the same, its targets list each discovery's paths by who or what holds them and how strong each is, and its tools' discovery bullet says so. The mouthpiece's prep and per-turn note keep "its revelation has landed".
- `77acb56` **The roles.** The companion's (`companionRoles`) lists the ways into an ending that her own act records and that still need what isn't reached (`waitingEndings`: the clerk's signed-murder and signed-suicide, the Rat's kell-takes-him, the Heron's in-the-act), and holds that act back until the ending can be taken: her role says only that she writes the case down and waits, never why or on what. The tools section: a line someone has said, or a question the player has answered, comes out of their role, even as done; and a clock's role names this exchange's one new step, read from the last replies, never a menu of examples or a step already shown.
- `130ca6a` **The probe owes a lead's name** (`--owe <discovery>,…`): the rebuilt bookkeeping gets the owed step, as if the co-GM before had been refused it (the round's code never was), so a reply asked again is told who names them, after the round's own directives; each try prints the names it left owed.

##### Judgement calls

- **What says nothing is the route's own words.** `saysNothing` reads a reason's words against the route's label, id and target's title and a short list of little words and null answers ("none", "n/a", "done", "taken"): "Out with the true book" for away-with-book says nothing, "Nell walked out of the land door with the book under her coat" does. Cues too, as asked: "The noon bell" for cart-comes is refused, "twelve exchanges in, its outer limit" isn't. On the four rounds' 172 moves whose routes the campaign still has (qa1, qa2, qa3 and the smoke after round 2) it catches H8's "none" and nothing else.
- **A cue's denial is refused before it's overdue, and allowed after.** Round 1 exempted cues from `deniesItself` because a cue's move is what brings it about ("the bell hasn't rung yet: it rings now"). That stays once the cue is overdue; before, a reason that denies itself says the moment hasn't come: the smoke's Master brought in early on "Not yet: the midnight bell has not rung" (its open item), round 2's g04 bell rung a reply early, while only due soon, on "it has not rung in the latest exchange", and a probe of g06's turn 3 that rang the Rat's bell on "has not yet rung… there is still time for the player to act". Opening "Not yet" or "No", or "no longer needed" (round 3's g04 rang the second bell on "the barge-sailing cue is no longer needed", held by the bad cue's hold), is refused overdue or not.
- **The denials and the future tense are kept to what the rounds said.** Round 3's co-GM took two discoveries on reasons that denied them (g04's kell-saw-him on "No: Nell is concealed inside while Kell remains outside; she has not witnessed Sallow's transaction", g06's find-the-loft on "but no one has established that Hobb was in the tally loft"), and the probes gave three more; the new patterns catch these, and on the 172 moves nothing that wasn't a denial or a bad move. `stillToCome` is kept to naming verbs ("will identify", "can name"), so what was said stands: "who will stand behind it", "she can swear to it", "Master Sallow would come" pass. Its three catches are H7's two and round 1's Hobb on "Kell can name him".
- **A lead is the clerk's Hobb and the Rat's master.** "A discovery an ending requires" reads as a route into an ending requiring it (`endingNeeds`, which counts the clerk's murder too, whose revelation names nobody elsewhere); with `unsaidIn`'s people of the story's other scenes, that's the clerk's Hobb (Big Hobb Mallory) and the Rat's master (Master Josiah Sallow). The Heron's true book and the King's what-moves-him name only their own cast. The old hold, with its "take it again", stays for any other discovery naming someone unsaid (the motive's 'J. Sallow', a detail of it).
- **Any move into a lead owes its name, whatever its reason.** The first ordering refused a reason that denied itself before reading the lead, so it owed nothing: but the round's co-GM moved Hobb's lead on "but no one has named the companion as Big Hobb Mallory" (g05, turns 12 and 16) and "but neither she nor the player has yet established that he was…" (g07, turn 17), which is exactly the moment the name is owed, and a probe moved it on "none" once its targets said to. So the lead is read first, and only a move into it owes the name. The risk is a co-GM that moves the lead before its person has been described, when Kell would name a man nobody had; the targets and the tools say to move it once he's described, and the probes showed no early move.
- **The name owed is a step on the path, not a new column.** The path already holds steps that move nothing (the rules', the reactions', a talk's confide), so `owed:<lead>` from the stage to itself, once, needs no schema change, survives the rows, reaches every later request with the bookkeeping, and moves no turn count (`reachedTurns` skips a step to itself). The settle answer's `owed` is an optional protocol field, which the simulation's commit turns into the step.
- **Who gives an owed name is whoever's hook path has it.** A hook is a pointer given freely once its moment comes; the clerk's Kell's ("A porter the size of a door who's champion of the arm-wrestling at the Drowned Rat is Big Hobb Mallory… she names him and writes it down") is the one the task names. The Rat's master has no such path: Hobb's is earned last ("once he's talking, asked for it straight"), and Kell's, Cob's and Nan's are suggestive and asked for, so a direction there would have Hobb blurt his master's name. That lead waits for the name to come out as it's earned, with no direction.
- **The direction is the reply endpoint's**, beside the move's, every reply until the name is said or the lead reached, while the holder is here; it reads the campaign's story (for who's unsaid, as the settle does) only while a name is owed, so a reply otherwise never waits on it (the pipeline test of a vocabulary that doesn't read). It isn't on a closing.
- **A lead moved again in a turn gets a short answer.** The probe's tries, refused, moved it again on a naming Kell had never made ("Kell named the described huge companion as Big Hobb Mallory"), round after round: 2 of 3 tries at g05's turn 17 ran into the 45 s deadline and lost their roles. The refusal now says not to move it again this turn, and a second move is told no reason changes it.
- **The fake follows the direction.** The fake mouthpiece reads the direction's first sentence and adds the holder's line ("[Sergeant Agnes Kell] “Big Hobb Mallory.”"), as a model following it would, so the clerk's walk takes Hobb a turn later: the local host's test and the e2e's steps 14, 23 and 31 walk one line more and check the owed step, the direction and the name. The Rat's master on the fake waits for someone to say Sallow, which nothing on the fake does: the arc and the e2e end the Rat as the owner (end --exit hobb-talks) so neither changes, but the dev stack's whole-story recipe (`?fake=arc`, KM_AI_FAKE) stops there until the player says the name or ends the scene (hosts/dev/fake.ts says so).
- **The targets list each path by who holds it and how strong it is, not its clue.** The prep has every clue and what earns it; repeating the clues would add about 3,000 characters to the clerk's input every turn. The probe of g08 took the motive with the index alone.
- **The companion's waits read the ending's condition outside its brackets.** A condition naming the companion there has her do what it records ("Kell signs Prout in", "Kell has arrested him", "Kell writes her objection"); one naming her only in brackets (the Heron's overpowered, attack, ablaze and sunk; the King's justice) doesn't. Her role says only that she writes the case down and waits, never why or on what, after a probe's role said "Do not sign the body in as murder until the required lead has been named", which the mouthpiece would have said aloud (round 2's "I still need a lead"). The content job's cart event changes to match through this text alone.
- **Stale roles are the co-GM's to drop.** Both rules are in the tools section; nothing in code reads a role's lines against what's been said.

##### The probe

The round's turns, and round 1's and round 2's Hobb guard cases, replayed on the real model (openai/gpt-6-luna) with `npm run loop -- probe` (KM_LOOP_DIR at `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/loop`, read only; logs in `~/git/kingmaker-scratch/v2/cogm3-probe`), three tries a turn. "Before" is this branch's base, the round's own code; "after" the final code unless it says otherwise. Every co-GM try took 6 to 45 s and cost $0.001 to $0.005; the probes cost $0.264 in all (the before passes $0.055, the passes while the wording was found $0.085, the replies asked again, the guards and the roles $0.080, the final pass $0.043).

| Turn | What should happen | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| qa3 g02 t13 (H8: the counting-box, Sallow's hand on his cane, a Crake on the stair) | out with the true book not taken | 1 of 3, on "but has not left the Heron" (the round: 1 of 1, on "none") | 0 of 3, and 0 of 3 on an earlier pass; one try took the fight on a sword it said was out, which wasn't (Open) |
| qa3 g05 t17 (H7: Moll's description given at turn 7) | Hobb not taken, the name owed, Kell's role with no condition | Hobb 0 of 3, nothing owed, her role conditional 3 of 3 ("If Aurelian asks…") (the round: Hobb taken on the hold's exception) | Hobb 0 of 3, owed 2 of 3, "names him now" 2 of 3 (the pass before: 3 of 3 and 3 of 3) |
| qa3 g05 t18, the round's Hobb left out, the name owed (`--owe`), the reply asked again | Kell names him, and no ending before she does | Hobb 0 of 3 (the round's reply, no name) | Kell names him 3 of 3, Hobb taken after it 3 of 3, no ending 3 of 3 |
| qa3 g05 t12 (the round's first move into Hobb, refused as a denial) | the name owed | (the round: nothing owed) | owed 3 of 3, beside the motive 3 of 3 |
