#### The mouthpiece (`claude/v2-fix3-mouth`)

The mouthpiece's share of the round was five patterns, one of them something round 2 had fixed in words that didn't hold. **Events landed abruptly or not at all** (g02/2, g03/4, g05/1, g07/4, g08/4, the round's biggest pattern): the Master's entrance, his climb and his look at the page crammed onto the tail of the player's bargain with Scrope, with the warning the player had posted Kell to give skipped; the eleventh bell and noon with no sign before them; twice the corporal and his constables simply there. **The setup still gave away the roll** (g05/4, g07/2, g08/1): "the lock holds… The door remains shut" before a roll that tore it free, the cord mark shown and recorded before a failed Medicine check took it back, the player through the gap with the loft in view before the Athletics roll. **The loft's docker's knot lost its meaning** (g05/6, g07/3, g08/7's): "a compact, practiced knot", once with "nothing about it identifies the hand that tied it" in answer to exactly the right question, because Cob's secret has the knot too, the per-turn note's hidden list kept his secret back though the murder's path via the tally loft gives it with no dice, and the stage manager then wrote the invention into the rope's note. **Two lines went to "Someone"** (g04/1, g06/3). **Big reveals landed flat** (g06/2, g08/6): Sallow's name, given Kell with the confession or read off the Roll beside the corporal's order, went into her notebook and nowhere else. The user's two prompts are untouched; everything is in code and what's appended ([two-part.ts](../ai/gm/two-part.ts), [speakers.ts](../ai/gm/speakers.ts)).

What changed:
- `e37a8b2` **Who says a line** ([speakers.ts](../ai/gm/speakers.ts) `saidBy`, `quoteSpeaker`): a line after a verb of speech said of someone named just before it is theirs, its called name's extra words and all ("…, and the forewoman with the sack-needle calls,"), where the "and the" before the name had read as a mention; one after a verb said of someone the prose can't name ("…, and a voice calls,") is nobody's we know; and a line whose lead-in is about nobody who might speak ("The room’s attention follows.") goes back a sentence, to whoever that one is about alone (the young lighterman who planted his elbow).
- `b70fb1c` **An event lands as a moment of its own, and the clock is felt before it** (two-part.ts `movedDirective`, `setUpEarlier`, `clockNote`): the reply after a move to an event answers the player's turn briefly if it needs it, then gives the event a paragraph of its own, last (or opens on it when it's the answer), whoever it brings on seen coming in, by which way and doing what first, even where the player's turn already speaks of them, and ends there for the player to answer; what the player set up for it happens as they set it, whoever they asked doing it, the warning first while the newcomers are still coming, with their words quoted when they asked to be warned. The per-turn note has the scene's clock in the author's words and one new sign of it to show in passing, every third reply while an open cue's outer limit runs and every reply from the one before it runs out, never at the opening or on a closing. The probe makes a move to show again (`movedAgain`).
- `d26abc4` **The check decided first** (two-part.ts FORMAT and SCHEMA, `speak`; the fake; the probe's `roundSchema`, `replyOf`): the reply's schema is `{check, narration, outcomes}` in that order, the check's skill and DC (or null) before a word is written, the narration with a check the setup alone, stopping at the attempt, and the outcomes carrying everything the roll decides; the format says so field by field and reads what the prep says for certain first, since that's now decided first. A talk's is `{narration, check: null}` as before.
- `2147372` **What the place holds** (two-part.ts `reachNote`, SEEN): the per-turn note lists, after what's hidden, the clues of each discovery's path via the place the world has the player in whose earn is no dice ("In the player's reach here"), theirs with no check, said as the prep has them, meaning and all, not held back for a secret that has them too, what else is there still taking what its path says; what the player sees and hears says the same of a place's own clue for whoever gets there. The probe's view puts the player where the round's world had them.
- `60ba34c` **What lands** (two-part.ts SEEN's "When something lands", the companion's points): a name, a link or a confession that touches someone's card, said by the player or there on what they've found, is reacted to in the same reply, in character and from the card (a tell, going very still, a sharp question, what they'd say of it openly or the card's first words: "I'd met him."), never a secret told on the strength of it, and the conclusion stays the player's; the companion reacts before she writes.

##### Judgement calls

- **The setup made structural by the schema's order, with no cap in code.** With the narration first and the check after it, the model wrote the attempt through to its result and called the check as an afterthought, whatever the words said; deciding the check first makes it write the narration as a setup. The narration still streams first of the long fields (a few tokens in, `{"check":null,"narration":"`), so the first words aren't held up by five outcomes, as putting the check with its outcomes first would have. A cap on the setup's length in code wasn't added: with the check first no probed setup gave anything away (none of the 24 checks the passes called on g05 t14, g07 t8 and g08 t18), and a setup cut after it had streamed would show more than it kept. A check whose outcomes don't come is dropped as before, its setup then standing alone; none did in the probes.
- **What the prep says for certain is read first in the check's field,** before the criteria, now that the check is decided before the narration: with the criteria first, the loft's knot (no dice, the per-turn note saying so) was rolled for in 1 of 3 tries at g07 t9 and 1 of 3 at g05 t18, then 1 of 3 at g07 t9 with the note saying "with no check"; after the reorder, 1 of 6 at g07 t9 (g05 t18's one check after it was for the desk, the knot given as a docker's in its setup). The no-dice turns probed for checks it might add (round 2's H4 promise to Cob, round 3's promise and kindness to him) called none in 15 tries.
- **The probe asks a round in its own schema.** The call log keeps only the schema's name, so `roundSchema` reads the format's words: "with two fields" with a check on most turns is the old shape (the narration first, the check with its outcomes inside it), "`check`: always null" a talk's, anything else this checkout's; the before arm is then as the round sent it, and `replyOf` reads either shape.
- **The player's setup is found by its words and quoted.** The directive's general words ("If the player set something up for this moment…") had Kell warn of the Master in none of 9 tries at g02 t6, the round's co-GM role for her saying only "come at Corvin's signal or the first shout"; with the player's line quoted, 3 of 3. The words looked for ask to be warned ("If the Master comes, I want to know", "warn me", "let me know", "give a whistle if"), not "watch", which players say of themselves ("I watch the floor"); over the three rounds' 399 player lines they find g02's turn 2 and one promise ("And if the men come looking, they'll find me holding the paper"), which "If that was for this moment" leaves to the model.
- **The event goes last, the reply ending on it.** "Open on it" alone let the reply go on past it (Cob's whole account after the corporal in 2 of 3 at g07 t12); "answer the player's turn first, briefly, then the event, last" kept the player's question answered and the event the last thing, 3 of 3. The reply may stop on the warning itself, the Master still at the door (1 of 3 at g02 t6), as the reviewer's better version asked.
- **Seen arriving even where the player's turn speaks of them:** the testers' roster ("Here:") showed the corporal before any reply did, and the players' lines named him; the directive doesn't take the player's turn as the arrival. The roster is the testers' view, another job's.
- **The clock's pacing is in code, its signs the author's.** A sign every reply would be wallpaper (the round flagged Kell's pencil and Moll's fist as tics); every third reply into the scene, and every reply from the one before a running limit runs out (overdue and held too, as a bad cue waiting on a better ending is), with the scene's clock quoted so the signs are the ones it names, never the moment itself. Any open cue with a running limit counts (the Rat's Arlo too, whose reply before his limit gets a sign of the night), since nothing in the format says which cue is the clock's; a field for it would be the format's and the plugin's.
- **A place's clues by the place's name, no dice only.** A path's `via` is matched to the world's area by name, its bracketed part aside ("the tally loft (above the yard, inside the granary)"), or by id; only paths whose earn begins "No dice:" count, since being there is what earns them (the Roll's entries via the same loft need a look at the book and a check, and aren't listed); a discovery already reached keeps its place's clues in reach, as the knot is still there to see; paths via things or people (the body, Cob's boot) aren't, as being near them isn't what earns them. The note is made from the world before the player's turn, so the reply that takes them in (the check on the stair door, the squeeze through the board) doesn't have it: what the player sees and hears says the same in general words for that, and it holds only partly (below).
- **The probe's view puts the player where the round's world had them,** read off the world part's "the player is in …": rebuilt from the place, every probe had them where the place starts them, in the yard, and the loft's clues were never in reach.
- **A reaction isn't a secret told.** The first wording ("one line of what they know of it") brought Kell's whole visit from Prout out with the page at g05 t12 in 3 of 3, where the events fix alone had none of 3 (the round's own request: 3 of 3); the round's co-GM role for her there said "If the missing page is shown, tell him once…", though her card opens on the page in her hands. Its line is now what they'd say openly or the card's first words ("I'd met him."), and what the card keeps back waits for what opens it; with the round's role it still comes out (below).
- **The speakers' walk back is one sentence,** and only to a sentence about one person who isn't only mentioned, never past someone the prose can't name: over the three rounds' 1056 kept lines the first version changed 11, two of them wrongly (Sallow's surrender after a sentence about Hobb and Scrope, the talking brother's line after the forewoman); the stops leave 9, each from nobody to the one a reader would name. The list of words for someone the prose brings in grows by kin and a few trades (brother, twin, bruiser, ferryman, widow…).

##### Judgement calls

- **The pushed lead is an event, so it moves the stage.** A cue goes to an event or an ending, and only an event brings someone on (`enters`), so the porters' break is the stage once it comes: its notes carry Tull's patience and Kell's signing on from the cart's, and the endings' routes, which leave from the cart, stay open (a test walks to signed-murder from it). It goes last in the clerk's nodes and routes, so the tests that index the scene's lists move only where the new beat shows.
- **Four exchanges after the cart.** The cart's own limit is twelve into the scene, so the lead comes by reply 16 at the latest and leaves the signing and the closing inside a 20-turn round; the condition brings it sooner when the case has stalled. In the probes it came at the limit, not before (below).
- **"Not needed while" for a lead about to land.** The co-GM's tools bring an overdue cue about unless its condition says it's no longer needed, so the times the porters shouldn't come over a lead (Cob or Moll mid-telling, a description just given to Kell) are written that way, not as "not while", which an overdue cue would read past.
- **Old Wat holds the porters' path, at the break only.** The path was the porters at the far gate, a thing in a few words, reached by asking around the yard before the cart; it's now a cast member who is in the yard only once the porters come in, and before that anyone asking after them hears they'll be in at the noon break. The lead's other three paths (Cob, Moll and Kell) are open from the start as before.
- **The bandaged hand is one of Kell's descriptions,** as asked; she knows Hobb by the stairs' talk, not the man, but every description of him in the scene has his size with it ("a big man with a bandaged hand"), and a description that reached her before the cart counts (g05's Moll had given hers at turn 7).
- **Kell's lead is a name, a man to go and find.** "The lead to who was there is in her notebook" let the mouthpiece take Moll's description for it (it signed without the name in a replay, below); "by name" with her naming him first from a description is what the replay held.
- **The Crakes are the first and the second.** Nobody can tell the twins apart until one of them speaks, so neither "talking" nor "silent" is anything the player can see, and both carry cudgels; "the second bruiser is coming behind the first" is how the round's model wrote them in g04 anyway. The members keep "Crake" in their names, so the name pass still keeps the name unheard (round 2's g08/4); a `called` for members (a format and name-pass change) was the other way, and is the mouthpiece's code.
- **The true book's label is kept in the prep,** where it's the GM's handle (fifty-one times in the Heron, fourteen in the King's morning, the endings' conditions and the tests that read them), and the narration's sources are what changed: the world's name for it (what the world view, the player's sheet and the map show), the setting and Scrope's look (narrated as written), and a word at the wharf's start. The endings' notebook lines ("We have the Heron's true book") are left: by then Scrope has usually explained his two books, and they're the story's record.
- **`onStart` on the scene, not the campaign.** The format had no way to start a quest but an ending's effect, and the first scene is what gives the King's request; a scene field takes effects as `onEnd` does, so the check, the effects and the plugin's handling of effects all carry over. It applies once, as the scene's row is made: on a map, when it's built as the lead (the game's first as the game begins, before the player has walked into the yard), else as it opens; never again as a waiting scene opens. **What M3's editor will need:** the scene model's list of the file's keys (`onStart` before `onEnd`), `EffectsAt`'s scene field (`'onStart' | 'onEnd' | 'afterwards'`), the facts' tests over its conditions as over `onEnd`'s, and a place to edit it beside `onEnd`; until then the editor keeps it on save (unknown keys are kept).
- **What the player can see is worked out from the look layer's dressing:** its walls (glass is seen through, and a wall is crossed at an open door) and its indoor areas (outdoors sees outdoors, over a low wall or through a gate); it's what's in sight now, with no memory of rooms the player has left. A passage (the loose board) is a way found, not seen, and is listed once the narration has mentioned it. People in sight are listed as the game's place panel names them (nameOf, the names heard), and the map's stage drawing above the legend is the game's own, as before.
- **Arrivals wait for the world, not only the roster.** The place panel draws the world, so holding back only on_stage would still have drawn the corporal at the gate; the settle keeps the move first and joins only who the player has seen, and the reply commit that shows the move joins the rest. The AI service still counts them present from the move (the roles, the moved directive), so the arrival reply plays them arriving in a world that hasn't them yet, which is true before it; a group arriving in that reply speaks by its members all the same (knowledge.ts takes a present group's members from its card when the world has none yet).
- **The stand-in's one line is "The fight: …"**, what the aftermath's GM, the arc and the client's flow already read; the act's line ("Steel, dust and lantern light: …") went. story-design.md's walkthrough still shows both, as the design docs are left (M1).

##### Judgement calls

- **The owed name and the move to show, in one reply.** Both jobs' directions stand side by side in the reply's "For this reply", the move's first: it has the reply answer the player's turn briefly and end on the event, and the owed name has its holder say it "after answering the player's turn", so the name comes between the two and the event stays last. Neither text was changed for the other, and the smoke never had both at once.
- **What owes a lead's name.** The co-GM's job read a move into a lead before its reason, so any move into it owed the name (its judgement call above), and its Open names the risk: a co-GM that moves the lead before its person has been described. The merged clerk met it at once on real models (below): with the scenes' job's porters' break the way in for a player nobody has described Hobb to, a move on "none" at turn 10 had Kell name him from nothing, three exchanges before the cart. So a reason that says nothing is refused before the lead is read (H8's refusal, a lead's included), and a lead's move whose reason denies the lead itself, not only its name (`deniesTheLead`: a denial outright, before any "but", "though", "however" or semicolon, or one that denies a description or an account of who it names), is refused with nothing owed or held; one that says what has landed and denies only the name owes it as before. Of every logged move into a lead (rounds qa1 to qa3, the fake round and the smoke), it takes only qa3's g07 at turn 15 ("Not yet; nobody has described or identified the second man…"), which came before anybody had described him, and none of the five after a description. Probed on the smoke's turn 10 ($0.015 in all), the co-GM owed the name in 1 of 3 tries with only "none" refused first (moving it again on "Brann has not yet learned a description or name of the large man"), in 1 of 4 with an outright denial refused too (on "…a razored-out page in the loft, but no description or name of the man", hence the description clause), and in 0 of 4 now, each of its notes refused.
- **Kept as the co-GM's job left it:** a lead's move on a reason that says what's still to come (H7's "she will identify him in the next reply") owes the name, since it says the description has landed.

### Rolls back with the GM, in the user's words (2026-10-18)

After qa4-1018 the user played the hanged clerk on #673's preview and reported "No rols?" (`~/Downloads/kingmaker-report-20261018-110853.json.gz`). It was right: all 11 replies the report holds had `check: null`.

**Why the rolls had gone:**
- M2's re-authoring moved the dice off the scenes' routes. #666's clerk had 9 dice routes; the campaign now has 6 of 67, none in the clerk.
- 72 of the 80 discovery paths open "No dice:".
- The check rule was narrowed in rounds 1 and 2 ("a clear, risky attempt at a turning point, never for … a question"; "what your prep says for certain wins over the dice") after a failed roll lost the scene's lead (qa2's H4).

The user reviewed the mouthpiece's prompting in Obsidian and rewrote it (notes in their `lore/Mouthpiece prompt 2026-10-18`):
- **`ai/prompts/two-part-gm.md`:** the paragraph on skill checks. Rolls connect the character's stats to the role-playing and have consequences (someone on side, moved in the world, a hook). Passive Insight, Medicine, Arcana or History get a stalled conversation moving instead of an awkward hint.
- **FORMAT, "How to reply":**
  - the mouthpiece decides when to roll (the prep may suggest), regularly but spaced out;
  - rolls fall in three situations: physical feats, persuading or deceiving someone into what they wouldn't normally do, and noticing more or less, actively or passively;
  - a line on what each check is for (Insight reads a person and never makes anyone talk);
  - when to leave it null;
  - a high roll reveals more than a poor one;
  - a major failure gives another path forward;
  - a major success can bring someone round several steps.
  
  Their words are taken as written, with only typos fixed: roll, character's, require, endurance, "is to", "used in". "A high roll reveals more…" moves from the narration's bullet to the outcomes'.

**Replays.** I replayed 21 logged turns on the real model, two tries each: the report's 11, three Moll moments, and seven turns QA had flagged. It cost $0.24 over three passes.
- **Rolls:** 0 logged, against about 3.5 with this text. They come where a DM would roll:
  - Medicine or Perception on the body, graded;
  - Insight on Moll's lie, which she tells while the roll decides what the player reads;
  - Persuasion when the player bargains;
  - Investigation on the knot.
  
  Plain questions stay roll-free.
- **Tried, not in this build:**
  - the generic "people in the scene don't do the player's work" rule in place of "The companion", with Kell's specifics on her card;
  - the prep's "No dice:" read as a suggestion.
  
  Together they give about 5 rolls per 21 turns, but Kell still refuted Moll once when no roll was called.
- **Still to do:**
  - Cob's bargaining in the report (t7 to t11) still gets no roll;
  - the user wants a failed roll's repair moved to the co-GM.

**Checked:** the type check is clean and npm test passes 994 of 994, with two-part.test.ts asserting the new rule.

M1's step 24, the ruling and its plain check, went with M2.

## M3: scenes in Obsidian

### What changed

- **The scene rules are shared code:** [shared/scene/rules.ts](../shared/scene/rules.ts) has the campaign check's rules for one scene (`sceneRuleProblems`) and where the story goes from it (`sceneStoryProblems`: overtaken, assumes, an interlude going on), each problem with where it is (the scene and its field, a node, a route, a person of the cast). What they need of the campaign comes in a context: whether a lore path exists, the campaign's scenes, talk template, places and quests, and its other scenes. [hosts/dev/campaign.ts](../hosts/dev/campaign.ts) calls them with the folder as that context and prints exactly what it did (its tests are unchanged).
- **The plugin edits the game's scene files** ([tools/authoring/src/scene/](../tools/authoring/src/scene/)): opened with the game's parse, written back byte for byte when nothing changes, with an inspector for every field the format has, the cast as cards, and the scene rules run as it's edited in a problems panel that takes the author to each problem. Its README says what it does ([tools/authoring/README.md](../tools/authoring/README.md)).
- **The vault is the campaign folder:** `npm run install:vault -- ../../campaigns/caerwyn`. Its shared settings are committed (below), the rest of `.obsidian` and `.trash` are ignored ([campaigns/caerwyn/.gitignore](../campaigns/caerwyn/.gitignore)), and a scene's lore paths are the game's, relative to `lore/`.
- **Version 1 scenes open read-only**, with a note that the format has moved on. The plugin's old graph editor for them is gone (nothing else used it). The scenario and map editors keep working on their version 1 files, untouched but for what the change made them share (below).
- **Removed** (in history at `b0fca85`): the importers `scripts/import-hanged-clerk.mjs`, `import-main-campaign.mjs` and `main-scene-conversion.mjs`, with `tests/prototype-game.mjs` and the importer's tests (`import.test.mjs`, `import-cli.test.mjs`, `import-journey.spec.ts`).
- **Moved:** the plugin's `examples/` to `tests/fixtures/v1` (the scenario, the harbour map and the imported version 1 clerk, which only the old editors' tests use now). The two Objects notes (the imported clerk's) are dropped: `git show b0fca85:tools/authoring/examples/Objects/<name>.md`.
- **CI:** check.yml's `authoring` job runs the plugin's checks.
- **Docs:** the plugin's README is rewritten; ARCHITECTURE.md, the README and campaigns/README.md say the campaign folder is the plugin's vault, and name rules.ts.

### Judgement calls

- **What moved into shared/ is the per-scene rules, whole.** `sceneProblems` moved as it was, each `p(...)` given its place, and the per-scene part of `storyProblems` beside it. The talk template's rules and the vocabulary's stay in the campaign check: they need every scene's effects and the lore's private entries, or the vocabulary file, and they aren't about the scene being written. The plugin doesn't run them.
- **Where a problem is:** a lore path that isn't there is where it's first named (the scene's lore, its fronts, a person's card, a node); "no good or mixed ending that can be reached" and the runtime's own validation are the scene's; an effect's is its ending's (onEnd's and afterwards' are the scene's fields).
- **Four loop variables in shared/world are annotated `Entity | undefined`.** The plugin type-checks the shared code it imports under its own compiler options (TypeScript 5.9, no `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`), which read `world.entities[id]` as never undefined, and then refused the loops' `undefined`. Annotating them is what they always were; the alternatives were the plugin moving to the game's TypeScript and options (every index of its own code to revisit) or a hand-written declaration file for the shared types (two copies of the contract).
- **Byte for byte by never rebuilding the file.** Every scene of the campaign (and the tests' clerk) is exactly `JSON.stringify(scene, null, 2)` and a newline, so the plugin keeps the parsed object and changes one value at a time: an existing key stays where it is, unknown keys too, and a key that's new goes where the format and the campaign's files order it (the orders are in [model.ts](../tools/authoring/src/scene/model.ts), taken from the seven files; a key the order doesn't know stays after the key it followed). An optional field emptied is taken out of the file (a check unticked, requires all cleared, a fear deleted), so the file reads as one written by hand; a required one (a look, `knows`, a node's `lore`) stays, empty, for the problems to name.
- **Never a file the game can't load.** An edit whose result fails the game's shape check is refused with a note rather than saved, and the removals take what named the removed thing with them: a node takes its routes, the requires and on-failures that name it and the arrivals at it; a fact takes every beat's and route's establishes of it; a person takes the start's and events' notes for them. What the shape check allows but the rules don't (a path via someone removed, an effect naming them) stays, for the problems panel to name.
- **Ids are asked for once and shown after.** A new node's title is asked for in the add dialog (with an optional id), so its id is a slug of a real title rather than of "New discovery"; a person's id is asked for with their name (it should be short, like `kell`); a route's id is made from where it goes (`find-<discovery>`, `to-<node>`); a slug drops accents and apostrophes and keeps 48 characters. There's no renaming of an id afterwards: game state keys on them, and the format says never to rename a played scene's. A group member's id can be edited, as a slug.
- **New nodes and routes:** a discovery starts with an empty revelation and no paths, an event with nothing but its title, an ending as a good progress ending (the author grades it); a new route is a cue into an event (the authoring guide's way to an event) and an outcome otherwise, with no dice and the campaign's DC of 12 beside it. With a node selected, a new node comes with a route from it, as in the old editor.
- **Grouped routes work as the old editor's connections did:** one connection on the graph for the routes from one node to another, each route its own in the inspector, added and removed one at a time, deleted together, and moved together to other ends. A moved route keeps its id, even one made from where it went (`find-murder` moved elsewhere): the id is a name the game keys on, not a description.
- **A new scene's skeleton** is a start, a discovery and an ending (ids `start`, `discovery` and `ending`, slugs of their titles) and one route, from the start into the discovery: the format's smallest graph with each kind. Its file is named for the slug of the title, which is the scene's id (the campaign check's rule), and the problems panel lists the thirteen things left to write. It isn't added to campaign.json (the panel says the game won't install it until it's listed): which scenes a campaign has is the scenario's to say, and M4 brings the scenario to the campaign's files.
- **Which files are version 1:** a `.kmscene` with `format: "kingmaker"`, `version: 1` and `kind: "scene"` opens read-only, with its title and size and a note. Anything else that isn't a scene of the format (a version 1 scenario in a `.kmscene`, a version 2 file with a dangling route) opens as an error naming each problem, as before.
- **The old editors and the new files.** The scenario editor reads the vault's files as version 1 graphs: a version 2 scene is listed as one it doesn't read, and an open version 2 or read-only version 1 scene isn't an editor it can update in place (a port edit to a version 1 scene writes the file, which the read-only view then reloads). Its "Create new scene file" now makes a version 2 skeleton, which it can't wire. Making it read version 2 is M4's work.
- **The vault's shared settings** are two files: `.obsidian/app.json`, setting where a new note goes (`lore/`: a note made by following a link, or by hand, belongs with the GM's docs, not at the campaign's root beside campaign.json), and `community-plugins.json`, with the plugin on. Everything else is personal and ignored by an allowlist in campaigns/caerwyn/.gitignore: the workspace files and the cache as asked, but also the installed plugin (build output), appearance, hotkeys, the graph view and `core-plugins.json` (it carries choices like Sync, which is an account's). No attachment folder is set: see Open.
- **The installer adds no files to the vault** beyond the plugin's own. Its example files would have been files of the campaign, and on a case-insensitive file system the example's `Scenes/` folder is the campaign's `scenes/`.
- **The lore the plugin offers** is every note under `lore/`, but of each person's folder only their public card (their private notes, GM notes and memories are what the game keeps for them). A note's name is its folder's for a public card (`Cob`), else its file's; its kind is its top folder's (Cast, Places, World, other). The rules check a lore path against every file under `lore/`, as the campaign check does.
- **A scene's campaign** is the folder its `scenes/` folder is in, so the plugin works with the campaign folder as the vault (as set up) or with a folder above it, a whole checkout say. Its campaign.json, places and other scenes are read through Obsidian's vault (an open editor's unsaved draft standing for its file), again a moment after any file changes.
- **A path's via** is offered from the cast (their ids) and the place's things and areas by their names, since a via of one word must be a cast id and the things' ids are one word; anything typed is kept. A carried discovery's things are the place's and those another scene carries.
- **Effects:** forms for the story's seven ops, their fields in the orders the campaign's files have them, and the conditions as checklists of the scene's facts (one fact written as its id, several as a list, as the files do); changing an effect's op makes a fresh one of the new op, keeping its conditions. Any other op is JSON, saved once it parses as an object with an op; a new world op of afterwards stays a draft until it also names what it acts on (the shape check requires it).
- **Cast notes:** the start offers a note for each person there at the start (and keeps any other's), an event for everyone. An event's "who arrives here" is each person's `enters`, so ticking someone moves them from another event.
- **React Flow and the problems.** The graph keeps a node's object unless what it shows has changed: React Flow keeps a node's measured handles by its identity, and the campaign's late answer (and the problems computed from it) replacing every node lost them, so a scene opened right after another sometimes drew no edges (the talk template did, in the test that opens every scene). The problems are computed from a deferred copy of the scene, so typing doesn't wait on them.
- **The harness and the tests.** The browser harness's vault is the real campaign folder's campaign.json, places, scenes and lore (`KINGMAKER_CAMPAIGN` names another, in place of `KINGMAKER_LORE_ROOT`), opening on `scenes/hanged-clerk.kmscene`. The old editor's journeys went with it; the scenario journeys keep their own version 1 seeds in scenario-journeys.spec.ts, but for one that opened the old vault's `Places/Granary Yard.md` from the example scenario (the lore is under `lore/` now, and the scenario is M4's). The host tests run the save machinery (conflicts, echoes, late callbacks) on version 2 scenes and the scenario's port edits on maps, its own files; they bundle plugin.tsx with esbuild now, since it imports the game's shared code, with `./App` stood in for as before.
- **The bundle check is a Playwright test,** loading `dist/plugin/main.js` (or a vault's installed copy) as Obsidian loads a plugin, CommonJS with the `obsidian` module given, in a page: the bundle carries React DOM, which needs a real DOM, and the host tests' Node has none (jsdom isn't a dependency). Its mock of Obsidian's API is the host tests' in kind.
- **The plugin's version is 0.9.0,** its description the game's scenes in the campaign folder; the package keeps its name.

### Checked

From tools/authoring, after a fresh `npm ci`: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (89 of 89: the scene model's 8, the host's 36, and the version 1 model's, scenario's and map's 45); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` (45 of 45: 15 scene journeys on the real hanged clerk, the bundle check, 18 map journeys and 11 scenario journeys). Again with the root's node_modules moved aside: typecheck, build and test:host pass, so CI's job needs only the plugin's own packages. From the root: `npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (970 of 970, the shared rules' four new tests among them); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems, as before); `npm run build:site` (the campaign's new hidden files stay out of the page's content); the CI workflow test (every run step parses). Then a scratch copy of the campaign folder (`/tmp/kingmaker-m3-vault-*/caerwyn`, not the repo's): `npm run install:vault` into it put the plugin's three files in `.obsidian/plugins/kingmaker-authoring/` (its `main.js` byte for byte the build's) and left `community-plugins.json` as it was, already naming the plugin; the bundle check, pointed at that installed `main.js` and that vault (`KINGMAKER_PLUGIN`, `KINGMAKER_VAULT`), opened all seven scenes in the scene editor (each with its nodes drawn, its title, no problems, nothing written, no errors); and the copy matched the campaign folder afterwards but for the installed plugin.

### Open

- **Obsidian itself wasn't run.** The host tests and the bundle check stand in for its API; the first time in the desktop app is the user's.
- **The scenario and map editors are version 1's** until M4: they don't read version 2 scenes, and a scene the scenario editor creates is one it can't wire. *Done in M4: the scenario, map and place editors are the game's files' (below).*
- **Attachments.** Obsidian puts a pasted image in the vault, which is the campaign folder, and the campaign tools read every file of it as text (`readCampaignFiles`, so the install would carry it). Either the vault's `app.json` sets an attachment folder the tools skip, or the tools learn to skip what isn't a campaign file; left for the user's call.
- **Lore renames.** Obsidian doesn't rewrite the lore paths in a scene's JSON when a note is renamed or moved: the problems panel names each broken one, and the author re-links it.
- **A new scene isn't listed in campaign.json** by the plugin (the panel says so); listing it is the author's, or M4's scenario. *M4: the scenario (`caerwyn.kmscenario`) is the list now, and the scenario editor's Scenario tab lists a scene or makes one listed.*
- **The user's old vault** (their checkout's `lore/`) is untouched; the README says how to move.

### Fixes from the user's first try

The user tried the scene editor in the harness and asked for four things: every value of a scene authorable in the tool, nothing hard-coded (an ending's GOOD looked like a tag nobody could set: its Result was a dropdown halfway down the inspector, and its Exit kind box was cut to "Prog"); an event edited with the same [+ add …] flow as an ending's effects rather than a form listing the whole cast; the facts visible, with where they're used; and the scene's own metadata out of the inspector's little tabs, which are for the selected node.

#### What changed

- **The layout** ([SceneEditor.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/SceneEditor.tsx)): under the scene's title, a line with its problems (which opens them) and a bar of views, Graph, Scene, Cast, Facts and Problems, keyboard-reachable (a tab list: Tab, the arrows, Home, End) and remembered for the file. The inspector is the Graph's and only ever what's selected on it, with a short hint when nothing is. The Scene view ([SceneView.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/SceneView.tsx)) groups the scene's fields as asked (the scene itself, what the player knows, the prep, when and where, the world, the story, lore); the Cast view ([CastView.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/CastView.tsx)) puts the people in a list beside the chosen one's card; the Facts view is new ([facts.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/facts.tsx)); the Problems view ([ProblemsView.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/ProblemsView.tsx)) takes the author to each problem's very field.
- **The + add flows** ([inspectors.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/inspectors.tsx), [fields.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scene/fields.tsx)): every list is cards or chips, each removable, with a [+ Add …] that opens a short list in place (searchable when long, Esc to close), and every optional part a [+ Add …] until it's there: an event's pressure, arrivals and roles, the start's roles, a discovery's paths (cards with a strength choice and [+ Add a roll]), facts settled and carry, a route's requires, roll and facts, an effect's conditions.
- **Every value authorable**, with the plugin's README holding the coverage table (each field of the format and where it's created, edited and taken out). The gaps it closed: an ending's result (a choice at the top of its inspector, with a line on each for the story and QA, beside its exit kind in full; the graph's badge opens it); the ids of nodes, routes, people, facts and the scene (renamed by asking); a beat's kind (discovery or event); the world ops' and the fight's fields, and every effect's conditions (before, only the story's seven ops had forms, and a world op's condition was only in its JSON); values a file can hold that the inspector didn't show for that kind (an event's paths or required mark, a discovery's pressure or roles: shown where a file has them, to take out).
- **Facts:** made in place from any [+ Add fact] or condition (a sentence, and an id that's a slug of it unless typed) in the same step of undo as settling or testing them; the Facts view's settled by and tested by, each a link there; renamed everywhere; removed, after asking, from every establishes and now every condition too (before, a removed fact left conditions naming it, which the problems then flagged).
- **The model** ([model.ts](../tools/authoring/src/scene/model.ts)): renameFact, renameNode, renameRoute, renameCast, settlesOf and testsOf, makeEvent and makeDiscovery, idProblem; removeFact leaves the conditions. **The shared rules** ([rules.ts](../shared/scene/rules.ts)): a node's, route's or person's problem says which part of it (a dot path: `closing`, `paths.1.clue`, `effects.0`), as the scene's already said its field; the campaign check prints the messages alone, unchanged.
- **The styles:** the scene editor's rules are `.km-workshop.km-scene`, a class more than the shared styles.css's. The bundle puts styles.css after scene.css, so its equal rules won: `.field-row .field:last-child {max-width:65px}` was what cut the Exit kind box to "Prog" (and the DC beside a skill), and its `min-height:150px` made every text box tall. Text boxes now grow with their text.

#### Judgement calls

- **The inspector is the Graph's.** The other views take the whole main area, without it: the selection is on the graph, and a half-screen pane hasn't room for a view and an inspector. Narrower than 760px the inspector lies over the graph's right side, and only while something is selected (empty, it hid the nodes it asked you to click). Sizes follow the editor's own width with container queries, not the window's with media queries, since an Obsidian pane is narrower than its window.
- **Remembered per file, for the session.** The view (and the graph's pan and zoom, since the graph is unmounted while another view is open) is kept for the file's path while the plugin is loaded: reopening the file, or the editor being remade by a change from outside, comes back to it. Nothing is written to disk or to Obsidian's workspace.
- **Ids renamed, by asking.** M3 had ids made once and never renamed, since game state keys on them. The user's principle (no value the tool sets that the author can't change) and the fact renames they asked for reverse that: a node's, route's, person's, fact's and the scene's id has Rename, which checks for a free slug and warns that a game already played loses track of the old one; everything in the file naming it follows in one edit (a node: its routes' ends, requires, on-failures and arrivals; a person: the roles keyed by them in their places, the paths via them, the effects naming them, the talk's companion; a fact: establishes and conditions). Files outside the scene aren't rewritten (campaign.json, other scenes' next and overtaken, the place's people, looks.json): the problems name what's left. The scene's own id is changed in the file and not the file's name, which Obsidian renames: the problems say until they agree.
- **A removed fact's conditions go,** rather than the effects that test it: an `unless` on it is then exact (it can never be settled), and an `if` makes its effect run whatever happens, which the confirmation says, naming what settles and tests it. A list left with one fact is written as its id, as the files write one.
- **No empty roles or pressures in the file.** An empty note would reach the co-GM as an empty line. A role picked with [+ Add role] is a card with its text focused and is written once it has words; a role or a pressure emptied and left is taken out, and its card or section with it (while it's being retyped it stays).
- **A beat's kind** is a choice at the top of its inspector: a discovery made an event loses its revelation, paths and required mark; an event made a discovery gains an empty revelation and no paths and loses its pressure, its roles and its arrivals (a discovery is never the stage, and the rules want arrivals at events). It asks first when it would take something out. An ending stays an ending: it has different fields and no routes out, and it's simpler to add another.
- **Values a kind doesn't use** are shown only where a file has them, offering to take them out, never to add them: an event's paths (the rules refuse them) or required mark, a discovery's pressure and roles.
- **Effects:** a form for every op the format has, each with the fields the world's ops read (from `applyOps`: what, to, how, feet, name, kind, text, the states added and removed, quantity) or a fight's set-up (enemies, allies, at); keys a form doesn't show are named under it, and JSON edits the whole effect. Changing an op keeps the fields the two ops share in kind (who, what, text) and the conditions. Afterwards' world ops have no conditions (the game applies them whatever the ending), and a new one must say what it acts on before the file can hold it: it's a draft card until then, saved in place the moment it does, so typing goes on in the same field.
- **The result badge opens the result** with the current one focused (an arrow key changes it) rather than cycling it on a click, which would change an ending's grade by accident.
- **New nodes' defaults are as before** (an ending good and progress, a route with no roll and the format's DC of 12), shown where they're set: the ending's at the top of its inspector, the DC when a roll is added (the format keeps a `dc` with no roll; nothing uses it until then).
- **Paths move up and down,** as effects do: their order means nothing to the game, but they're numbered, and the problems name them by number.
- **The cast card says where the scene uses someone** (arrival, roles, paths via them, effects naming them), each a link: not asked for, but it's the facts' "where it's used" for people, and it's where an author renaming or removing someone looks.

#### Checked

From tools/authoring: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (92 of 92: the scene model's 11, three new for the renames, the facts' uses and the kind switch); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` (51 of 51: 21 scene journeys, among them one for each new flow, each asserting the saved file; the bundle check; 18 map and 11 scenario journeys). From the root: `npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (970 of 970); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems). Screenshots of the new layout (the graph with an event and an ending selected, a route, the Scene, Cast, Facts and Problems views, a problem clicked, and a half-screen pane) are in kingmaker-scratch/v2/m2/after/.

#### Open

- **Obsidian itself** still hasn't been run; the harness and the bundle check stand in for it.
- **Renames stay in the file:** campaign.json, other scenes, the place's people and looks.json aren't rewritten (the problems name them), and a scene's file isn't renamed with its id.
- **An ending can't become a beat,** nor a beat an ending. (Done in the author's pass below.)

#### Checked as an author

The fixes above were then checked by writing a new scene from nothing in the browser harness, with Playwright driving it and every value set through a control (the file never edited), at the harness's full width and in 700px and 1000px panes, and by going through the README's coverage table row by row against the UI.

The scene, **The Short Weight** (`scenes/the-short-weight.kmscene`): a miller accused of short-weighting the almshouse flour, whose son swaps the sacks at the loading door. Its prep (description, hook, setting, premise, truth, clock, the time, a limit, what locals know). Its cast: Osric Hale the miller (every field of a card, his roles palette, two things he knows and a secret), Brother Anselm and Tam Hale. The start, *At the mill door*, with conversation roles for Hale and Anselm. Two discoveries: *The weights are true* (the skeleton's, renamed `weights-true`: two paths, one via Hale, and the fact `weights-checked`), and *Tam swaps the sacks* (`tam-swaps`, required: three paths, via Anselm, the loading door and Tam, Tam's with an insight roll at DC 13). An event, *The cart comes early* (`cart-early`, a cue from the start), where Tam arrives, with his role there, a pressure and the fact `cart-came`. Three endings: *Tam owns up* (good, persuaded, on to caerwyn-afternoon; Anselm's attitude, and Hale's memory only if `weights-checked`), *The sacks are made good* (mixed, progress; a flag) and *Hale takes the blame* (bad, leave; Hale's attitude, and a note on him). Seven routes: the two into the discoveries (one relabelled and renamed `check-weights`), the cue into the event, and from the event into each ending; the way into the good ending from the required discovery requires it, has a persuasion roll and settles `tam-confessed`, and a second way in from the event requires it too; the mixed ending's requires the weights. The Problems view found none, and the game's own check found none: a copy of the vault's campaign folder with the scene added to campaign.json's `scenes`, `npm run campaign -- check` on it from the root, reported "7 scenes (30 beats, 71 routes, 23 endings) … no problems", and the game's loader (`sceneOf`, `validateScene`) loads it. A coverage pass then set every other field through the UI on it (the scene's rest, interlude, milestone, place, background, assumes, overtaken, onEnd with an unless, afterwards, fronts, lore and id; a person's other names, lore note, group, statblock, weapon, members and their ids, enters, and a rename; a node's lore, dragging, what the story carries and its things; paths moved and a roll removed; every effect op's form, an op changed, a promise, a quest, a fight, effects moved and removed, and an effect edited as JSON; a route's phase, a roll with a failure and its removal, a route added beside another and removed, and routes moved; a fact's sentence, rename, add and remove; the talk template's companion), checking the saved file each time: all held.

What writing it found, and what changed:

- **A new node landed on top of another.** Each node added went 400 to the right and 60 down of the node it came from, so a second discovery from the start sat exactly on the skeleton's, and an event from the start on both. A new card now goes there and then straight down until it's clear of the cards in the way (the model's `freePosition`, each card its measured size).
- **The skeleton's route kept saying "Find Discovery"** after the discovery was retitled, until the author found the route and relabelled it. A node retitled takes the labels of the routes into it that are still the ones made from its old title (`retitleNode`); a label the author wrote stays, and ids never follow.
- **An ending could never become a beat, nor a beat an ending:** the one value of the file the editor set once and didn't let the author change. A node's Kind (every node but the start) is now Discovery, Event or Ending, asking first, naming what goes, when something written would (`changeKind`, `makeEnding`, `makeBeat`, `kindLosses`). A beat made an ending loses its beat-only fields and its routes out (none leaves an ending), what required it (only a beat is required) and who arrived there, and is a good progress ending as a new one is; an ending made a beat loses its closing, result, exit kind, next scene and effects.
- **In a 700px pane, what you selected was under its own inspector,** which lies over the graph's right side there: an ending at the right, clicked, disappeared under the inspector that opened for it, and a node shown from a problem was centred on the whole canvas, half under it. A node clicked there now slides into the strip of graph beside the inspector (at its zoom), a node shown from elsewhere is centred in that strip, and a fit (the bar's, F, and the graph's own Fit View, which now goes through the editor's) fits the graph into it.
- **In a 700px pane a route couldn't be drawn to a node under the inspector,** which was usually the node it went to (routes go right). The inspector now stands aside from the moment a route is begun (Add route to…, or a drag from a node's edge) until it's made, and then opens on the new route.
- **In a 700px pane a double-click on a node didn't open its larger editor** once the slide above was in: the first click slid the node away and showed the inspector where it had been, and the second click landed on the inspector. The inspector over the graph now shows a quarter of a second after a click, the slide happens then, and a double-click cancels it.
- **The minimap covered the bottom of the cards** at the graph's right edge in panes up to 1000px; it's put away there.
- **A fight's allies and where it breaks out couldn't be left out:** a new Start a fight was written with `"allies": []` and `"at": ""` (which the rules then named as a place that isn't there), and neither could be taken out but by its JSON, though both are optional. A new one is only its enemies; allies emptied, or the place, are taken out.
- **An effect that would do nothing passed every rule.** An effect added as a form starts with its target empty (a give, a note, a move), and one left that way was saved and did nothing in play, with nothing telling the author. The shared rules ([rules.ts](../shared/scene/rules.ts), so the campaign check too) now say a world op names what it acts on (a create, what it makes) and a flag has a name; the scene format's list of rules says so.
- **An ending couldn't be sent to a scene still being written.** Next scene (and Overtaken) offered campaign.json's scenes only, so the first thing an author does with a new scene, sending the story to it from another scene's ending, couldn't be done in the tool until they'd listed it in campaign.json by hand, and nothing said why it wasn't offered. The scene files of the campaign's `scenes/` it doesn't list yet are now offered after its scenes, each saying so, and the problems say so until it's listed.
- **The Scene view's Rest and Milestone** said "A rest before it" and "A milestone of the story"; they now say what the game does with them (time passing before it opens; the character going up a level as it opens).
- **The harness had no way to begin a scene:** it now has a New scene button doing what Obsidian's New Kingmaker scene does, and `tests/new-scene.spec.ts` writes the Short Weight (shortened) through the UI as above, finds no problems, and sends the clerk's failed ending on to it.

#### Judgement calls (the author's pass)

- **Where a new card goes:** straight below where it's wanted, not the nearest free spot in any direction, so a node's new neighbours stack in a column beside it, as an author lays a graph out by hand.
- **Only a label that's still the default follows a retitle,** compared exactly with what a new route into that node would have been given from its old title: anything typed is the author's. Route ids don't follow (game state keys on them; Rename is there).
- **A kind change is a whole change, not a conversion.** A beat made an ending isn't kept "as it was" in case it comes back: what it loses goes from the file (undo brings it back), and the question names it first. The start stays the start, as a scene has exactly one.
- **The narrow pane keeps the inspector over the graph** rather than moving it below: a graph flows left to right, so a panel below hides as much as one beside, and the overlay is put away with a click on the graph's background. What changed is that it never hides what it's about, nor what's being drawn to. The quarter-second before it shows is a double-click's span, in both the CSS and the editor (a constant each, said beside the other).
- **The minimap goes at 1000px and under** (where the inspector beside the graph narrows it to about 670px), not only in the narrowest panes: at that width it covered cards at a fitted graph's edge, and the zoom controls and F remain.
- **A new rule in the game's shared rules** rather than a warning of the plugin's own: an effect that does nothing is as wrong in a file written by hand, and the campaign check should refuse it too. It's narrow (the world ops' target, a create's name, a flag's name), and every scene of the campaign keeps it.
- **Unlisted scenes are offered, not hidden,** with the problem saying so, rather than the editor writing campaign.json: campaign.json is the scenario's (M4), and listing a scene installs it in the game, which is the author's call.
- **Not changed:** the order of the cast, the facts and the lore links in the file, which the editor doesn't offer to change (an order rather than a value: new ones go last, and the talk template's companion-first is its own rule; up and down for people would be the next thing if an author wants the GM to meet them in another order); a world op in an ending stays a card from the moment it's added (the problems say what it lacks) rather than a draft as afterwards' are, which the format needs there.

#### Checked (the author's pass)

From tools/authoring: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (95 of 95: new for where a card goes, a retitled node's routes, a node's kind changed both ways, and the scene files campaign.json doesn't list); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` (54 of 54: 23 scene journeys, among them the 700px pane's and an ending made a beat and back; the new scene written from nothing; the bundle check; 18 map and 11 scenario journeys). The plugin installed into a scratch copy of the campaign folder, and the bundle check on its installed copy. From the root: `npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (971 of 971: a new rules test for effects that do nothing); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems), and on a scratch copy with the Short Weight added (7 scenes, no problems). Screenshots of the editor as it is now (the new scene's graph, an event, an ending with its kind, a route, the Scene, Cast, Facts and Problems views, and 700px and 1000px panes) replace those in kingmaker-scratch/v2/m2/after/.

#### Open (after the author's pass)

- **Obsidian itself** still hasn't been run; the harness and the bundle check stand in for it.
- **campaign.json isn't the plugin's to edit** until M4: a new scene is listed there by hand for the game to install it (the problems say until it is). *Done in M4: the Scenario tab lists it (below); the scene editor still only says so.*
- **Renames stay in the file,** as above.

### On M2's engine and loop (the restack merge)

M3 had branched from M2's foundation (`ea07041`), before M2's two-part engine and the GM loop came in; `claude/v2-scenes` (PR #670) is merged into it as `claude/v2-authoring-next`, so the plugin's scene editor and the shared scene rules sit on the game the scenes are played by. Only ARCHITECTURE.md and this log conflicted, each where both sides had added beside the same line, and both sides are kept whole: ARCHITECTURE.md has the GM loop's bullet and then the tools' as M3 words it, and this log has its sections in milestone order, M2's (its two-part engine, then the loop) before M3's. Nothing else needed changing: of the format's code M2 changed only beats.ts (the play semantics) and campaign.json's optional `gm`, neither of which the rules (shared/scene/rules.ts) or the plugin read, and the plugin's typecheck covers the shared code it imports.

Checked on the merge: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 997 of 997 (M2's 992 and M3's five rules tests); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` no problems (6 scenes, 27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings). From tools/authoring: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` 95 of 95; `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` 54 of 54, on a port of its own (a copy of the config with 4291, as another worktree may hold 4178).

### On M2's QA fixes, the salvage and the cleanup (the second restack merge)

`claude/v2-scenes` moved on a long way after the first restack merge (at `5518c16`): QA rounds qa1-1018 and qa2-1018 and their fixes, the old pipeline's salvage (memory as the GM's notes, talks on the two-part GM) and the old scene steps' deletion. It's merged into M3 again at `08113e1`, on `claude/v2-forward` (M3's tip, `9ee310d`), and `claude/v2-authoring-next` is set to the merge. Three files conflicted:
- **hosts/dev/campaign.ts:** M3 had moved the per-scene rules into shared/scene/rules.ts, and M2 had changed two of them where they were: a cue's outer limit counting from and until beats (a new check in the routes' loop) and the talk template's rules (its start's opening role for `{companion}`, `told` a discovery, a way into `goodbye` from the start). M3's structure is kept (the campaign check calls the shared rules), the limit check is in rules.ts's `routeProblems` with M2's words, at the route's `limit.<i>`, and the talk template's own rules are rules.ts's `talkTemplateProblems`, each problem with where it is (the companion field, the start's roles, `told`'s kind, `goodbye`'s ways in, the place), which the campaign check calls (its messages as M2 wrote them) beside the check that everyone who walks with the player has a lore entry, which needs the campaign's lore and stays there. That settles M2's open item for M3's rules (under the talks' salvage).
- **docs/scene-format.md:** the rules' list, where M3 had added that no effect may do nothing and M2 had rewritten the talk template's rule: both kept.
- **This log:** M2's sections (the QA rounds' fixes, the salvage and the cleanup) go before M3's, in milestone order, and this note ends M3's.

What changed on the way, for the plugin to keep up with M2's format:
- **A cue's outer limit** (M2's `limit`, a route's counts of exchanges from the start or a beat, until beats) is followed by the model: a node renamed renames it in every count, a node taken out takes the counts after it and leaves the counts' until, and a beat made an ending does the same (only a beat is counted from), a limit left with no count going; the kind change names it among what goes ("how 1 cue counts from or until it"). Without it, renaming or deleting the Rat's Arlo's challenge or the Heron's floor would have left a count naming a node that isn't there, which the format's parse refuses, so the edit would have been refused. The key goes where the format puts it, after `establishes`.
- **The route card edits it:** a cue has [+ Add an outer limit], a section of counts (each a card with Exchanges, Counted from: the scene's start or a beat, and Until: beats as chips with [+ Add beat]), [+ Add count], and the section's remove; an outcome's is shown only to take out, as an event's paths are; the card's badges say it ("By 15 exchanges in, or 8 exchanges after Arlo's challenge").
- **The talk template:** the Problems view holds it to the talk's own rules too (`problemsOf`), and its `told` says in its inspector that each talk's prep fills its revelation and paths.
- **The hints follow the format's comments as M2 changed them:** a path's Earn ("No dice:" up front, the free approaches before the roll), a route's Condition (a worse ending gives way to a better one by its id; a cue into an ending says what has it no longer needed) and the scene's Clock (how it's felt coming, never the same sign twice).
- **The README:** deleting, renaming and a kind change name the outer limit among what follows; the connection's fields, the coverage table's `data.limit` row, the Problems view's talk rules and the checks' new cases.

#### Judgement calls

- **A count after a beat that's gone goes with it,** rather than counting from the scene's start: it counted from a moment that can't happen now, and the cue's other counts (the bell's fifteen into the scene) still hold. A beat gone from a count's until just leaves it (a count left with none runs until it runs out), and a limit left with no count goes, as an empty `requires` does.
- **Adding an outer limit starts it at eight exchanges from the start,** since a count must have a whole number of exchanges to be the format's at all; the author changes it where it's shown.
- **The talk template's rules moved to the shared rules, not the lore check:** whoever walks with the player needing a lore entry reads the campaign's lore/ files by their front matter, which the rules' context doesn't carry, so it stays the campaign check's.
- **"No dice:" isn't checked by the plugin,** as M2's scenes job left it: the marker is prose for the mouthpiece's rule, so the editor says it in the Earn's hint and shows it as part of the earn.
- **Nothing else of M2's reached the plugin:** it never read campaign.json's `gm` (gone), the old pipeline's steps or the gm_state columns the cleanup dropped.

#### Checked

On the merge: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 978 of 978 (M2's 971, M3's five rules tests and the two new ones: a cue's limit naming an ending, and the talk template's rules where they are); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` no problems (6 scenes, 27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings, a talk template). From tools/authoring, after `npm ci`: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` 96 of 96 (the model's new case: a cue's outer limit following its beats renamed, taken out and made an ending, and its key's place; and the talk template's problem where it is); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` 55 of 55 (the new journey: the clerk's noon bell's limit changed, a count added after a beat and until another, taken out a part at a time and whole, undone, added again, and none offered on an outcome), on a port of its own (a copy of the config with 4291, as M3's config has no `KINGMAKER_HARNESS_PORT` yet: M4 brings it).

### On M2's QA round 3 fixes (the third restack merge)

`claude/v2-scenes` moved on again after the second restack merge (at `08113e1`): QA round qa3-1018's fixes, the co-GM's, the mouthpiece's and the scenes', merged and smoked at `06160fa` (M2's "Fixes from QA round qa3-1018"). It's merged into M3 again on `claude/v2-authoring-fwd3`, cut from `claude/v2-authoring-next` (`63e1ecd`). Three files conflicted:
- **hosts/dev/campaign.ts:** M2 had added `onStart`'s effects to the effects the campaign check holds to the rules, where the per-scene rules were before M3 moved them to shared/scene/rules.ts. M3's shape is kept (the campaign check calls the shared rules), and `onStart`'s effects are checked in rules.ts's `storyWiringProblems`, before `onEnd`'s, each problem at `onStart.<i>` with M2's words ("onStart: quest lost-dog isn't one of campaign.json's quests").
- **docs/scene-format.md:** the rules' list, where M3 had added that no effect may do nothing and M2 that the rule is `onStart`'s, `onEnd`'s and the endings': one sentence with both.
- **This log:** M2's "Fixes from QA round qa3-1018" goes before M3's section, in milestone order, and this note ends M3's.

What changed on the way, for the plugin to author M2's format, and the clerk as M2 left it:
- **`onStart` is authored wherever `onEnd` is** (M2's note for M3's editor, under its scenes' judgement calls): the scene model's key order has it before `onEnd`, where a new one goes; a fact renamed or removed follows it into `onStart`'s conditions, and the Facts view's Tested by lists its effects first ("As it starts (onStart) · effect 2 (journal), unless settled"); a person renamed follows it into its effects; the Scene view's The story has **As it starts** before On every ending, effect cards with conditions as an ending's, its hint saying what it's for (once, as the scene comes into the story, before it's played; the clerk's quest with the King's request); the Cast view's In this scene links its effects ("as it starts (effect 1)"), the Problems view places a problem in it ("Scene · As it starts · Effect 2"), and `EffectsAt` takes it. The README's Scene view, its effects bullet, the coverage table (a row for `onStart`) and its checks say so.
- **The clerk's porters' noon break, its cue and Old Wat** needed nothing new of the plugin (an event with an arrival, five roles and a pressure; a cue whose outer limit counts four exchanges after the cart until the lead; a person holding a path), and the checks hold them as the others: a host test (the event, its roles and its arrival; the cue's limit, and what counts until the lead; Old Wat renamed, with his arrival, his role in its place and the lead's path via him following and no problems; taken out, the path via him the one problem, at `paths.2.via`; the break taken out with its cue, and the lead taken out leaving the cue counting until nothing) and a journey (the card an event, clear of every other card, "Old Wat arrives"; its inspector's arrival, roles and pressure, the pressure changed; its cue's card a cue badged "By 4 exchanges after The dead-cart arrives (until Hobb was spotted at the scene)", its count changed to five; Old Wat in the Cast view as "the old porter", arriving at the break, with his role and his path, renamed `old-wat` everywhere he's named with no problems, and undone to the clerk's bytes). The tests that list the clerk's nodes, routes and cast, or what a kind change takes, count them: the views' bar's five people, the cart's three routes out made an ending, the eight connections, renamed nodes taking the cue's count with them (`after: dead-cart`, `until: [hobb-spotted]`), and Hobb made an ending taking the cue's until with it ("how 1 cue counts from or until it").
- **The porters' break's card moved** from (1000, 420), where M2's content job placed it by hand, over the suspicious and failed endings' cards, to (800, 460), under the cart in the events' column, clear of every card. Over the failed ending it covered that ending's left handle, so the journey that draws a route from the motive to the failed ending found nothing to drop it on.

#### Judgement calls

- **`onStart`'s effects keep their conditions in the editor,** as M2's note asked ("the facts' tests over its conditions as over `onEnd`'s"), though nothing is settled as a scene comes into the story (its row is made with nothing established), so an `if` there never holds and an `unless` always does. Saying so as a problem would be a rule of the format's, not the editor's (open).
- **"As it starts"** is the label (its cards "As it starts 1"), beside "On every ending": the scene's start node is "the start" in the editor, and the format's "The start" in "What it means in play" is the scene coming into the story, so the label says when, and the hint says what that means.
- **The card's position is M3's to fix:** a node's position is the editor's alone (the game never reads it), and M3 is the editor's milestone. M2's branch still has it at (1000, 420): a later change of M2's to that node's position would conflict here, any other edit of the file merges.
- **The overlap check is the clerk's new card's, not every scene's:** the Heron's endings are stacked 180 apart and its taller cards (up to 210) run a few pixels onto the next, their headers clear, as before this merge (open).

#### Checked

On the merge, on ports of its own: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1001 of 1001 (M2's 994 and M3's seven rules tests); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` "6 scenes (28 beats, 65 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 15 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems". From tools/authoring, after `npm ci`: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` 98 of 98 (two new: the clerk's porters' break, its cue and Old Wat; `onStart` held to the effects' rules at its effects; and `onStart` in the key order, the facts' and the people's renames); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` 56 of 56 (one new: the porters' break, its cue and Old Wat; the Scene view's journey with the clerk's quest as it starts; the new scene's with a journal line as it starts), on harness port 4391 (an uncommitted copy of the config, M3's having no `KINGMAKER_HARNESS_PORT`: M4 brings it).

#### Open

- **A condition on `onStart`'s effects** can't hold as the scene comes into the story: the format could refuse `if` there, or the editor drop the When section for it, if that's wanted.
- **The Heron's endings' cards** run onto each other by a few pixels (180 apart, up to 210 tall): a re-layout of the Heron's column, if wanted, is a position change only.
