## M4: the scenario, its maps and its places in Obsidian

The format is [docs/scenario-format.md](scenario-format.md), copied from kingmaker-scratch/v2/m4 as its spec was written: the campaign's scenario, maps and places become files the game loads and the plugin edits as they are, beside the scenes. The game's side came first (below), then the plugin's editors for them (after it).

### The game's side: what changed

- **The format's code:** [shared/scene/scenario.ts](../shared/scene/scenario.ts) has the scenario, map and place documents, their file names (`<id>.kmscenario` at the campaign's root, `maps/<key>.kmmap`, `places/<key>.kmplace`), a parse for each (the shape the game reads, unknown keys kept, a `FormatError` naming the file and every problem) and `asCampaignFile`, `asMapFile` and `asPlaceFile`, which make the shapes the game played on before (files.ts's `CampaignFile` and `PlaceFile`, tiles.ts's `MapFile`). Tested on Caerwyn's files ([scenario.test.ts](../shared/scene/scenario.test.ts)).
- **The campaign converted:** `campaign.json` is `caerwyn.kmscenario`, `maps/*.json` are `maps/*.kmmap` and `places/*.json` are `places/*.kmplace`, made by `tools/campaign-files.mts` (in history at `c6f9492`, deleted with the old files at `d691cb9`: nothing old is left to convert). The old files are in history at `c6f9492` (`git show c6f9492:campaigns/caerwyn/campaign.json`).
- **Every reader switched:** the pack (hosts/dev/pack.ts: the folder's scenario, places and maps loaded into the old shapes), the campaign check, the dev host and the doctor (`readCampaign`), the AI service's campaign (the PUT's `<campaign>.kmscenario`, a scene's place as a `.kmplace`) and its stores (a version is installed once its scenario is there), the in-process host and the page's (`contentCampaigns`), the page's content allowlist (`.kmscenario`, `.kmmap`, `.kmplace`) and the site's check of content.json, the simulation's test pack, the client's screen fixtures, every test that read the old files, and the authoring plugin's scene editor (the campaign around a scene is the scenario and the `.kmplace` files; its harness, installer and README too).
- **The check's rules** for the scenario, maps and places are shared code, [shared/scene/scenario-rules.ts](../shared/scene/scenario-rules.ts) (the old ones moved from hosts/dev/campaign.ts and the format's new ones), each problem with its file and where in it, for the plugin to run too.
- **Docs:** campaigns/README.md, the README's campaign row and checks table, ARCHITECTURE.md's campaigns line, the scene format's place key and quests, the arc's quests pointer, the plugin's README; and docs/scenario-format.md says what the game's side did where it had to choose ("As built").

### The game's side: judgement calls

- **The game keeps the shapes it had.** The files are converted where they're read, so `CampaignPack`, install_campaign's arguments, the campaign, place_def and map_def rows and everything played on them are as they were: `asCampaignFile` and its kin give the old files' fields in the old files' order (checked against each old file when converting), the envelope and the plugin's `layout` left with the file. Storing the documents whole, as M2 did for scenes, would have renamed fields under every reader of the rows (`name` to `title`, `key` to `id`, `version` to `campaignVersion`) and left saved games (the page's IndexedDB, a dev stack's data) holding the old shapes, for no gain: the scenes needed their words in the simulation, and these don't. The names `CampaignFile`, `PlaceFile` and `MapFile` stay, their comments saying they're the game's shapes of the files now; schema.ts's comments only, so the bindings don't change.
- **Written as the scenes are:** each new file is `JSON.stringify(doc, null, 2)` and a newline, so the plugin can keep M3's rule of saving byte for byte by changing one value at a time. The maps had been laid out by hand (a legend entry or a region to a line); written out they're longer (the town 23 KB to 30 KB), but a first save by the plugin would otherwise have rewritten every line of one.
- **Descriptions:** the envelope needs one, and the old campaign.json and maps had none, so the scenario and each map have a line written for them (the places' are their worlds' own). The maps' titles are their names as the game shows them.
- **"The loader reads either"** is taken to mean the campaign's version can come from `campaignVersion` or, in a scenario written before the rename (the campaign's version a string in `version`, no `campaignVersion`), from `version`: the parse takes it, the game loads it, and the check asks for `campaignVersion` and version 2. A scenario with `version` 2 and no `campaignVersion` is refused.
- **`maps` is required** in the scenario (the spec's interface has it so), where campaign.json's was optional: a campaign without maps lists none.
- **Finding the scenario:** a folder's scenario is its one `.kmscenario` at the root, found by its extension (the pack is handed files, not a folder's name), and the check holds its id to its file's name. The AI service looks for `<campaign>.kmscenario` by the PUT's name. A version installed in an AI service's store before the switch has no scenario, so it counts as not installed; the dev stack installs at every start, and the page serves its build's files, so nothing old is read.
- **The parse checks shape; the rules are scenario-rules.ts's**, as the scene's parse and rules.ts divide it. One exception: a thing's kind, size and material are held to the world's lists in the parse, since an unknown size makes a world that can't be built (its hit points are looked up by size). The lists are written out in scenario.ts, and a type check fails if the world's grow without them.
- **A region with a scene lays it or opens it.** The spec has every region with a scene carry a `worldOrigin`, but the town's `heron-wharf` names the Heron with a trigger and no origin: stepping onto the wharf's end opens the scene, which is played on the Heron's own map, laid by its region there. The game reads it that way (explore.ts lines a world up with the region that has its scene and an origin), so the check does: a region with a `worldOrigin` lays its scene's place, which must fit on the map from there (feet to tiles of five, rounded up: the Heron's 105 by 90 feet are 21 by 18 tiles from (2, 1) on a 23 by 19 map); one with a `trigger` and no origin opens a scene some region lays; one with neither is a problem.
- **Everyone a place starts is in its scene's cast** was already a scene rule (M3's rules.ts: "<place>'s people: <id> isn't in the cast"), so the check says it once, at the scene, rather than again at the place.
- **The files before the rules.** The check now starts with the scenario and every scene, place and map it lists: each one missing is named ("its scene drowned-rat has no file (scenes/drowned-rat.kmscene)", where it stopped at the first missing file before), and a scenario, place or map not of its format's shape stops the check there, its problems its shape's; a scene's shape still doesn't stop the rest (as before).
- **The legend:** every character a grid uses that its legend hasn't got is named, with where it's first used and how often, and every entry's terrain is one the game knows (tiles.ts exports the list), where the build stopped at the first unknown character and took any terrain. A map with a legend problem isn't built, so it says nothing more.
- **Reachable:** from the start map through the exits, the scenes the regions of those maps play, then every ending's `next` from what's reached, until nothing more is; with no start map, from the first scene. The endings are read from every scene file, whatever its shape, so a misshapen scene doesn't make the scenes after it unreachable too; a start on a map the scenario hasn't got, or a map on the way that doesn't build, is its own problem, and nothing is said of what's beyond it. On Caerwyn the regions play the clerk, the Rat, the Heron and the King's morning, and the endings reach the two interludes.
- **A few checks beyond the spec's list,** each cheap and each a mistake an author could make by hand: each file's id is its name, with a title and a description; the scenario lists nothing twice and its talk template isn't one of its scenes; a map is 160 by 120 tiles at most (the spec's limit for the painter); a region's `opensFor` and `exteriorOf` name the scenario's scenes, as its `sceneId` must.
- **The plugin's version 1 editors and the new files:** the vault now holds the campaign's scenario and maps as version 2 files, with the extensions the old scenario and map editors are registered for. They open them as files they can't read, saying so, and keep their bytes (a view never writes what it couldn't parse). The plugin's README says so; editing them is the plugin's half of M4.
- **Messages:** install_campaign refuses an id that isn't "its campaign's" (it said campaign.json's), and the scene rules' unknown quest "isn't one of the scenario's quests".
- **The milestones:** this log's list had problem reports as M4; the plan moved them to M5 when M4 became the scenario in Obsidian, so the list says so.

### The game's side: checked

From the root: `npm run typecheck` (both configs); `npm test` (980 of 980: the scenario format's five new tests and the check's five new ones beside the 970); `npm run screens` (PASS 712); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` ("caerwyn 0.1.0: 6 scenes (27 beats, 64 routes, 20 endings), a talk template, 4 places, 4 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems", as before); `npm run build:web` and `npm run build:site` (28 files; content.json's campaign is 174 files, the scenario, four maps and four places among them, nothing hidden); `npm run web:check` (PASS, 80 checks: the page installs the build's campaign from its scenario, and the yard opens and is played on the fake). The conversion checked each of the nine new files, parsed back and made into the game's shape, against its old file (it would have stopped on any difference).

`npm run e2e -- --port 3470 --ai-port 8957` once, in namespace mode (another worktree's e2e held 3430): steps 1 to 13 pass (90 checks; step 1 installs the converted campaign in both services on a real SpacetimeDB, and step 2 starts a game in the granary lane on the town's map and opens the yard by talking to Kell), and step 14, "An ending", stops as it did in M2: its fake takes the old clerk's outcome `make-case`, which the re-authored clerk hasn't got, so the scene never closes ("A waited 60.0 s for the scene to close"). That's M2's engine work, on another branch.

From tools/authoring: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (89 of 89); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` (45 of 45: the scene journeys on the real clerk, the bundle check, the map and scenario journeys). Then a scratch copy of the campaign folder (`/tmp/km-m4/vault/caerwyn`, not the repo's): `npm run install:vault` into it found the scenario ("Open a scene from scenes/"), its installed `main.js` is byte for byte the build's, the bundle check on that installed copy opened all seven scenes with no problems (so the bundled editor read the campaign from the scenario and the places), and the copy matched the campaign folder afterwards but for the installed plugin.

### The game's side: open

- **The map's knowledge of the story's scenes is still code.** shared/map/places.ts's `PLACES` (each story scene's map, region and place, who waits there before it opens, its time, the lead marker's tile and label, and where the player comes out afterwards) and `INTERLUDES` (an interlude's time and the scene it leads to) are the prototype's, written for Caerwyn's scenes. The files give a scene's region (its `sceneId`, `worldOrigin`, `trigger` and `label`), its place and its time, but the town and the simulation's start read the rest from `PLACES`, so a scene put on a map in the files and missing from it gets none of that. For a campaign authored wholly in Obsidian it should come from the files (the lead from the region that opens the scene, who waits from the scene's cast, its time from the scene, and an `after` the format hasn't got yet): a game-side follow-up, needed before a scene the plugin puts on a map plays as the arc's do.
- **The plugin's half of M4:** the scenario's diagram, the painter on the game's maps (the legend as its palette, regions, exits, people, the start), the version 1 maps' migration, a place drawn on its map, and a scene's place's things offered for its paths. Until then the old editors open the campaign's scenario and maps as files they can't read.
- **A path naming a thing the place hasn't got** (the spec's "the authoring check warns") isn't checked: a `via` is a cast id or a few words ("the body", "the tally loft") that needn't be a thing's id, and the rules have no warnings. It's the plugin's half to say how it warns.
- **`layout`** isn't written: the plugin's diagram lays out what has none.
- **Merging with M2's engine branch** (`claude/v2-scenes`, 8 commits past `ea07041`): eight files both branches change (ai/service/endpoints/pipeline.test.ts, ai/service/server.test.ts, hosts/dev.ts, hosts/local.test.ts, shared/scene/files.ts, simulation/src/host/testing.ts, simulation/src/scenes.ts, simulation/src/schema.ts). It gives the campaign a `gm` ("two-part" or "pipeline") that its tests set in campaign.json: it goes in `caerwyn.kmscenario` now (`asCampaignFile` carries a key it doesn't know on to the game's campaign, so the engine reads it as it is), and the tests set it there. *Done in the restack (below): the tests' `gm: "pipeline"` is set in the scenario, as planned here.*
- **An AI service's store from before the switch** holds versions with a campaign.json and no scenario, which count as not installed now: the dev stack installs at every start and the page serves its build's files, so only a store kept somewhere else would need its version PUT again.
- **M3's open items stand:** Obsidian itself not run, attachments in the vault, lore renames.

### The plugin's side: what changed

- **The models** ([tools/authoring/src/campaign](../tools/authoring/src/campaign/), [src/map](../tools/authoring/src/map/), [src/place](../tools/authoring/src/place/), [src/scenario](../tools/authoring/src/scenario/)): the campaign around any of its files read with the game's parses and held to the scenario format's rules with an editor's draft in place ([files.ts](../tools/authoring/src/campaign/files.ts)); the scene model's way of editing JSON a value at a time for every kind ([json.ts](../tools/authoring/src/campaign/json.ts)); a map's grid as cells, written back a row at a time, its legend, regions, exits and people, resizing and the default legend ([map/model.ts](../tools/authoring/src/map/model.ts)); a version 1 map's migration ([map/migrate.ts](../tools/authoring/src/map/migrate.ts)); a place's feet and tiles and its parts moved, added and removed ([place/model.ts](../tools/authoring/src/place/model.ts)); the story's flow drawn from the files that own it, and the layout ([scenario/model.ts](../tools/authoring/src/scenario/model.ts)).
- **The editors:** the scenario as a diagram of the story with an inspector for its own fields ([ScenarioEditor.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/scenario/ScenarioEditor.tsx)); each map on the isometric painter, its legend the palette, with regions, exits, people, the start and a scene's place laid over its region, and the game's renderer as its preview ([MapEditor.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/map/MapEditor.tsx)); a place on its own grid ([PlaceEditor.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/place/PlaceEditor.tsx)); a version 1 map's migration ([Migration.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/map/Migration.tsx)) and a version 1 scenario read-only. The scene editor picks a path's via from its place's things and warns of one naming neither someone of the cast nor a thing of the place, and lists its place's things.
- **The host** ([plugin.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/plugin.tsx)): the four kinds of file each in its editor; reads of another file give an open editor's draft first; an editor's edit of another file is written only if that file is what it read (on disk, or in an editor that has it open, which is given the new text), atomically when it's closed; new scenes, maps, places and a scenario from the file menu and the commands. The browser harness ([browser.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/browser.tsx)) is the same over the real campaign, its maps and looks now too.
- **Removed** (in history at `820a33f`): the version 1 scenario editor (`src/ScenarioEditor.tsx`), map editor (`src/MapEditor.tsx`, `MapFeatureDialog.tsx`, `map-features.ts`, `MapHover.tsx`, `map-pixi.ts`, `map-stroke.ts`, `map-preview.js`), their model (`src/model.ts`, `src/map-model.ts`) and their tests (`tests/model.test.mjs`, `scenario.test.mjs`, `map.test.mjs`, and the old `map-journeys.spec.ts` and `scenario-journeys.spec.ts`, rewritten for the game's files).
- **Tests:** the models' (`tests/map-model.test.mjs`, `migrate.test.mjs`, `place-model.test.mjs`, `scenario-model.test.mjs`, and the via warnings in `scene-model.test.mjs`) on the campaign's real files and a copy of the user's own harbour study (`tests/fixtures/v1-study/Harbour study.kmmap`, byte for byte their old vault's `Maps/Harbour study.kmmap`); the host tests ported to the game's files; the journeys (`map-journeys`, `scenario-journeys`, `place-journeys`, and the via in `journeys`), each asserting the files as saved; the bundle check opening the scenario, every map and every place too. check.yml's `authoring` job runs them as it ran M3's (every `tests/*.test.mjs` and `tests/*.spec.ts`); it needed no change.
- **Docs:** the plugin's README (the scenario, map and place editors, version 1 maps, the files, the checks), ARCHITECTURE.md's tools line, the README's pointer to the plugin, campaigns/README.md, and the scenario format's "As built (M4, the plugin's side)". The plugin is version 0.10.0, its description the campaign's files.

### The plugin's side: judgement calls

- **The version 1 editors go, their files don't.** A version 1 map opens with what it holds and the offer to migrate it (the spec's "offers the migration"); a version 1 scenario opens read-only, as M3 made version 1 scenes: its wiring was of version 1 files, and with those read-only or migrated there's nothing of the game's to keep wiring. Their bytes are never written but by a migration asked for.
- **The flow's edges are the owners', always.** Each edit of an edge is written to the file that owns it and nothing is kept in the scenario but `layout`: an ending's `next` in its scene, a region's `sceneId` and an exit's destination in its map, the start's map and tile and the first lead in the scenario. Taking a destination away (the Delete key on a way out, selected by its edge or its port) asks first, and means what the owner can mean: an ending then ends the story, a region stops playing its scene (it stays a region), a way out is deleted (an exit can't lead nowhere), and the start without a map opens the first scene at once. Choosing "Nowhere" in an ending's own select doesn't ask: it's the choice itself. The first lead can't be taken away (the format requires it).
- **Ways out are grouped** by where they lead and what they say: the town's four wharf tiles to the Heron are one port, re-aimed together, each landing where it does; aimed at another map they land just inside its way back to the map they're from (the standable tile nearest it that isn't a way out, a step straight out before one aslant, so the palace gate's way lands where the town's file has it), or nearest its middle. The author moves the tile in the inspector or on the map, and the check says if nobody can stand there.
- **The diagram's cards:** the start, the scenario's scenes in the story's order along the top, its maps beneath. The talk template isn't drawn (it's never on the story's way): the Scenario tab lists it. A region that opens a scene laid elsewhere (the wharf's end) is a dashed edge, as is the first lead.
- **The layout's keys** are `start`, `scenes/<id>` and `maps/<id>`, since a scene and a map can share an id (the Drowned Rat is both). It's written only when a card is dragged, in whole pixels; nothing places what it doesn't, so a new scene or map goes where the default puts it.
- **Writes to other files are text, compared whole.** An editor reads a file as an open editor's draft first (refused when two open editors of it disagree) and writes it only if it's still that text, on disk or in every editor that has it open; an open editor is given the new text as if the file changed outside it, and starts again from it (its undo, as for any change from outside). An editor that can't take an edit (a version 1 map's offer, a file that didn't open) refuses one. What's written is held to the game's parse first. These are the version 1 overview's guarantees (conflicts, atomic writes to a closed file, an editor opening during a write, outside edits never overwritten), carried over to the game's files, and their host tests ported.
- **Undo spans files, and writes queue.** A step is the editor's own file and the other files the edit wrote, all or nothing (a write that fails puts back the ones before it); undo writes each back only if it's still what the edit made. An edit of another file shows at once and its write follows the last one, so typing in a field of the place laid on a map doesn't drop letters, and the typing in one field is one step there as it is in the editor's own file.
- **New files from the scenario** (a scene, a map, a place) are made from their skeletons (M3's scene, a map from the default legend, a place of one area) and listed; undo takes them off the list and leaves the file, as the version 1 overview did. A quest's key is made once from its title, since the scenes' effects name it. Unlisting asks first.
- **The legend is the palette, every entry its own.** An entry's name is its decor's, or its look and terrain ("Carpet floor under a palace roof"), with its character and its count. A new entry's character must be one you can see, not in the legend; it can't be changed after (the grid is written in it), and deleting one that's painted asks what replaces it. Blocks and opaque are "as its terrain", yes or no, since a door that blocks is an override the town uses. Keys the format doesn't know are kept in an entry and its decor; a decor's name and glyph are always written (the format requires them).
- **The default legend** is the version 1 palette and its clutter, as the spec says, the characters the town uses where it has them (`,` grass, `.` cobbles, `_` boards, `~` water…) and the clutter on cobbles (`l` a lamp, `s` a sign, `k` a crate, `b` a barrel, `n` a bench), each writing only what differs from its terrain's defaults. A new map is 32 by 24 of grass, the version 1 editor's blank.
- **The grid a row at a time.** The painter works on cells and writes back only the rows it changed; a changed row keeps its own length where what's past it is the void (the palace's first row stays five tiles), so painting a map changes its file where it was painted. The void is a palette entry like any other. The first paint and erase materials are the map's two most used entries.
- **The painter's look** is the game's: each entry's colours from the renderer's palette (client/map/palette.ts), lifted for the authoring view, its decor's glyph drawn on it, the void a dark outline. The atlas is made again when the legend changes.
- **Regions:** added from a tile's menu or drawn with the region tool (**N**), named in a dialog, their id a slug of the name free across the campaign's maps (the check's rule); a region's rect, origin and the rest are its inspector's. Selecting a region that lays a scene's place shows the place (the spec's "select a region"), and a button shows or hides it.
- **The start** is set from a tile's menu ("the spawn point is the scenario's start"), and lists the map in the scenario's maps when it isn't, if the file is `maps/<id>.kmmap`; on a map anywhere else it's refused, since the start can only be on one of the campaign's maps. Its flag on the map moves it.
- **Resizing** keeps the version 1 painter's handles, up to 160 by 120 and down to 2; new tiles are the Erase material. A north or west edge moves the map's regions (and their origins), exits and people, and also what other files say of its tiles: the scenario's start and other maps' exits that land on it, in the same step. Cropping asks, naming what goes (a region is cut to what's left of it).
- **A place on its map** is the region's scene's place at its origin, a spot in feet on the tile the game puts it on (`floor(feet / 5)` from the origin). Areas are rectangles with a label to drag; things with a spot of their own are markers by their kind; things held by someone or in something aren't drawn (they're a thing's contents, listed in its inspector); people are their looks (looks.json; the player `@`), someone without a spot at their area's corner until moved. A thing or someone dragged keeps where in its tile it was and goes into the area under it, but a held thing stays held. Removing a thing takes what's in it; an area goes only once it's empty; a person's start goes alone. A place opened by itself is drawn from above on its own grid (five-foot squares), not on a map: that's the spec's "edited on its own grid", and it names the regions that lay it.
- **A path naming a thing the place hasn't got** is a warning, the plugin's, as the game's side left it: the rules have no warnings and a via is words, so the campaign check is unchanged. A via of one word is a cast id (the rules' own check), so only a via of more than one word is weighed, against the cast (id, name, called, also known as) and the scene's place's things and areas (id or name, since "the tally loft" is an area), as words: no case, no leading article, nothing in brackets ("the counting-box" is "the counting-box (up a short stair, over the floor)"). A scene with no place (the interludes) has nothing to check. The warnings are listed apart from the problems, under them, and not in the tab's count, which stays the campaign check's; the toolbar counts both. The via picker writes a thing's or area's name, as M3 offered them, since its id would be one word. On the campaign that's nine warnings in four scenes, each a thing the scene's prose has and its place hasn't: left for the author.
- **A version 1 map's migration:** its id is its file's name, slugged (`Harbour study.kmmap` is `harbour-study`, as its version 1 id was). Each palette id used and each pair of a palette id and clutter is a legend character, only those used, as the spec says (the New entry dialog offers the rest as presets); the default legend's characters, so a migrated map paints like a new one, and clutter on anything but cobbles a character of its own, its clutter's in capitals if free (the study's lamps on grass are `L`). A spawn point becomes the scenario's start only when the author says so and the map is the campaign's (`maps/<id>.kmmap`); otherwise it's kept as a region of one tile named for it, so nothing is lost. An interaction whose exit led to a map is an exit (landing on the version 1 map's spawn it named, or just inside a map of the format), one whose exit led to a scene a region that plays it (opened by interacting, its port's name the label), and any other a region of one tile, its event message its description and its script notes kept as `notes`, a key the format keeps. The file's other keys (its lore, when it had any) are kept. Its id isn't checked against the scenario's other maps' regions' ids beyond the ones it makes (they're made free of the campaign's).
- **The user's study** is copied into the tests byte for byte, and the journey puts it in the harness's `maps/` as `harbour-study.kmmap`, so the start question is asked; the model test migrates it both ways and checks it tile for tile.
- **Problems in the map and place editors** are the campaign check's for their file (and for the place shown on a map), each taking the author to its region, exit, person or legend entry; and a note when the file isn't one of the scenario's or isn't named for its id.
- **The Game preview reaches the game's renderer through a JS module with a declaration** (`src/map/render.js`), as the version 1 preview did: importing client/map/render.ts from TypeScript would have the plugin's type check follow it into the client's code, which uses Node's timers and is checked under the game's options; CI's job has no root packages to give it those types.
- **The form styles are shared:** the scene editor's field, inspector and problems styles apply to the new editors too (`:is(.km-scene,.km-forms)`), rather than a second copy.

### The plugin's side: checked

From tools/authoring: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (68 of 68: the scene model's 9, the host's 37, the map model's 7, the migration's 3, the place model's 6 and the scenario model's 6); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` (29 of 29: 16 scene journeys, the bundle check, 8 map journeys, 3 scenario journeys and the place journey). The commit with the editors was also checked as its own tree, exported with no root `node_modules` (typecheck, the host tests and the journeys), as CI's job runs. From the root: `npm run typecheck`; `npm test` (980 of 980); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` (no problems, the same line as before).

A real map and place edited through the plugin's test host and saved: in the browser harness, the town given a new legend entry (a market cross, painted on the square), boards painted into the river, a region and a person added, and the granary yard shown on it with a thing added and its fields set; the palace given an exit back to the town. Written into a scratch copy of the campaign folder (`/tmp/km-m4p/caerwyn`), the files changed only where they were edited (the two painted rows, the new entries), `npm run campaign -- check /tmp/km-m4p/caerwyn` says "no problems", and the game's own loaders take it: the pack the simulation installs (hosts/dev/pack.ts), every map built as the game builds it (the town 64 by 46, 22 regions, 31 people, its decor with the cross) and every place's world (the yard's with the pigeon in the yard).

Then a scratch copy of the campaign folder as a vault (`/tmp/km-m4p/vault/caerwyn`): `npm run install:vault` into it put the plugin there (its `main.js` byte for byte the build's), the bundle check on that installed copy opened every scene, the scenario, every map and every place with no problems and nothing written, and the copy matched the campaign folder afterwards but for the installed plugin.

### The plugin's side: open

- **Obsidian itself wasn't run**, as in M3: the host tests and the bundle check stand in for its API. The first time in the desktop app is the user's.
- **The map's knowledge of the story's scenes is still code** (the game's side's first open item): a region the plugin ties to a scene plays it on the map as the files say, but who waits there, the lead marker and where the player comes out after are still shared/map/places.ts's for Caerwyn's scenes.
- **An editor given another's write starts again from it**, losing its undo, as for any change from outside; two editors of one file open side by side see each other's edits that way.
- **Renaming** a map, a place or a scene (whose id is its file's name) isn't the plugin's: Obsidian's rename doesn't rewrite the scenario's lists, the exits that lead to a map or a scene's place, and the problems name what breaks. Ids (a region's, a legend character, a quest's key, a thing's) are made once and not renamed. *Done in the author's pass (below): each is renamed by asking, with what names it in its file following (a quest's key in every scene's effect, in their files too); a file still isn't renamed with its id.*
- **The nine via warnings** on the campaign's scenes are the author's to settle (pick the thing, add it to the place, or keep the words).
- **A region that opens a scene laid elsewhere** doesn't show the place (it's laid by the other region, on its own map).
- **The version 1 scenario** isn't migrated: it's read-only.
- **M3's open items stand:** attachments in the vault, lore renames; and the game's side's: merging with M2's engine branch, an AI service's old installs.

### Reviewed

M3 and M4 were reviewed together on this branch (claude/v2-scenario at `eb9440a`, holding M3 as it was at `a0acbdc`), as they'd reach main: every check run again from scratch, the one-format claims held to the files, and the plugin used as an author would use it, in the browser harness driven by Playwright.

- **Nothing broken.** From tools/authoring after a fresh `npm ci`: the type check, `test:host` (68 of 68), the build and Playwright (29 of 29); then the same on a `git archive` export of the branch with no `node_modules` anywhere above the plugin, as CI's job has it (its steps are the README's checks, and they pass: only its comment was M3's). From the root after a fresh `npm ci`: `npm run check` (the type checks, 980 of 980, the screens PASS 712), the campaign check (no problems, the same line), `build:site` (28 files), `build:web` then `web:check` (PASS, 80 checks; content.json's 174 campaign files); and `npm run e2e -- --port 3490 --ai-port 8977` in namespace mode: steps 1 to 13 pass (90 checks), and step 14, "An ending", stops where M2 and the game's side said it would ("A waited 60.0 s for the scene to close": its fake takes the old clerk's `make-case`, M2's engine work on claude/v2-scenes).
- **One format, no copies.** The campaign folder has its scenario, scenes, maps and places as `.kmscenario`, `.kmscene`, `.kmmap` and `.kmplace` files and no `campaign.json`, `maps/*.json` or `places/*.json`; no tracked JSON anywhere has a scenario's, a map's or a place's fields (ai/gm/testdata/scenes holds the GM tests' frozen runtime scenes, M2's); and the game's readers name only the new extensions. Every real file (the scenario, seven scenes, four maps, four places) opens in the plugin's models and saves to the same bytes, a map through the painter's cells and back too. The diagram's edges, worked out from the files apart from the plugin (each ending's `next`, each region's `sceneId`, dashed without a `worldOrigin`, the exits grouped by map and label, the start's map and first lead), are the 31 the plugin's `flowOf` gives and the 31 the page draws, none missing and none extra.
- **Usable.** In the browser harness, as an author, every value through a control: a new scene from the Scenario tab (The Lamplighter: its prose, two people, a discovery with two paths, one via a thing picked from its place, an event, and a good and a bad ending, each problem cleared from the problems panel); a new place (Chandler Row) with two things; a new map, cropped to 17 by 15 and painted (a cobbled street, a wall with a door, three lamps), with a region that lays the scene's place and plays it, and ways to and from the town's Cheap Alley, so the scene can be reached; then 34 ways on the diagram and no problems. Written into a scratch copy of the campaign folder, the files changed only where they were edited (the town's file gained its way out, the scenario three list entries), `npm run campaign -- check` says "7 scenes (29 beats, 68 routes, 22 endings), a talk template, 5 places, 5 maps, …; no problems", the game's pack takes it (the install's arguments round-trip), the new map builds as the game builds it, the place's world has its two things and the scene's people, and the copy's 34 edges are the files' and the diagram's alike.
- **The log** is as run: the history it points at is there (`b0fca85`, `c6f9492`, `820a33f`, `779c6b5`), its numbers are the checks' (the plugin's tests file by file too), the via warnings are nine in four scenes, and the user's study is byte for byte their old vault's.

What the review changed, a commit each:

- **A place's parts could be put off its floor** (`095ed29`). A right-click just off the new place's grid offered to add a thing there, and added the ladder at (-7, 5) feet with nothing saying so; the map's menu offered a laid place's parts on any tile, and a drag went wherever the pointer did. The editors now offer a place's parts only on its floor (on a map, only on its tiles), a thing or someone dragged past an edge stops just inside it and an area stops at the edge, and the campaign check names an area past the place's edges and a spot or a start off its floor.
- **The dark views' hover under a light theme** (`57721a9`): the map, place and migration views took the host's hover tint, which a light theme makes pale under their pale text (a place's rows of areas and things, a toolbar's key chips).
- **A new scene's or map's card out of sight** (`32357fa`): made from the Scenario tab, it went at the end of its row, past the diagram's right edge, and the diagram didn't move to it.
- **check.yml's comment** (`74e0d12`) said the authoring job ran on the scenes alone.

Judgement calls:

- **The place's floor is the check's rule, not only the editors'.** The game takes any spot, but it lays a place on its map from its corner, so what's past its edges stands off the place's tiles (the review's ladder was two tiles off its region). The rule is strict, from 0 up to but not at the width and depth (a spot at the far edge is on the next tile); Caerwyn's places have nothing off their floors. On a map, a place's part-covered last row and column still offer its parts, their spot brought onto its floor (the yard's 52 feet end two feet into its eleventh tile, so a thing added there stands at 51).
- **A drag is kept on the floor, not refused:** it stops just inside the edge it went past. A spot typed in the inspector stays as typed, for the problems to name.
- **The new card is brought into view, not selected:** the selection and the tab stay as they were, so several can be made in a row.
- **The scene editor's rough edges met while authoring aren't fixed here.** M3's author's pass on claude/v2-authoring had fixed each already (a node added from the skeleton's discovery landing on its ending, `eddc71d`; the skeleton's "Find Discovery" label after the discovery is retitled, `93aaa41`; a problem taking the author to a person's card but not to the field, `135f7e3`), and fixing them again here would only add to what the restack below must reconcile.

Checked again after the fixes: from tools/authoring, a fresh `npm ci`, the type check, `test:host` (69 of 69), the build and Playwright (29 of 29), and the same on a clean export of the branch; from the root, a fresh `npm ci`, `npm run check` (the type checks, 981 of 981, the screens PASS 712), the campaign check (no problems, the same line), `build:site` (28 files), `build:web` and `web:check` (PASS, 80 checks), and the e2e once more (steps 1 to 13 pass, 90 checks; step 14 as before). The scratch copy authored above still has no problems under the new rule, and with its ladder put back at (-7, 5) the check names it ("thing the-ladder stands at (-7, 5), off the place (40 by 30 feet)").

Open:

- **This branch is behind M3.** claude/v2-authoring has 19 commits after `a0acbdc`, where this branch began (M3's author's pass, 2026-10-18: the scene editor's views and facts, problems by field, ids renamed, an ending going on to a scene not yet listed, …), and they read campaign.json, which this branch replaced with `caerwyn.kmscenario`. A trial merge (`git merge-tree`, which writes nothing) conflicts in 13 files: campaigns/README.md, docs/migration-log.md, docs/scene-format.md, shared/scene/rules.ts, tools/authoring/README.md, src/browser.tsx, and in src/scene ProblemsPanel.tsx, SceneEditor.tsx, ScenePanel.tsx, campaign.ts, inspectors.tsx, scene.css, with tests/scene-model.test.mjs. M3 replaced ProblemsPanel and ScenePanel with ProblemsView and SceneView, which M4's `f866a98` (a scene's campaign read from the scenario and the places) and `261be9d` (vias picked from the place's things, the warnings apart from the problems) changed: those come back on M3's views, and M3's reads of campaign.json move to the scenario, when this branch is restacked on M3. *Done: restacked on M3's author's pass and M2's engine and loop (below).*
- **The e2e's step 14** is M2's engine work. *Done: on the restack the e2e is M2's, on the two-part GM, and passes every step (below).*
- **Noted, not changed:** shrinking a map from the keyboard asks to crop at each press (a drag asks once); a place's header says "Laid on chandler-row at chandler-row (2, 5)" when the map and its region share an id; a place's things show their names on its grid only on hover; a region made to lay its scene's place while it's selected shows the place only when asked. *All four done in the author's pass (below).*
- **Still standing:** Obsidian itself not run; attachments in the vault; lore renames; the places code (a scene put on a new map passes the check and the game's loaders, but who waits there, its lead marker and where the player comes out after aren't in the files).

### On M3's author's pass and M2's engine and loop (the restack)

This branch had begun from M3 at `a0acbdc`, before M3's author's pass and before M2's engine and loop. It's restacked as `claude/v2-scenario-next`: `claude/v2-scenario` with `claude/v2-authoring-next` merged in (M3's author's pass, itself with M2's `claude/v2-scenes` merged in), M3's design first wherever the two met. Sixteen files conflicted: the thirteen the review's trial merge named, and ARCHITECTURE.md, README.md and ai/README.md, which M2 brings.

- **The scene editor's code.** M3 replaced the ProblemsPanel and ScenePanel this branch had changed with its Problems and Scene views: both old files go, and what this branch did in them is redone in the views. The Problems view lists the via warnings under the problems, apart and not in the view's count, each taking the author to its discovery; the Scene view lists the place's things under its Place, with Open the place. The path cards (M3's) have the via picked from the cast and the place's things and parts (Pick via, named with their ids) and the note under a via that names neither. campaign.ts keeps this branch's scenario and M3's scene files not listed yet, and every word M3 wrote about campaign.json names the scenario (the problems' note, Next scene's and Overtaken's choices, the quest effect's hint, the Scene view's id warning). The harness keeps this branch's editors and file host with M3's New scene button; M3's version 1 graph host goes with the editors this branch removed. shared/scene/rules.ts has M3's rules for an effect that would do nothing beside this branch's "isn't one of the scenario's quests".
- **What M2's code needed for the scenario's files.** M2's engine reads the campaign through the readers this branch switched (the pack, readCampaign, the test pack, the AI service's loader), so it merged as it was. What read campaign.json itself: the loop's campaign loader (qa/loop/scenes.ts: its id, version and scenes now its scenario's, found and parsed as the AI service does) and its stack's installed copy (hosts/loop.ts: a version the AI service kept counts when its scenario is there, as the stores count it), the local host's tests (their `gm: "pipeline"` set in the scenario) and the beats test of every scene's thorough end (the story's scenes from the scenario). The comments and docs that named campaign.json's `gm` name the scenario's (the simulation's test pack and `playsTwoPart`, ai/README.md, two-part-design.md).
- **The docs:** both sides kept: ARCHITECTURE.md's loop and this branch's tools line, the README's check row and the loop's, ai/README.md's errors (the scenario) and its ceiling (M2's, with no notes), campaigns/README.md's check (this branch's) with M3's Problems view, the scene format's effect rule (M3's) with the scenario's quests, the plugin's README on M3's views with this branch's additions, and this log with M3's later sections under M3, before M4.

#### Judgement calls

- **The warnings are counted on the line under the scene's title** ("No problems · 3 warnings"), which M3 made what the toolbar's count was; the Problems tab's count stays the campaign check's. M3's journeys that read that line now expect each real scene's warnings on it (nine in four scenes, as before).
- **The scenario, map and place editors keep their look.** They take the scene editor's form controls and problems styles (`.km-forms`), which M3 rewrote at one class more than this branch had them, and that beat their own stylesheets (a fieldset's border, a dialog's tighter fields, a code's font) and wrapped a region's x, y, w and h onto two rows. The shared rules are M3's declarations at this branch's specificity, so those editors' own rules still win; their rows of fields stay on one line; and what they use of the scene editor's old inspector, which M3's has no more (its tabs, a toolbar's count, a section with its count, the kind over its fields), is kept for them. Screenshots of the scenario's tab, a new region's dialog and inspector, and a place match this branch's but for M3's textareas, which grow with their text.
- **`Section` stays** in fields.tsx for the scenario's inspector, where M3 replaced it with groups in the scene editor's views. Making the scenario, map and place editors follow M3's design is their own pass, not the restack's.
- **NumberField reports undefined only when it's optional** (its type says so), so this branch's 22 number fields compile as they were written, where M3's change of its type to take an empty field out would have had each one handle a value it never gets.
- **`gm` stays a key the scenario keeps** and the game carries onto its campaign, as this branch's note planned, not a field of the scenario format: it's the tests' switch, and Caerwyn says nothing. *Overtaken at the second restack (below): M2's cleanup took the switch, so nothing sets or reads `gm` now.*

#### Checked (the merge)

From the root: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1008 of 1008 (M3 and M2's 997 and this branch's 11); `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` no problems (the same line). From tools/authoring: the type check, `test:host` 75 of 75 (the version 1 model and editors' tests this branch removed are gone, M3's scene model tests are kept), the build, and Playwright 38 of 38 (M3's 23 scene journeys, the new scene and the bundle check, this branch's 8 map, 3 scenario and place journeys, and the via journey on M3's views), on a port of its own.

### The author's rules for every editor (the scenario, map and place editors' pass)

The user's rules for the authoring plugin's editors came from their first try of the scene editor, and M3's rework applied them to scenes (above): everything a file can hold authorable by hand, where an author would look first, the tool setting no value the author can't change; the right-hand panel the selection's inspector only; a document's own fields in views of their own, on a bar under its title; lists as **[+ add]** cards or chips, each removable, an optional part **[+ Add …]** until it's there; cross-references visible both ways, and made where they're picked; and a check by writing a new document from nothing with only the UI, in panes 700px and 1000px wide. This pass applies them to M4's three editors, writes the coverage tables and closes their gaps, fixes the review's four small points, and writes a new map, its place and the scene laid there from nothing.

#### What changed

- **Shared** ([campaign/views.tsx](../tools/authoring/src/campaign/views.tsx), [views.css](../tools/authoring/src/campaign/views.css)): the scene editor's design for the other three: the title with its problems (a button to the Problems view), the bar of views (keyboard-reachable, remembered for the file), the inspector (what's selected, with its kind and id in its header; in a pane 760px wide or less over the canvas while something's selected, showing a double-click's span after a click), a list beside the chosen item's card (the Cast view's), uses (where something's named, each a link), word chips, the new-file form, the problems list, and the keys the format doesn't have.
- **The scenario editor** ([scenario/](../tools/authoring/src/scenario/)): **Diagram**, **Scenario**, **Files**, **Quests** and **Problems**. The inspector is the diagram's card or way out, and nothing else; the old Selected and Scenario tabs are gone. The Scenario view groups the campaign, the start, the starting purse and the world a backstory is written for; the Files view has the scenes (in order), the talk template, the maps and the places as cards, each saying where the other files name it; the Quests view has each quest with the effects naming it. The diagram's pan and zoom are kept while another view is shown, and a card clicked in a narrow pane slides into the strip beside the inspector.
- **The map editor** ([map/](../tools/authoring/src/map/)): **Paint**, **Preview** (the game's renderer, which was a mode switch), **Map**, **Legend**, **Regions**, **Exits**, **People** and **Problems**. The inspector is what's selected on the painter: a region, an exit, someone about, a legend entry, the start, a part of the laid place, or a tile, picked with the new **Select** tool (**V**), which says what's on it and offers what a right-click offers. The Map view has the map's own fields, its size by numbers, the story's start on it, the ways in from other maps and the places laid on it; the Legend, Regions, Exits and People views list theirs beside the chosen one's card, with **Show on the map**; people are filtered by the hour there and on the painter. In a narrow pane the palette is its swatches, the tools their icons, and a selection on the painter moves into the strip beside the inspector.
- **The place editor** ([place/](../tools/authoring/src/place/)): **Plan**, **Place**, **Areas**, **Things**, **People** and **Problems**. The plan names its things and people under their markers, its inspector is the selected area, thing, start or spot of bare floor, and in a narrow pane the plan fits the strip beside it; the Place view has its own fields and where it's used (the scenes played in it, the regions that lay it).
- **The review's four points:** a keyboard crop asks once for an edge while its handle keeps the focus (and again if more than the ground would go); a place says where it's laid by the map's title and the region's name ("On Chandler Row, over “the chandler's shop” (4, 3)"), not "Laid on chandler-row at chandler-row"; a place's things and people are named on its plan (and on a map it's laid on), with a toggle; and a region selected shows its place the moment it lays one, with no button to press.
- **Docs:** the plugin's README has the three editors rewritten on this design, and a coverage table for the scenario, a map and a place (each field and where it's created, changed and taken out); the checks name the new journeys and `KINGMAKER_HARNESS_PORT` (the tests' harness port, for checkouts side by side).

#### The coverage tables' gaps, found and closed

Going through every field of the three formats against the editors (README, "Every value, and where it's edited: the scenario, maps and places"):

- **Values the tool set and the author couldn't change:** the scenario's, a map's and a place's id (renamed in the file now, as M3's scene id: the problems say until the file's renamed to match); a quest's key (made once from its title: renamed now, every scene's effect naming it following in its own file, one step of undo); a legend entry's character (the grid is written in it: renamed now, the grid rewritten where it's painted, only those rows changing); a region's id; an area's and a thing's id (what's in them follows).
- **Values nothing showed:** a thing's `ac`, `hp` and `quantity` (the format and the game have them; the inspector had none); keys the format doesn't have (a migrated region's script `notes`, the tests' `gm` in a scenario), kept but never seen: shown now under the file's own fields or the region's, edited when they're text, and taken out.
- **Values only reachable by a right-click, or only in a summary:** adding a region, an exit or someone needed a right-click on the map; now also the Select tool's tile inspector and **[+ Add …]** in each list view (its tile asked for in the dialog). The map's regions, exits and people were rows of a panel that selected them; now each is a card with every field, beside the map.
- **Lists that weren't + add cards or chips:** the docs that aren't canon were typed lore paths (chips now, picked from the lore); a decor's, someone's, a thing's and an area's states and tags were boxes of text (chips); the purse's coins couldn't be moved (cards, moved and removed).
- **Optional parts always open:** a region's shut and opens for, locked after, exterior of and locked doors were five fields on every region; each is a **[+ …]** section now, and **[+ Play a scene here]** holds the scene, its place and its trigger. A decor is **[+ Something stands on it]**, a thing's armour and hit points **[+ Its own armour and hit points]**.
- **References made only where they're kept:** a region's scene, an exit's map, an ending's next scene, the first scene, the start place and map and the talk template had to exist (listed in the scenario) before they could be picked; each picker makes one now, made from its skeleton, listed and picked in one step. A scene's place, from the region that lays it, is chosen or made there (written in the scene's file). A place's start can be someone new of its scene's cast, made in the scene's file with their start.
- **References one way only:** a scene now says where it's played and what goes on to it, a map its ways in, a place its scenes and regions, a quest its effects, a thing the paths and effects that name it, and someone starting in a place the scene whose cast they're of.

#### Authored from scratch

`tests/new-map.spec.ts` writes, through the controls alone, twice (the map, the town's way in and the scenario in a 1000px pane and the place and the scene in a 700px one, then the other way round, so each document is written at each width): **Chandler Row**, a map made from the scenario's Files view, cropped to 16 by 12 from its Map view, painted with a new legend entry (a timber floor under a thatch roof) and a cobbled street; a region picked with Select, **the chandler's shop**, playing **The Tallow Thief**, made where it's picked, and laying **The chandler's shop**, a place made from the region; a way back to the town and the town's way in; a candle-seller by day and the night-soil man at night; the place's two areas (the skeleton's floor renamed and resized, and the back room), three things (the tallow vat, the ledger, a broken shutter) and two starts (Maud, and her apprentice made in the scene's cast where his start is picked); the scene's fields, Maud's and the apprentice's cards, a discovery with two paths (the second's via picked from the place's things: the tallow vat), an ending and the route to it; and the scenario wired: the ending going on to the Drowned Rat on the diagram, the start moved onto the row from a tile and back from the Scenario view. Every Problems view finds nothing. Written to a scratch copy of the campaign folder (`/tmp/km-m4ux/caerwyn`, the five files it changed), `npm run campaign -- check` says "7 scenes (28 beats, 66 routes, 21 endings), a talk template, 5 places, 5 maps, 154 lore docs, 5 builds, 14 looks, the vocabulary of 19 scenes (44c6d4a); no problems", and the game's loaders take it (kingmaker-scratch/v2/m4/load-authored.ts): the pack, the row built as the game builds it (16 by 12, a region, an exit, two people), the town with its seventh exit, the shop's world (eight entities: the vat in the back room, the ledger and the shutter on the shop floor, Maud there and Jory in the back room), and the scene parsed (three nodes, two routes, its place, Maud and Jory). The two files of the campaign it changed changed only where it wrote: the scenario's three lists a new entry each, the town's exits one more.

What writing it found, each fixed:

- **The map's status line wrapped** under a hovered tile's long name ("Timber floor under a thatch roof") in a 1000px pane, so the canvas got shorter mid-stroke and the tiles under the pointer moved: the rectangle painted one column short. It's one line now, whatever it says.
- **A scene's place set from a map** was read from the campaign as the map editor last read it, which hadn't the scene just made from the same region yet: the place was made and listed, and the scene's file not written. The scene is read when it's written now, and the region's card says it's reading the scene until it's there.
- **The diagram drawn again** after another view lost its pan and zoom (the fit had been once a file), leaving cards under the inspector; it's kept now.
- **A place's start needed the scene's cast first**, so writing a new place meant leaving it for the scene and coming back: someone new of the cast is made where their start is picked.

`tests/coverage.spec.ts` walks the coverage table's rows the other journeys don't reach (the renames, the purse, the backstory's docs, a quest across scenes, the talk template, the layout forgotten, a region's optional parts, the start from the Map view, people by the hour, a thing's armour, hit points, quantity and holder, a bare spot of floor, the plan's names).

#### Judgement calls

- **The views.** The scenario's own fields split by what an author is doing: the Scenario view (the campaign, its start, the purse and the backstory's world), the Files view (what the scenario lists, with where each is named) and the Quests view (a list with uses, like the scene's Facts). A map's legend, regions, exits and people each a view, a list beside the chosen card as the Cast view has it, since the town has 52, 21, 6 and 30 of them; its place's own fields stay the place editor's (a region's card links to it and says its size), so the map has no Place view. A place's areas, things and people likewise.
- **One form, two places.** A region's, an exit's, someone's or a legend entry's form is the same component in the painter's inspector and in its view's card; the card hides the kind line the inspector's header already says.
- **The tile is a selection.** A click with Select (V) selects the tile: the inspector is what's on it and what can be added there, the right-click menu's choices where an author would look. The brush stays the first tool, since painting is what the painter is for. On a place's plan an area covers most of the floor, so a click there selects the area, whose inspector adds a thing or someone where it was clicked; a click on bare floor selects the spot.
- **Made where it's picked** means: the file made from its skeleton (as the Files view and Obsidian's commands make it), listed in the scenario, and the reference set, as one step of undo; undo takes back the listing and the reference and leaves the file, as M4's new files did. A new map an exit leads to is landed on in its middle (it has no way back yet).
- **Renames rewrite only what the file, or the editor's own step, owns.** A file's id is renamed in the file, not its name (Obsidian renames files; the problems say until they agree), as M3 did for a scene. A quest's key is renamed in every scene the scenario lists (and the talk template) in the same step; a scene file the scenario doesn't list isn't read for it. An area's or a thing's id takes what's in it along, but not the scenes' vias and effects that name it (another file each, and a via is words: the warnings and problems say). A region's id warns that the town's code (shared/map/places.ts) names a few of Caerwyn's.
- **Someone new of a scene's cast**, made from a place's start, is a card with its fields empty, as the Cast view's [+ Add person] makes one: the scene's problems name what it needs (the journey writes it there).
- **A keyboard crop asks once for an edge** while its handle keeps the focus, but again when the next would take a region, an exit or someone off the map; a drag still asks once a gesture.
- **The narrow pane.** Under 760px the inspector lies over the canvas while something's selected, as the scene editor's does: what's selected is moved into the strip beside it (on the diagram a double-click's span after the click, so a double-click still opens the file; on the painter at once; the place's plan fits the strip). Show on the map centres the item in that strip.
- **Not made where they're picked:** lore docs (the primer, the docs that aren't canon, a place doc: they're Obsidian's notes, written in its own editor), the scenes a region opens for or is the outside of (always an existing scene's), and an area for a thing or a start (added on the plan or in the Areas view). The scene editor's pickers of the scenario's quests and places are M3's and its host can't write the scenario: still pick-only.
- **Keys the format doesn't have** are shown for the scenario, a map, a place and a region (where the migration puts them), not for every object of every file: what an author could meet.
- **The tests' harness port** is `KINGMAKER_HARNESS_PORT`'s when it's set (4178 otherwise), since checkouts side by side each run their journeys.

#### Checked

From tools/authoring after a fresh `npm ci`: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:host` (80 of 80: five new, the scenario model's uses both ways and the quest rename, the map model's legend rename and people by the hour, the place model's renames and a place's uses, all on Caerwyn's files); `npm run build`; `npx playwright test --workers=1` on its own port (44 of 44: the 24 scene journeys and the new scene, the bundle check, 9 map journeys, 3 scenario journeys and the place journey, these on the new views; the new map, place and scene written from nothing at each pair of widths; the three coverage journeys; the same, then 43 with the authoring at one pair, after a fresh `npm ci` and on a `git archive` export of the branch with no root packages, as CI's job runs them). The first two commits' trees (the scenario editor's rework alone, then the map's and the place's) were checked on their own too: the type check, the host tests and the journeys. From the root: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1008 of 1008; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` no problems (the same line as before). The authored files: the campaign check and the game's loaders on a scratch copy, as above. Screenshots of every view and every kind of selection's inspector, at 1400px and in 1000px and 700px panes, are in kingmaker-scratch/v2/m4/after/ (85; the before in before-ux/).

#### Open

- **Obsidian itself** still hasn't been run; the harness and the bundle check stand in for it.
- **The scene editor's quest and place pickers** don't make a new quest or place where they're picked (its host can't write the scenario); the scenario's Quests view and Files view, and a region's card, do.
- **A file isn't renamed with its id**, here as in M3: the problems say until it is.
- **The map's knowledge of the story's scenes is still code** (M4's first open item): a scene put on a new map passes the check and the game's loaders (the Tallow Thief does), but who waits there, its lead marker and where the player comes out after aren't in the files.

Fourteen files conflicted:

- **`onStart` in the scenario's and the place's editors:** a quest renamed in the scenario follows into every scene's `onStart` too (`renameQuestIn`), the Quests view's Named by lists it ("The Hanged Clerk: as it starts", effect 1, start, first), and a place's thing counts the scenes' `onStart` effects that name it among its uses ("as it starts: effect 2"; the clerk's own names no thing). Before, renaming the clerk's quest would have left its `onStart` naming the old key, a problem in the clerk the rename didn't say it made.
- **The clerk's porters' path is Old Wat's,** someone of the cast, so the via warnings on the real scenes are the clerk's two ("the body", twice) where they were three ("the porters at the far gate" gone): the host test and the journeys (the scenes' warning counts, a problem's link, the via journey's warnings before and after picking the body) count them.

#### Judgement calls

- **sight.ts needed nothing:** what the player can see reads the scene's world (its areas, its things and their doors' states, from the place's `.kmplace` as this branch's world builder reads it) and the look layer's dressing (the walls, the glass and which areas are indoors, client/look/dressings.ts, by the scene's id), which this branch didn't move into the place's file. Its tests read the places through `loadPlace`, so they test this branch's files.
- **`onStart` with the scenario's start:** the game's first lead is built from the scenario's `firstScene` (map/start.ts, unchanged by this branch but for its comments), and the scenario's quests title the quest it starts (story/effects.ts reads `campaignFile`, the scenario's): M2's tests of the quest started on and off the map pass as they were.

## M6: the zoo and a combat ring

The user, on the combat to come: "If you get time can you also implement a combat test in the zoo? I'd like to iterate on that. The combat before kinda worked but I think it was a bit confusing. I probably want there to be animations for each move the NPCs make." So: a design note to react to first ([combat-design.md](combat-design.md)), then the fight as simulation state and moves, the other side's turns played one at a time with a moment for each thing they do (what a screen would animate), the zoo's rings to start one, and headless play. The screen that plays the moments came next ("The fight's screen", below); the story's fights keep the stand-in until the user has iterated.

### What changed

- **The design note** ([combat-design.md](combat-design.md)): the turn flow, what the screen shows, how each NPC move is animated, how a fight ends, the hand-back to the story, and what was confusing in the prototype's fight (from its code and screen) with what this one does about it.
- **The shared part** ([shared/fight/](../shared/fight/)): the prototype's tile geometry (`grid.ts`) and flavour (`flavour.ts`), copied whole from `bc2ab1f:src/game/combat`; the fight's types (`types.ts`: its board, its setup, its moments, the player's options, how it ended); the pace (`pace.ts`: how long each part of a moment plays, which the simulation spaces the other side's turns by); and the board as text (`board.ts`, after the prototype's `render.ts`).
- **The simulation** ([simulation/src/fight/](../simulation/src/fight/)): the prototype's `skirmish.ts`, `ai.ts`, `moves.ts` (as `named.ts`), `rules.ts`, `setup.ts` and `controller.ts`'s `summarise` and write-back ported at their seams onto v2's engine and the board; boardgame.io's game and the controller replaced by `turns.ts` (a turn passing, the AI's turn, auto) and the rows (`rows.ts`: `play`, a move on a fight as one call; `start.ts`: `startFight`, the one way in; `step.ts`: `fight_step`; `moves.ts`: the player's moves); `caption.ts` new. Four tables (`fight`, `fight_rules`, `fight_event`, `fight_clock`) and three reducers (`start_ring`, `fight_turn`, the scheduled `fight_step`); `not_your_turn`, a new refusal; the map's `walk`, `attack` and `cast` are fight moves while a fight is on, and its other moves say why not.
- **The zoo** ([simulation/src/zoo/](../simulation/src/zoo/)): the arena's rings, after the prototype's (`bc2ab1f:src/game/zoo/arenas.ts` and `wings/arena.ts`, its Heron grid for the grain floor), each on a map of its own, and `start_ring`.
- **The client and headless play:** the connection, the port link and the Replica carry a game's fight and its moments (`client/fight/view.ts`); `observe` says `MODE: fight` (`client/fight/text.ts`); `act` takes the fight's ids; `npm run play -- ring` starts one; the page host runs the fight's clock.
- **The e2e** has a step 40, a fight in the wolf run on a real SpacetimeDB.

### Judgement calls

- **The board is JSON on the fight row, beside typed columns for what every client reads** (round, whose turn, the order, the status, the outcome, the last moment's number), **and a moment's details are JSON too.** The user will iterate on the fight: a field added to the board or a moment changes no schema (SpacetimeDB can't migrate a typed object column's new field), and a saved game in a page restores. V12's plan had the prototype's `CombatG` as JSON on a `fight` row; this is that, less the engine's campaign, which goes in `fight_rules` (private: it's the engine's whole event log, and changes with every move).
- **The fight plays on a copy of the player's own campaign** (rules_state's, into `fight_rules`), the setup's people made in it from their statblocks, and the encounter closed in it at the end (the prototype's `writeBack`, its engine half). It's written back to rules_state and the sheet only when the setup keeps the player (`keep`: the story's fights, later); the zoo's rings rest the player first (`fresh`) and leave their sheet as it was: practice. Everyone the setup names is made afresh: the story's people the rules already know (rules_state's `npcs`) aren't reused yet, since the rings' are their own; the story's fights will want the prototype's "the same person, if the same build".
- **One roll of the game's dice per move** (a player's act, or one of the other side's turns): the engine reseeded from the dice row's seed and its next count, as a check's is (`shared/rules/engine.ts opened`), every die in the move drawn from it; a roll outside the engine's planners (a blow at a lantern) has its own stream beside it, as a rest's hit dice do. The prototype kept one engine's dice state in `G.rng` across moves; this replays the same way from the rows.
- **The other side's turns are a scheduled reducer, not a procedure:** `fight_step`, run by a `fight_clock` row, as `tick_place` is by `place_tick`. A turn needs no HTTP, and a reducer is one transaction. One turn a step (the player's own while they're down too: its death save is its start), its row deleted as it runs, the next scheduled once this step's moments have had their time on screen (`stepDelayMs`, at most 5 s), so a spectator sees the same pace as the player; `fast` schedules them at once, for QA. The schedule table is `fight_clock` because SpacetimeDB refuses a table and a reducer both named `fight_step` (their generated types clash).
- **The player's moves are the map's own reducers on their turn**, as the brief asked: `walk({ to })` moves them, `attack` and `cast` take the action menu's ids (`attack@x,y` with the first weapon that reaches, as the prototype's `attack_<target>` did; `shove@x,y` knocks a brazier or a lantern down, the prototype's `use_<prop>`), and one more reducer, `fight_turn({ act })`, has the fight's own acts (dash, dodge, disengage, end_turn, yield, leave, auto), a typed string as `rest`'s length is, rather than a reducer each. The client's existing menu and aiming can drive a fight unchanged once it draws one. The menu's other acts (a feature, a leap, hiding, a rest, an item, a throw, a pocket) are refused in a fight with why, as the prototype's typed lines refused them; the map's other moves say there's a fight on.
- **A moment for each act** (`Skirmish.moment`): a move, an attack, a spell, a turn's start, a dash, a shove, a remark, a yield, the end. Its hits are what changed in it (each unit's hit points, state and conditions before and after, with the damage, healing and saves the engine reported), so nothing has to parse a dice line to animate it, as the prototype's `fightEffects` did. An opportunity attack splits the move it interrupts into three moments (the move up to it, the swing, the rest of the move). Moments with nothing in them (a round's end where no fire spread) aren't kept.
- **The caption is written from the moment's details** (`caption.ts`: who did what to whom with what, the damage, a fall, the dust going up), in the house style's plainness, so it says exactly what the dice did; the prototype's flavour lines (Sallow's "Regrettably", the Crakes' walking off) stay as the moment's lines beside the dice, and a remark's caption is its line.
- **Everyone's hit points are shown**, the other side's too: in the order on `observe`, and in the dice lines ("A bandit takes 10 slashing damage (HP 1/11)", where the prototype said "(badly hurt)"). The design note says why and the user can turn it back.
- **The player's turn passes by itself only once nothing at all is left of it** (no action, no attack of Extra Attack's still to come, under 5 ft of movement, no bonus-action spell they could cast), with a moment that says so; the prototype passed it once the action was used and the movement gone, whatever bonus action was left.
- **The fight's end is on screen until out:** the fight row stays `over`, with how it went, and the map's moves wait (`FIGHT_OVER`); `walk out` makes it `done` and the fight's end is the map's last words, as a scene's end is. A game keeps its latest fight only: the next one's start takes the last one's rows away, and deleting a game deletes them.
- **A turn the rules code throws on is passed over**, the module's log saying why, and the fight goes on: a bug in the AI mustn't freeze a fight the user is iterating on.
- **The zoo is the arena's rings only, started from any game out of a scene still being played** (`start_ring`, the host's move): there's no zoo campaign or hub yet (V19's), and the zoo is for QA and demos, so a ring can be rung from any game. A ring is a layer over the map: the player stays where they were and walks out there again, its map is carried on the fight row (`fight.map`, a map file), and its lines say it's in the Arena. The rings: a practice ring with one bandit (new: the quickest fight, and the tests'), and the prototype's wolf run, goblin pit, porters' floor and grain floor (on the prototype's own Heron grid, with Sallow, the Crakes, the lantern-boy, Kell, its dust, lanterns, brazier and sacks).
- **The hazards came cheap** (they're in the Skirmish the port copies whole), so the grain floor has them: a Fire Bolt or the brazier into the dust blows it up, the sacks burn and the fire spreads each round.
- **Left out for now, each with what it's for:** a word with weight (Influence) and Search or Study checks (V13's typed lines), Reckless Attack, a punch beside your weapon, a higher slot asked for (the prototype's `_slot<n>`), class features, hiding, jumping and throwing in a fight, and the scenes' world under the fight (V18).

### Checked

From the worktree's root, after a fresh `npm ci`: `npm run typecheck` clean (both configs); `npm test` 1030 of 1030 (22 new: the fight's 12, the rings' 6, the fight's text 3, the headless fight against the page host 1); `npm run screens` PASS 685; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` with no problems (the campaign is unchanged); `npm run build:web` (26 files, 5.66 MB, 1.53 MB gzipped: the fight bundles into the page's server worker); the bindings regenerated (`start_ring`, `fight_turn`, the fight's tables). Every ring played to its end by the AI on both sides on the memory host, in a test.

`npm run e2e -- --port 3610 --ai-port 9110 --root runs/e2e-zoo` once, in namespace mode on ports of its own: PASS, every step, 363 checks in 396.8 s. Its new step 40, on a real SpacetimeDB: the wolf run set out (in initiative order wolf-2, wolf-3, the player, wolf-1), the two wolves before the player each a turn of their own (a move with its path, a bite with its roll: "A big grey wolf closes in on you, 30 ft.", "A lean grey wolf bites you: 5 damage.") played by `fight_step` on SpacetimeDB's schedule 1703 ms apart, the spectator seeing the same moments and refused its own act, the player's blow by its id, auto, the fight's end after 2 rounds (the wolves won: 17 moments, numbered in order), and out back to the map with the fight's end its words; `fight_step`'s turns took 3 to 20 ms each, as the module logged them.

### Open

- ~~**The fight's screen**~~: built, below ("The fight's screen").
- **The story's fights** on this one (combat-design.md §5): the Heron's and the town's brawls as setups from their places (the prototype's `battlefield` and `heronEncounter`), the aftermath picking up from the result, the story's people reused in the engine, the arc's checks 11 to 13 on (V12's). Until then the stand-in tells every story fight as won.
- **The zoo beyond the arena** (V19): its other wings, and a way into it from the title. (The zoo is a campaign now, with the title's way in and its combat ring: "The zoo", below.)
