### The fight's screen

The brief, from the user's words ("I probably want there to be animations for each move the NPCs make"; "the combat before kinda worked but I think it was a bit confusing"): the fight on screen in the terminal client and the page, on the map's renderer, with a turn-order bar, the player's turn as the map's action menu with its reach and targets shown and what each costs, every move of the other side animated in order from the moments (a step every 80 to 120 ms, a blow's flash, a shot or a spell travelling the line, a hit's flash with its number popping up and drifting, "miss", a fall changing the glyph, each move's caption on a caption line and in the log), paced with a beat between combatants, skippable, reduced motion respected, Caves of Qud-style procedural ASCII in the look layer, the same moments as headless lines, and fight screens in the screen check.

#### What changed

- **The fight's rows say what a screen needs** (`shared/fight`, `simulation/src/fight`): the pace laid out in parts (`pace.ts partsOf`: the start's order, a turn's beat between combatants (new, 250 ms) then its start, the path a tile at a time, the act, what it did, each fall, the caption held), which the screen's playback and the simulation's spacing both read; each of the player's options with its name, cost, aim and target, reach, an area's tiles and a move's feet; the options with the player's full movement and the tiles a walk to would draw a swing; each hit with how its target stood before it (`was`); `hurry`, a new `fight_turn` act, the screen's skip to the player's turn. The arena's rings moved to `shared/zoo/rings.ts`, unchanged.
- **The playback** (`client/fight/script.ts`, `client/fight/player.ts`): a moment as beats on the pace's parts; the moments scheduled one after another from when they come, what's on screen a pure function of the clock, folded into what's shown as each ends, the board's own again once nothing's left to play; finish, skip, reduced motion; a fight seen from its start wound back by the hits' `was`.
- **The look** (`client/look/fight.ts`; the effects' `slash`, a flight's time and arc; the renderer's `flash`): who's who on the board, falls tipping over (lit red, `/`, `_`, then `x`; `%` dead), blows by kind (a blade's sweep across the blow's way, a club's knock, a bite), shots by their way across the isometric screen, bolts in their element's colour, areas as a fireball or a shockwave, sparks and numbers, words off someone (miss, parried, saved, a condition), the hit-point gauge, tags, a hit's flicker, whose turn's breathing light, the board's tints.
- **The screen** (`client/ui/fight.tsx`, `client/fight/model.ts`, `client/fight/draw.ts`; the app's new "fight" screen): the turn-order bar, the board, the caption line, the log, the panel with whose turn (YOUR TURN, the action and bonus action, movement as a gauge), the action menu by what each line spends, aiming on the board, walking by a click or the arrows, the bottom line, the help, the whole log, the sheet and the menu with motion full or reduced. Reduced motion is the pace's new `reducedMotion`: `KM_REDUCED_MOTION` in a terminal, `prefers-reduced-motion` on a page.
- **Headless** (`client/fight/text.ts`): under each moment's caption a `▸` line of what the screen plays of it.
- **Checks:** the screen check's fight screens (`client/check/fight-screens.tsx`) from a recorded wolf run (`client/check/fixtures/fight-wolves.json`, recorded by `hosts/fight-fixture.ts` from the game on the memory host); the playback's, the script's, the look's and the model's tests; the screen against the page host (`web/server/fight-screen.test.ts`); the e2e's step 40, the screen's script for the wolves' moves and `hurry`.

#### Judgement calls

- **The playback is the client's, from the moments as they come, and a pure function of the clock.** Each moment gets a slot (start, end) from when it arrived and the one before it ended; what's on screen at any instant is the moments ended folded in plus the one playing as far as it's got, so a frame mid-step is exact however it was reached (the screen check draws an instant), and finishing or skipping only moves slots. Once nothing is left to play, what's shown is the board's again: the rows are the truth, and anything a moment didn't carry (a condition the engine dropped between moments) comes right there.
- **One pace table, laid out in shared** (`partsOf`), so the screen and the simulation's spacing can't drift: the simulation waits exactly as long as the screen plays. The beat between combatants is a part of a turn's moment, so the simulation's spacing grew a little with it (a turn's start 650 ms where it was 400; a miss and a shove now have their 450 ms for what they did).
- **"The options from the map's action menu" is the same menu fed from the fight's options.** The map's own menu (`shared/map/menu.ts`) aims on the town's world (its catalogue, its lines of sight, the walker's tile), and a ring is a map of its own; the M6 note said the client's menu could drive a fight unchanged, but it can't on a ring. So the fight's options, which the simulation already computes as the rules see them, are the menu's lines in the same component (`client/ui/actions.tsx`), one line a name with its targets aimed on the board, and they now carry what the menu and the aiming need. The ids are still the map's.
- **One way to act, the menu; walking direct.** A click on a blue tile walks; a click on someone opens the menu aimed at them (what can be done to them) rather than attacking at once, so the reach and targets are seen before it's done (the note's §6.3, the prototype's click that aimed first and attacked second).
- **The swing tiles are the simulation's** (`options.swings`): the path a walk takes there (the same search the move runs), leaving the reach of someone standing with their reaction ready and a melee weapon; none once disengaged. An approximation of the engine's own opportunity rules, but from the same paths.
- **Skip to your turn asks the simulation** (`hurry`): the other side's turns are paced by the simulation (each a step on its clock), so skipping only the screen's playback would still wait. Finishing a move is the client's alone. On the player's own turn `hurry` says there's nothing to hurry.
- **Space finishes, F skips.** Not Enter (it opens the menu on the player's turn, and three Enters would have skipped, opened the menu and done its first line), not `s` (WASD steps).
- **Reduced motion** shows each moment's end at once with its caption for the caption's least time (500 ms), no effects; the moments still come one after another so each caption can be read.
- **A fight seen starting plays from its first moment; one already on as the client comes up starts where it is,** its moments so far in the log, nothing replayed.
- **The player's own moments are played too** (their walk, their swing, its hit). The menu waits for the other side's moves to play (YOUR TURN appears when the bar reaches them); acting while the player's own moment still plays finishes it first.
- **The camera** holds the whole ring when it fits, else sits between the action and the player, moving only when either nears the edge, eased a tile at a time.
- **Props and hazards are drawn as the rows have them,** not played: a lantern brought down is gone when its moment's rows come, not at its blow's beat.
- **The fixture is recorded from the game** (`hosts/fight-fixture.ts`, the memory host), not made by hand, so the screen check reads real options, captions and dice; record it again when a fight's rows change shape.
- **No way into a ring from the screens here:** the zoo (the work beside this in the same branch: its campaign, its menu on the title) is the way in; until then `npm run play -- ring --session <you> --ring wolves`, then `npm run client -- --session <you>`.

#### Checked

On a clean copy of the branch (`git archive` of its tip, the worktree's `node_modules`), since the zoo's work was going on beside this one in the same worktree: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm test` 1086 of 1087 at `593ea80` (the new: the pace's 4, the fight's options, a hit's `was` and `hurry` 4, the script's 6, the playback's 8, the look's 6, the model's 6, the board's tints 3, the headless lines' 1, the screen against the page host 1; the one failing is the zoo's own `web/server/zoo.test.ts`, which expects the brindle mastiff unhurt at the player's first turn, and Kell's blow on it is in the dice: it passed on the clean copy at `71ca42f`); `npm run screens` PASS 767, the fight's 62 among them; `npm run campaign -- check campaigns/caerwyn` no problems; `npm run build:web` 26 files, 5.74 MB (1.55 MB gzipped). `npm run e2e -- --port 3650 --ai-port 9150 --root runs/e2e-screen` once, on the clean copy at `71ca42f`: PASS, every step, 363 checks in 393.4 s. Its step 40: the wolf run's first wolf walked six tiles and bit (a critical, 9 damage), the screen's script for each as long as the simulation waited (660 ms, 800 ms); after the player's blow and their turn's end, `hurry` brought the end 16 ms later (the wolves knocked the player out in round 1); `fight_step`'s turn took 25 ms.

#### Open

- **Wolves put their hands up:** the prototype's AI lets a beast surrender, and its flavour has it "drop the wolf bite and put both hands up" (seen in the recorded wolf run).
- **The line to an aimed target**, red when something's in the way (combat-design.md §2), isn't drawn; the reach ring, the targets and the bracket are.
- **The menu's spell lines don't say the slots left** (the options don't carry them), and a higher slot can't be asked for (M6's open item).
- **Props and hazards aren't played** (above), and the grain dust going up plays as the area's blast only.
- **Kingmaker Glyphs:** a fall tips the letter over to an x, sprites or not; a sprite of someone down would read better.
- **Headless `act --id hurry`** is answered at once on the player's own turn now; from the other side's turn it waits for the player's turn as any act does.



### The zoo

The brief: the zoo as exhibits you open from the title (z), the command line (`npm run zoo -- <exhibit>`, `npm run play -- new --zoo <exhibit>` headless) and the page (`?zoo`, `?zoo=<exhibit>`), each a small setup (a map or place, people and a starting situation) made data in the campaign format where it can be, so the user can author more in Obsidian; and its first exhibit, the combat ring: a small arena (stone walls, a sand floor, a couple of pillars for cover, a brazier), the player as a ready-made fighter, wizard or rogue with Kell beside them, Sallow and the two Crakes with their statblocks and the cast's notes for how they fight, initiative as you enter, again in one key, and a small menu to pick the foes (the Crakes alone, Sallow and the Crakes, a pack of dogs). The user's note on zoos: "Can you build zoos that help demonstrates every feature in the game e.g. all the world effects, and interactions, scenes, combat etc." The fight's screen was built beside this, in the same worktree (above); the zoo's keys are on it.

#### What changed

- **The zoo is a campaign** ([campaigns/zoo/](../campaigns/zoo/README.md)), an Obsidian vault as Caerwyn is: its scenario lists the exhibits (`exhibits`); the combat ring is its map (`maps/ring.kmmap`: raked sand, a low stone wall, two stone pillars, one gate), its place laid over the whole map (`places/ring.kmplace`: where everyone starts, the brazier, the pillars) and its scene (`scenes/ring.kmscene`: Kell, Sallow, the talking and the silent Crake and three mastiffs, each with their statblock, weapon and notes on how they fight); the ready-made fighter, wizard and rogue; the cast's looks; a primer; and its README, with how to open it, how to make an exhibit and the exhibits to come.
- **An exhibit's types and rules** ([shared/zoo/exhibits.ts](../shared/zoo/exhibits.ts)): an exhibit names its scene, and a fight exhibit who fights beside the player and its line-ups of foes, each fighter by cast id with how the fight's AI plays them; `exhibitLayout` reads a line-up off the files (everyone where the place starts them on the map whose region lays it, the lit things as what a fight can knock over); the campaign check and the module's install hold the exhibits to their files. The scenario's `gm` may be `none`.
- **The simulation** ([simulation/src/zoo/exhibits.ts](../simulation/src/zoo/exhibits.ts)): `visit_zoo` (a visit as one of the zoo's builds, the caller's last visit gone, the host on the exhibit's map with no lead, and the exhibit opened) and `open_exhibit` (the exhibit opened again in the visit: its fight afresh, the same foes and pace unless asked otherwise); a fight carries the exhibit and line-up it came from (`zoo`) and its time of day (`time`); `my_games` says each game's campaign; a visit can be watched; two refusals, `no_exhibit` and `no_line_up`.
- **The installs:** the dev stack installs the zoo in the module beside Caerwyn at every start, the page's content carries it and the page host installs it, `npm run install-campaign` takes it; none of them gives it to the AI service.
- **The client** ([client/zoo/](../client/zoo/)): the title's line and `z`, its menu (the exhibits, then who to be, [client/ui/zoo.tsx](../client/ui/zoo.tsx)), a visit kept off the games to carry on; `npm run zoo [-- <exhibit>]` and the client's `--zoo`; the page's `?zoo` and `?zoo=<exhibit>` (`&build=`, `&foes=`), on the fake GM; `play new --zoo <exhibit>` headless, with `again` and `foes_<id>` in a zoo fight's ACTIONS; and on the fight's screen, for a zoo fight, `n` again (Enter once it's over), `o` the line-ups to pick from, and the side panel and keys saying so.
- **The map's palette** has a sand ground (the look layer had the cell already).
- **Checks:** the exhibits' tests (the ring's line-ups as its files have them, each rule broken on a copy, the shape), the zoo's moves' (a visit, again, other foes, the refusals, a second visit, each line-up played to its end by the AI), the client's (the zoo from its row, a visit kept apart, a zoo fight's line-ups and actions, the title's menu), the zoo in a page with no server (`web/server/zoo.test.ts`: play new --zoo ring, again, foes_dogs), the screen check's zoo (the title's menu, and a zoo fight's keys and list at 80x24 and 130x42), and the browser check's step (`?zoo=ring`: the fight, a turn, again, the dogs).

#### Judgement calls

- **The zoo is a campaign in the same format, not code:** a map, a place and a scene the plugin already edits, and one entry in the scenario. The arena's rings stay code ([shared/zoo/rings.ts](../shared/zoo/rings.ts)) for headless QA, the tests and the e2e's step 40; each can move into the zoo as an exhibit (its README lists them).
- **An exhibit is a scene's cast on its place, laid on a map, with the fight's own data in the scenario's entry.** The cast's statblocks, weapons and notes on how they fight are the cast cards' own fields (`statblock`, `weapon`, `attitude`, `limits`); where they stand is the place's `people`; the map is the one whose region lays the place. What only a fight needs (who's an ally, the line-ups, how the AI plays each) is the exhibit's, so nothing new goes on a cast card that the scene editor doesn't know. The plugin keeps the scenario's `exhibits` as it is, and has no editor for it yet: it's JSON by hand.
- **The ring's scene is a real, small scene** (a start, a "Roll for initiative" ending and a walk-out), which the zoo never opens: it's where the cast live, and a GM could play a talk before the fight from it later.
- **No GM plays the zoo, so it goes to the simulation alone** (the scenario's `gm: "none"`): the AI service serves one campaign version per process and refuses another (409 other_campaign), and nothing in a visit asks it anything. An exhibit the GM plays (a scene) needs the AI service to serve both first.
- **A visit is a game of the zoo's, one a player at a time:** visiting deletes the last visit (a visit is for seeing an exhibit through, not for keeping), and the title keeps visits off its games (`my_games` says each game's campaign). The host is put on the exhibit's map with no lead and no scene waiting (`putOnMap`, split out of the story's start), and the game can be watched like any other.
- **"Entering the ring rolls initiative" is opening the exhibit:** from the title's menu, `npm run zoo -- ring`, `?zoo=ring` or `play new --zoo ring`, the fight starts at once. Stepping into the ring on its map doesn't (a map's region opens a scene, and the zoo as a place to walk between exhibits is still to come).
- **Again is `n`, other foes `o`:** on the fight's screen `r` is the log and `f` skips to the player's turn (the screen's own keys, built beside this), so the zoo's are n and o; once it's over Enter fights again too, where a story's fight goes back to the map (the ring's map has nothing on it once the fight's done). Again keeps the line-up and the pace; a new fight is a new fight to the client, played from its start. Headless they're `again` and `foes_<id>`.
- **Who you are is picked as you open the exhibit,** the fighter unless said: another build is another visit, so it's fresh and rested; there's no changing build in a fight.
- **The default line-up is Sallow and the Crakes** (the first in the list, and the brief's headline), then the Crakes alone and the dogs, as the user listed them. Without Sallow there's nobody paying the Crakes to stop, so they fight on (the AI's own rule). The dogs are the SRD's mastiffs (5 hit points, a bite that drags you down; no Pack Tactics, which the SRD's mastiff hasn't got).
- **The page's `?zoo` plays on the fake GM** unless `?fake` says otherwise: the zoo needs no GM, so asking for a key would only be in the way.
- **The ring is lit by its scene's time of day** (a fight's new `time`), so its sand is drawn in the afternoon: the fight's board drew any fight with a lit brazier at night, and the ring has one.
- **The page host's fight test from the fight's first part was flaky,** its dice its own: when the bandit won initiative there was no closing in to list. It takes either opening now, and stops the host's clocks however it ends, so a failure doesn't hold `npm test` for ten minutes.

The bindings are regenerated.

The dev stack on the fake, in namespace mode on ports of its own (`npm run dev -- --fake --fresh --port 3740 --ai-port 9240 --root runs/dev-zoo --no-keys`): its screen said "caerwyn 0.1.0 installed in both; zoo 0.1.0 installed in the module (no GM plays it)"; `npm run play -- new --session zoo-dev --zoo ring --build wizard --stack runs/dev-zoo` came back at the wizard's first turn in 2.4 s (Kell first, her club at the talking Crake), a Fire Bolt by its id (4 fire), `end_turn` with the other side's three turns played on SpacetimeDB's schedule in 5.4 s (the talking Crake's critical on Kell, Sallow's lunge putting her down, the silent Crake's cudgel on the wizard), `again` (everyone whole, round 1), and `foes_dogs`.

`npm run e2e -- --port 3760 --ai-port 9260 --root runs/e2e-zoo2` once, at the end, in namespace mode on ports of its own: PASS, every step, 364 checks in 389.6 s; its first step now installs the zoo in the module beside the campaign on a real SpacetimeDB, its words never sent to the AI service.

#### Open

- **The arena's rings as exhibits** (the wolf run, the goblin pit, the porters' floor, the grain floor), each a map, a place, a scene and a line-up in the zoo.
- **The other kinds of exhibit** (the user's note: world effects, interactions, scenes; and looks, the rules, the map), the zoo as a place to walk between them, and a tour that opens every exhibit and checks it, in CI (V19's).
- **An exhibit the GM plays** needs the AI service to serve the zoo beside the story.
- **The plugin has no editor for `exhibits`**, and a cast card can't say how the AI plays someone (the exhibit does).
- **Out of a zoo fight headless** (`out`) is the ring's map with nobody on it: the ring's place isn't laid in play, since the map's knowledge of the scenes it plays is still code (M4's open item).

### First play

The brief, after the fight, its screen and the zoo's ring were built: play the ring as the user will ("The combat before kinda worked but I think it was a bit confusing. I probably want there to be animations for each move the NPCs make."), three fights to the end, headless and in the page, asking each time whether it's always clear whose turn it is and what just happened, whether each of the other side's moves can be seen as it happens, whether the captions match the dice, and whether anything the prototype's fight got wrong (combat-design.md §6) came back; record the key moments; fix what's unclear or broken; and say in the design note what works and what to iterate on next ([combat-design.md](combat-design.md) §8).

#### Played

- **Headless** (`npm run play` on a dev stack of its own on the fake GM, ports 3870 and 9370): the fighter against the Crakes (they gave up in round 7, Kell down in round 2); the wizard against Sallow and the Crakes (lost in round 4, Kell down before the wizard's first turn; after the fixes, again, `auto` to a win in round 5: Sallow ran, the Crakes gave up); the rogue against the Crakes (out through the gate in round 1; again, a win in round 1: Sneak Attack left the talking Crake on 1 hit point and he gave up, the silent Crake's nerve broke and he ran, taking a critical as he went).
- **In the page** (`npm run build:site`, `web:serve` on 7871, Playwright driving headless Chromium at 130x42, its own driver in the scratch folder): the fighter against the Crakes (it stood still on the other side's first turn, twice; after the fix, they gave up in round 6), the wizard against Sallow and the Crakes (Sleep put the talking Crake to sleep and Kell's critical woke him; lost in round 3), the rogue against the Crakes (fled in round 1; again, the talking Crake down and the silent Crake gave up in round 3), and the fighter against Sallow and the Crakes for the record (they gave up in round 5, Sallow arrested).
- **Recorded** in `kingmaker-scratch/v2/m6/final`: the player's turn aiming (01), an attack landing (02) and swinging (02b) mid-animation, a fall lit red (03) and on the floor (03b), the end (04), and the other side's turns as a video (05-npc-turns.webm and .gif, 7.8 s: Kell's turn, then the talking Crake closing in and its critical); Playwright's video works headless here. The frames before the fixes are beside them (`f1*`, `f2*`, `f3*`).

#### What changed

- **The page's fight clock** (`web/server/jobs.ts`): a fight_clock row's timer waited the time left rounded down, the memory host runs a row only once it's due by its own clock, and a browser's timers and coarsened `performance.now()` disagree by up to a millisecond, so the timer woke a fraction early, nothing ran and nothing armed it again: the ring stood on the other side's first turn until F. Now the wait is rounded up, an early wake waits again for what's left, and a deleted row takes its timer. The browser check's zoo step now waits for round 2 without F (it pressed F, which is why it never saw this).
- **The words** (`simulation/src/fight`, `shared/fight`, `client/fight/text.ts`, `script.ts`): an area spell's caption names whom it caught with what it did ("catching a bandit (asleep)"; Sleep had said "catching nobody"), or "to no effect"; a sleeper's turn, the order and how it went say asleep, the dice lines "falls asleep" and "wakes up", and a caption the blow that wakes them; a feature's extra damage is named by its trigger ("Sneak Attack: a bandit takes 7 piercing damage (HP 4/11)"), and each damage or healing line counts from how the target stood before it (every line had shown the end's hit points); a Constitution save after a caster is hurt is "to keep concentrating", and a broken one ends the spell aloud; advantage and disadvantage say why where the fight can tell, in the dice line and the attack's option; a walk says it draws a swing only when its tile is one the board marks (Back off still warned after a disengage); how it went has a second telling for the player (`told`: "You were knocked out"), which the screen, headless play and the map's words after out use; the headless order says whose side each is on, its key no longer claims small letters are your side, the head line names the place once, the `▸` lines' verbs agree with you, and a line the caption already says isn't repeated under it.
- **The screen** (`client/look`, `client/fight`, `client/ui/fight.tsx`, `shared/fight/pace.ts`): the fallen use the look layer's own "down" (a sprite lies down, a dog curls up, a letter dims, the renderer's mark at their feet), the cast too (Kell was drawn standing, blue, at 0 hit points), lit red as they fall, never asleep on their feet for the beat before it; Sleep's sleepers lie down with a Z, tagged "asleep"; one of a kind by number (crake-2) keeps its campaign's letter and colour, so the Crakes are C and c, ochre and slate; the panel on the other side's turn shows theirs (glyph, hit points, foe or ally) above yours, labelled You; the bar's portraits sit on a tile of their side; the caption's mark is whoever its caption is about; the swing tint is red, and the panel says blue is safe and red a swing where it has room; an aim starts on whoever was aimed at last; an area aimed says whom it would catch, in the warning colour when that's you or your side; a caption stays up at least 900 ms.
- **Three flavour lines** fit any hour (the ring is fought in the afternoon, and Sallow's surrender said "night business").

#### Judgement calls

- **The fallen are drawn by the look layer's own "down", not always an x:** it's how the town and the scenes draw someone out cold, and it keeps who it is (a lying sprite in Kingmaker Glyphs, their letter dimmed with a mark at their feet in a terminal); the fight's own letters (the arena's wolves and bandits) still tip over to an x. The headless board keeps its x.
- **A numbered one of a kind takes its campaign's letter and colour** (`client/look/map.ts`, `appearance.ts`): the cast table has one look for every Crake, and two identical Crakes on the board made "the silent Crake swings at you" a guess. Only a ref the cast table knows by its kind alone ("crake-2") does this; the town's Crakes, whose looks are the table's, look as they did. The look layer's cache now keys on the cast it infers from an id, so a ref'd and a plain actor of the same name aren't one look.
- **900 ms a caption:** at 500 the caption line changed before it could be read while the eye was on the board; a round of four others' turns now takes 10 to 12 s where it took about 8, and the simulation's spacing follows (one table). F skips; the number is the user's to tune.
- **Reasons for the odds are the fight's, not the engine's:** the engine says only that a roll had advantage or disadvantage, so the fight says why where it can tell (a pack's friend beside the target, the target on the floor, out cold or dodging; long range, someone beside the shooter, from the floor), and says nothing where it can't, rather than guess.
- **`told` beside `summary`:** the story's version stays third person for the GM's aftermath (combat-design.md §5); the player's is written beside it, so neither is a rewrite of the other.
- **An area's warning is the client's count of who stands on its tiles,** as the simulation will take them (everyone on them, up or down; the caster's own tile is never in an area that goes out from them); it doesn't know who Sleep's dice would reach.
- **Red, not rust,** for a walk that draws a swing: on the ring's sand the rust tint read as sand.

#### Checked

From the worktree's root: `npm run check` (the type check clean; `npm test` 1097 of 1097, 10 new: the page host's clock on a slow clock 1, the dice lines 4, the walk's words and Sleep's caption, turn and order 2, the board as the look layer draws it 1, the lines under a caption 1, an area's catch 1; and the pace, the playback, the script and the model's tests moved to the 900 ms caption; the screen check PASS 770, 3 new: the other side's turn's panel at both sizes, and the wolves down at the end drawn on the floor); `npm run campaign -- check` on the zoo and Caerwyn, no problems; `npm run build:web` then `npm run web:check` PASS, 94 checks, its zoo step waiting for round 2 with nothing pressed. The wolf run's fixture is recorded again (only its advantage reasons and the walks' words changed).

`npm run e2e -- --port 3880 --ai-port 9380 --root runs/e2e-m6play` once, at the end, in namespace mode on ports of its own: PASS, every step, 370 checks in 398.0 s; its step 40's wolves' turns came 2104 ms apart on SpacetimeDB's schedule (1703 ms before, at 500 ms a caption), `hurry` brought the player's turn 10 ms later, and the map's words after out are the player's telling ("You were knocked out").

### On M2's QA fixes, the salvage and the cleanup (the restack)

`claude/v2-scenario-next2` (M4 with M3 and M2 at `08113e1` merged in: QA rounds qa1-1018 and qa2-1018 and their fixes, memory as the GM's notes, talks on the two-part GM, the old scene steps deleted with their gm_state columns and the `co_gm_result` table) is merged into M6 (`claude/v2-zoo` at `442fa2f`) as `claude/v2-zoo-next`. Six files conflicted:
- **shared/scene/files.ts, the campaign's `gm`:** M2's cleanup took the switch (`"two-part" | "pipeline"`) with the old scene steps, and this branch had added `"none"` for the zoo, which no GM plays (its install gives the AI service none of its words). `gm` stays for that, as `"two-part" | "none"`: "two-part" says outright what saying nothing means, and "pipeline" is gone; the scenario's parse (scenario.ts `GMS`) and scenario-format.md's `gm` say the same.
- **shared/protocol/port.test.ts:** the private tables a client never sees are M2's, without `co_gm_result`, and this branch's `fight_rules` and `fight_clock`.
- **hosts/dev/campaign.ts:** the imports, M3's shared `talkTemplateProblems` beside this branch's exhibit rules (no `TOLD` any more).
- **ARCHITECTURE.md and README.md:** both sides changed neighbouring lines, so each line is merged word by word (a three-way merge a word a line): this branch's client, simulation, shared and checks with the fight and the zoo in them, M2's AI service, turn, talk, notes and e2e words.
- **This log:** M4's note on its second restack ends M4's section, before M6's, and this note ends M6's.

**The schema and the bindings:** the textual merge of simulation/src/schema.ts, tables.ts and games/delete.ts kept every fight table (`fight`, `fight_rules`, `fight_event`, `fight_clock`) and reducer (`start_ring`, `fight_turn`, `fight_step`, `visit_zoo`, `open_exhibit`) with M2's drops (the `co_gm_result` table and its delete, gm_state's `guard`, `endingLead`, `sceneStatus`, `repeated`, `rulings`, `pendingExit`, `canon`, `notes`, `notesTurn`, `whispers`, `lastRepairTurn`, `offGraphTurn`, `oocTurns`, `opened` and `withheld`). `npm run simulation:generate` on the merged module wrote the bindings the merge already had, byte for byte (types.ts without M2's dropped types and columns, this branch's fight and zoo bindings beside them), and its private tables are `admin, creation, dice, fight_clock, fight_rules, gm_note, gm_state, heard, job, job_dispatch, look_invention, place_def, place_tick, reaper, rules_state, scene_def, seat, talk_record`. A dev database from before is wiped on publishing, as with M2's schema changes.

**Where the fight meets what M2 kept for combat:** the fight plays on the rules engine as M2 left it (shared/rules/engine.ts, the vendored engine through simulation/src/rules/session.ts) and writes its damage lines with shared/rules/acts.ts's `damageLine`, which M2 kept live for the map's act; in a fight the map's menu acts are the fight's moves (map/acts.ts hands them over before any rules' act), and out of one they go through `takeAct` as M2 left it. What M2 parked on the AI side (a line's mechanic read by `mechanicOf`, the reactions, the threat, fight.md) the fight doesn't call yet: it's for typed lines in a fight (V13) and the story's fights (combat-design.md §5), which still end on the stand-in. They meet as before, and the checks below run both: the fight's and the zoo's tests, the world bridge's (the parked reactions on an act the rules resolved), the e2e's step 40 (the wolf run on SpacetimeDB) and the arc's fight stand-in.

#### Judgement calls

- **`gm` stays, as `"two-part" | "none"`,** where M2's cleanup took the field with its switch: the zoo needs `"none"` (no GM plays it, so its words never go to the AI service, which serves one campaign at a time), and `"two-part"` is what saying nothing means and what the docs named. `"pipeline"` goes, so a scenario that still says it is refused by the parse rather than played by the two-part GM unasked.
- **The docs merged a word at a time:** where both sides had rewritten neighbouring lines of ARCHITECTURE.md and README.md, each line is a three-way merge of its words (git merge-file on a word a line), and every one merged clean, rather than one side's line taken whole and the other's edits redone by hand.
- **The bindings regenerated, not trusted to the merge:** `simulation:generate` was run on the merged module and its output compared with what the textual merge gave; they were the same, so the bindings committed are both.

### On M1's fixed dice (the merge)

`claude/v2-scenario-dice` (M1's "Dice in tests", by way of M2's, M3's and M4's merges of it) is merged in. Three files met it, each kept whole on both sides: the e2e's step 1 installs the zoo beside the campaign and then fixes the dice, with the AI service's `set_dice_seed` refused; the wire's comment names a fight's moves and `setDiceSeed` among those that aren't a player's; and this log, where M4's part ends and M6's begins. The schema's list has the fight's tables after the rest and `dice_seed` beside the dice, and the bindings regenerate as M6's with the reducer and `DiceSeed`'s type beside them. **Checked:** the type check clean, `npm test` 1104 of 1104. What M6's own tests roll is pinned after the merge, below.

### Dice in tests (2026-10-18)

M1's fixed dice (its "Dice in tests") reach M6's own tests here. A fight's dice are its game's, each move the dice's next roll (fight/rows.ts `nextRoll`), so a fixed seed fixes the whole fight; the page host's tests rolled the page's own until now, and #673's red run had its headless fight find the fighter hurt before their first turn ("28/28 HP" not there).

- **The page host's tests** each give the page host a seed (`HostConfig.seed`) and check what it rolls where they took either outcome. [web/server/fight.test.ts](../web/server/fight.test.ts) (seed 2): the fighter wins initiative (17 to 2), so the first observe has them at 28/28 and the bandit across the ring; the close-in it lists, the blow it offers then (a hit, with its dice), the bandit's turn after end_turn, and auto on to a victory. Its ifs (a close-in if listed, a blow if beside him, end_turn if the fight was on) and its either-way patterns (close or step, hit or miss, any of five endings) are gone. [fight-screen.test.ts](../web/server/fight-screen.test.ts) (seed 1): the menu's attack (a shot across the ring) with no close-in to fall back on, and a victory in round 4. [zoo.test.ts](../web/server/zoo.test.ts) (seed 1): the player's turn first, Sallow whole when it's fought again, the dogs whole.
- **The fight's own tests** ([simulation/src/fight/fight.test.ts](../simulation/src/fight/fight.test.ts), on the memory host's seeds) take the branch their seed takes, checked: a blow that hits (seed 7) and one that misses, a second case on seed 3 where the test took whichever came; the opportunity attack missing and the move going on; the bandit still beside them; the other side's turn a move and a blow, the other bandit's next; Burning Hands saved against; the fight's end a victory to the first blow, in round 1 (its loop of up to six tries gone); the zoo's rings on seed 1, where the wolves go first, so the act out of turn is always refused (seed 7 had the player first, and skipped it); the death save on the floor in round 1; the kept fight's 10 hit points; the hit that says how its target stood at the first blow (its four tries gone); hurry back to the player with both bandits still fighting; and Sleep on seed 7 at once, where it searched ten seeds for the bandits apart.
- **The e2e's fight (step 40)** is a game of its own: a ready-made fighter made just before the ring, its dice fixed (48), with B joining it to watch. The wolves win initiative twice over, so their pacing is always checked (it said "none to pace yet" when the player went first); the player's turn comes, their blow lands (a critical hit, a wolf down) and their turn goes on, so end_turn and hurry always run, and hurry brings it back to them with the fight on; auto plays it to a victory. It was the town's wizard's game, after whatever the town's steps had rolled.
- **The page:** `?seed=` fixes the page host's dice (page.js, the transport, the server worker's start, `startHost`), as the browser check's zoo ring does (`?zoo=ring&seed=1`), and the check's probe sees the server worker told it. It's one player's own game in their own browser, so the page honours a seed it's given; without one it draws its own from crypto, as before. README says so.

#### Judgement calls

- **Each page host test has a seed of its own,** picked for what it plays by playing its moves on seeds 1 to 8: the fight test's takes every branch its ifs allowed (close in, the blow, the bandit's turn, auto), the screen test's plays four rounds of auto, and the zoo's has everyone whole as the player's turn comes.
- **A hit and a miss are two seeds** in the attack test, so both captions stay checked.
- **The e2e's fight got a game of its own** rather than the town's, so the town's steps (two talks, a scene, a Fire Bolt) can change without moving it; its build is the fighter, as M6's own tests play the rings (the town's wizard lost to the wolves on most seeds). Seed 48 was picked from 40 to 79 on the memory host for the wolves going first, the blow landing and auto having rounds to play; SpacetimeDB played it move for move as the memory host had.
- **The browser check's page gets its seed from the address,** since its page host is the browser's own; the page takes it as a whole number or not at all.
- **Left as they were:** the AI's choice in its test (move or dash: it's the AI's, not the dice's), and the rings' and the exhibits' fights played to their end, which check that they end, not how.

#### Checked

The type check clean; `npm test`, 1105 of 1105 (the attack's miss the one new); web/server/fight.test.ts ten times, ten passes (317 to 351 ms each); fight-screen.test.ts, zoo.test.ts and contract.test.ts five times each, all passing; `npm run build:web` then `npm run web:check`, PASS (94 checks, the zoo ring's server worker told seed 1); `npm run e2e` in namespace mode on ports 3361 and 8861, PASS (425.7 s): step 40's wolves in the order wolf-1, wolf-3, the player, wolf-2, the blow "a critical hit, 13 damage! A grey wolf goes down.", and a victory after four rounds, three of them auto, as the memory host had it; step 24's natural 1 and the town's Fire Bolt hitting at its first cast, as on M1 and M2.

## M5: problem reports

The user, after the scenes and the editors: "add in the smaller missing features like bug reporting from the prototype". The prototype let a player report a problem from anywhere in the game (b on the map and in fights, `/report` in a scene, the Esc menu's Report a problem…, the title's item), and made one file with everything needed to look into it, which "report <file>" hands to Claude (`npm run report -- inspect` and `restore`). M1 cut it (client/ui/title.tsx: "Report a problem (b) is cut (V16)"). This is V16's half of it, as [verticals.md](verticals.md) has it: "a game's rows, its documents and its call log (`GET /v1/games/{game}/calls`), restorable into a local stack, and `b` or `/report` in the client", without the playtesters' allowlist and caps. The prototype's report is in history at `44c6d4a:src/report/` (dump.ts, redact.ts, recent.ts, inspect.ts, restore.ts, report.test.ts) and its wiring in `44c6d4a:src/app/report.ts`.

### What changed

- **The AI service gives a game's own** (shared/protocol/ai.ts `gamePath`, ai/service/server.ts): `GET /v1/games/{game}/calls` (its model calls and memory reviews, the latest 300 of each with how many in all), `GET /v1/games/{game}/docs` (its copy of the lore, every doc it wrote, the GM's notes among them) and `PUT /v1/games/{game}/docs` (a game restored from a report given its docs), behind the bearer, each answer with every key the service holds taken out (`redact`: hosts/ai.ts takes out the OpenRouter key, the module's bearer and its own token; the page host the player's key). The stores read their logs back (`CallLog.read`, `ReviewLog.read`): the folder's from its files, the memory stores' from their host (`Persistence.lines`), the page's from IndexedDB's `logs` store, which was "written, never read back here" (`Database.logs`, by a key range, everything handed in written first).
- **shared/report/redact.ts:** the prototype's redact.ts, its keys handed in (`keysFrom`) where it read them from the environment and the dev key file.
- **The game's rows for a report** (simulation/src/games/tables.ts, report.ts): every table's rows of a game, found as delete.ts finds them (by the game, its players, scenes, places, jobs and fights), each table with what its ids name for a restore; the tables that aren't a game's (the owner, the reaper, the campaigns' four, the looks invented by name) listed with why, and tables.test.ts holds every table to one list or the other. `report_game` (index.ts), a procedure only the game's owner (its host) may call, reads them in one transaction and then GETs the AI service's calls and docs for the game with nothing locked, and answers JSON (shared/report/format.ts `GameReport`, its u64s and the SDK's classes as shared/report/rows.ts writes them: `{"$big": "12"}`, and the fields structured clone leaves of a class, which `revive` makes classes again); a refusal is `{ refusal }`, since a procedure's caller hears only its text. The memory host runs the same body (`report`), its GETs through a new `aiGet` (the local host's and the page host's AI service in process). The bindings are regenerated (`report_game_procedure.ts`).
- **Restoring a reported game** (simulation/src/games/restore.ts): `restore_game`, the database's owner's, makes the report's rows a new game here, as it was reported, for a session to carry on: each table's rows written with new ids where the table gives them (the game, its players, places, scenes, entities, turns and fights first, then pointed at each other), every id column at the new rows, the player's character's key (`pc:<player id>`) and their lines' speaker (`player:<player id>`) rewritten wherever they're written (JSON columns too), a new uid (the restore gives the AI service's folder for it the report's docs), its host played by the identity given and seated in it now, its last played now (so Continue offers it first), whoever only watched left out, and the game it came from never touched. Each job it had in flight is made again (jobs.ts `restoreJob`): its plan's ids the new ones, its request built afresh from the rows as they are now, its state as it was, so a failed turn is retried with `/retry` and a turn under way thinks again. A new refusal, `bad_report`, says why a report's rows won't do (not JSON, no game, a campaign version this stack hasn't installed, a uid taken). The memory host has it among its moves; the bindings are regenerated.
- **The client asks for its game's report** (client/backend.ts `reportGame`, client/net): over SpacetimeDB the Connection calls the procedure through the SDK (`conn.procedures.reportGame`); over the page's port a new message, a call (`{kind: "call", id, name, args}`, answered with `value`, shared/protocol/port.ts `PLAYER_CALLS`), which the page's server serves as the caller (web/server/connections.ts), so the wire's `PROTOCOL` is 2. The page host's answer has its own latest log lines in it (`log`: the simulation's and the AI service's last 300, the ring buffer browser-design §8 left for V16) and the player's key taken out of all of it.
- **Reporting from the client** (client/report, client/ui/report.tsx): the prototype's reporting, for the client's screens. The app holds a `ReportModel` (AppModel.report), which the screens open: `b` on the map and in a fight (whoever's turn it is, and once it's over), `/report` in a scene (with its words after it, sent at once: `/report Kell asked twice`), `b` on The End, the map's and the fight's Esc menus' last line, Report a problem…, and the title's line and `b`, about the latest game played. "What went wrong?" comes up over whichever screen is up (the App draws it last, client/app.tsx), which it keeps as it was when it was asked for (from an Esc menu, the last screen noted before the menu); Enter files it and says where it went, to tell Claude (on the map, What happens keeps it too); Esc never minds. The report is made by `fileReport` (client/report/file.ts: the game asked of its host through `reportGame`, then shared/report/pack.ts's `reportOf` and `packReport`, the prototype's makeDump and packDump: every key the host knows of and anything key-shaped out, a screen with one on it not kept, gzipped with CompressionStream) and kept by the host's `Reporting`: the terminal client's and the headless one's under `runs/reports/<time>-game<n>.json.gz` (hosts/reports.ts: the build from git, the settings from the environment without anything named like a secret, and the keys it knows of: OpenRouter's from the environment and the dev key file, the AI service's and module's tokens, the session's token, the dev stack's owner's, service's and bearer), the page's downloaded as `kingmaker-report-<time>.json.gz` (hosts/web/client-worker.tsx posts it to the page, web/page/transport.js downloads it). The screens and errors are noted as the client goes (client/report/notes.ts, the prototype's recent.ts as a class; attach.ts reads the renderer's last frame, OpenTUI's or the page's stand-in's, notes it once a second while no Esc menu or question is over it, and watches console.error and console.warn; the hosts note what's thrown unhandled). The headless client has a `report` verb (`npm run play -- report --session <name> "<what went wrong>"`), its screen observe's text. The question's line is the report's own (client/report/model.ts `edited`), and its keys come before any screen's (`useKeysFirst` and `usePasteFirst` in client/ui/common.tsx: a listener put first on the renderer's key handler, which stops a key it takes from reaching any screen or focused box), so no screen had to learn that a question is over it.
- **`npm run report`** (hosts/report.ts, qa/report): the prototype's report tool, for the game's reports. `inspect <file>` gives the summary first (what the player said, when, the build, the settings, where they were and the screen as it was, the last turns as they saw them, each with its calls, the errors and the failed, slow and every call by operation), then each part in full: `--turn <n>` (or `<scene>:<n>`: the player's line and what answered it, the reply as the mouthpiece wrote it with the check it called, the co-GM's roles and moves with what came of each, the stage manager's operations, the turn's timing and calls), `--call <n>`, `--rows [<table>]`, `--errors`, and `--screens`, `--calls`, `--docs [<path>]`, `--log` and `--json` beside them. A bare name is looked for in runs/reports and in ~/Downloads (the page's, named kingmaker-report-*); `latest` is the newest; `list` lists them. `restore <file>` makes the game a new game in a local stack for a session (`--session`, else `report-<time>`, its identity minted by connecting): npm run dev's when its host is up, else one it starts in runs/report-stack on free ports (the loop's background dev host, hosts/loop/stack.ts, on the fake GM unless `--real`; `npm run report -- stack down` stops it); restore_game as the stack's owner over SpacetimeDB's HTTP API, the docs PUT to its AI service, the session's seat read back for the new game's number, and how to carry on said (`npm run play -- observe --session …`, the client's Continue). A prototype's report (format 1) is refused with where its tool is in history.
- **Tested end to end** (hosts/e2e/report.ts, hosts/web/check.ts): the e2e's step 41 plays a game a few turns on the fake model on a real SpacetimeDB (a ready-made rogue, Kell's opening and two lines), reports it from the headless client as a terminal's reports are kept (under the e2e's root), finds its game in it from report_game (its rows, 215 of them in 45 tables, and the AI service's calls and docs for it), has B's report_game of it refused (`not_yours`), reads it as `npm run report -- inspect` does (the summary, turn 1 in full, a call, a table), restores it into the stack for another session (restore_game as the owner over HTTP, its docs PUT to the AI service) and plays a turn on there from where it was reported. It raises the ceiling first, as every step after step 20 does (step 20 leaves $10 in today's ledger). web:check's play reports from the page, `/report` with its words after the first line's reply: the download named `kingmaker-report-<time>.json.gz`, the screen saying where it went, the file the report it should be (the words, where, the screen as it was, the build's stamp, and the server worker's game: the transcript with the line, the AI service's calls from IndexedDB, its docs and its log lines), the page hearing of it from the client worker, and Enter putting the word away. The reporting screen checks (client/check/report-screens.tsx) note the screen every 100 ms (attach.ts takes the beat; a host's is a second): at a second, a busy machine's late timer missed the note that a report from the map's Esc menu keeps, and that check failed once in a full run.
- **The docs:** README.md's "Reporting a problem" (how to report one from each screen, the question, where it goes, what's in it, never a key, and reading one), the browser section's line on it, the testing table's `npm run report` row and the e2e's step, and the layout's rows; ARCHITECTURE.md (the client, the reports' part, the state's rows, the checks' row, the docs list); ai/README.md (the new endpoints, and what the stores read back); web/README.md (the page's download); browser-design.md's §8 and its left for later (problem reports built, the ring buffer kept); and docs/guide/testing.md, new (the prototype's guide is archived in docs/prototype/guide), with "Investigating a player's report (for Claude)": what a report holds and how it's made, then read it, find the turn it went wrong in, look at the state, reproduce it in a local stack, and fix the cause.
- **The page's ready-made characters** (browser-design's "The page has no ready-made character"; client/creation/model.ts `readyMade` and `playReadyMade`, client/ui/create.tsx): on a page the create screen offers the campaign's ready-made characters under its fields (Caerwyn's five, from its builds.json as the campaign's row has it), a button each with its class, Tab's third stop (←→ picks one out, its concept under the buttons, and Enter plays it) or a click. One is played as the terminal client's `--build` plays one, new_game with its build, as the name typed (else the default) and the concept typed (else its own), straight to the map; a refusal stays on the create screen and says why. On a short page the examples give way to the row. The terminal's create screen is as it was (`--build` makes one there). The app model makes the move (`AppModel.newGame`, which puts what went wrong before it behind it, as `act` does). Checked by the creation model's tests, the screen checks on a page and in a terminal at both sizes, and web:check (a browser that keeps nothing plays the rogue to the map).
- **The doors and lamp posts as pictures in the browser** (README.md's "Not yet", browser-design §8's first item; M6's first play hadn't done it: client/ui/place.tsx and the map's and fight's views say where their doors are only when the overlay is wanted, and no host attached it, so the page drew the ASCII alone). The page half is the prototype's, copied whole: web/page/doors.ts (a canvas over xterm.js's screen; the client's word on where the doors are, an OSC sequence before each frame that moves them, taken when xterm has parsed the frame after it, so a door is never a frame out of place; each door's picture painted only on the cells it may, the wall it took given back under it) and web/page/lamps.ts (the lamp posts on the same canvas, lit with their glow after dark), their art the client's (client/look/door-art.ts, lamp-art.ts), bundled by the build as doors.js, which page.js imports with the build's stamp (site.ts lets it in, and holds page.js to stamping its import). The client worker attaches the overlay (`attachOverlay`, as the prototype's page build did) unless the page asks it not to: `?doors=off` sends `doors: false` through the transport, and the page draws nothing; `?lamps=off` leaves the lamp posts to the ASCII. web:check holds it to what the prototype's check did: the map's seven doors and its lamp post drawn, every pixel on the canvas in a cell the game said may be painted on a canvas exactly over xterm's (at 1x, and at 2x in a browser with that device scale), in step with the frames as the map scrolls (six steps walked, each frame's door cells compared with xterm's buffer as they're taken and as xterm draws), hidden under the action menu and back after it, the yard's three in the scene's place too, and `?doors=off` leaving the ASCII alone (no overlay, the client never saying where a door is); site.test.ts has the doors' module left out and its import unstamped among the broken sites.

### Judgement calls

- **A report's game comes from its host, as its owner asks for it.** `report_game` is a procedure that only the game's owner (its host, who made it) may call: a spectator's or anyone else's is refused (`not_yours`), as the rows of a game are its players' and the AI service's calls hold the GM's prep. The module fetches the AI service's calls and docs itself, after its transaction and with nothing locked, so the client never speaks to the AI service (it has no bearer, and on a page it would be the server worker's anyway). A procedure's caller hears only its text, so a refusal comes back as `{ refusal }` in the JSON, not as an error.
- **The page's host answers through the port, and holds its log.** A new message kind (a call, answered with a value) rather than a new reducer: the page host serves `reportGame` as the caller, so the wire's `PROTOCOL` went to 2 (an old client worker against a new server worker is told it's another build, as before). Its answer carries the server worker's last 300 log lines (browser-design §8's "V16's reports could keep a ring buffer instead"), and the player's key is taken out of all of it by the server worker, which holds the key; the client worker knows no key, so it takes out only what's shaped like one.
- **Restoring makes a new game, never the old one again.** New ids for every row a table numbers, each id column pointed at the new rows (JSON columns' `pc:<id>` and `player:<id>` too), a new uid (the report's, with when it was restored), the restorer its host and seated, and whoever only watched left out (their identities mean nothing here). Each job in flight is made again from the rows as they are now: a failed turn waits for `/retry`, a turn under way thinks again. A report from a campaign version the stack hasn't installed is refused (`bad_report`), not installed for it.
- **The question is its own, above every screen.** Its keys come first (a listener put first on the renderer's key handler, `useKeysFirst`, which stops a key it takes from reaching any screen or focused box) and its line is its own editor (`edited`), where the prototype taught each screen's model that a question was over it. A scene's Esc menu with a Report a problem… line was tried and taken out again: Esc already means something in a scene (it backs out of a choice: "choosing to attack asks with what" broke), and `/report` covers the scene. `/report` comes after `/quit` in the command list, so 80x24's six rows still show `/suggestions`.
- **What's noted, and when.** The screen is noted once a second while nothing's over it but what's open on it (never an Esc menu or the question), where the prototype noted one a moment after each of its Game's changes: the client's screens each have a model of their own, and a second's beat costs nothing. A report from an Esc menu keeps the last screen noted before the menu.
- **Where it goes, and what it's called.** `runs/reports/<time>-game<n>.json.gz`, the game's number where the prototype had its uid (it's what the player sees, and what `--join` takes); the page's `kingmaker-report-<time>.json.gz`, as the prototype's page named them. The headless client's `report` verb is the prototype's `report make`. A prototype's report (format 1) is refused, with where its tool is in history, rather than half read.
- **`--call <n>` is the summary's `#n`:** calls are numbered whole (a call's started line and its end are one call: qa/loop's `isWhole`), so the number in the summary is the number to ask for.
- **`restore` brings its own stack when there's none.** npm run dev's when it's up; else one started on free ports in runs/report-stack (the loop's background host), on the fake GM unless `--real`, kept until `npm run report -- stack down`, so a second restore or a client can use it.
- **The e2e's step 41 raises the ceiling** as every step after step 20 does: step 20 leaves $10 in today's ledger, and a step that forgets fails on the GM's budget, as the first run of it did.
- **Ready-made characters on a page only.** The terminal has `--build` for them and its create screen is as it was; on a page there's no command line, so the create screen offers them. On a short page the examples give way to them.
- **The doors' page module imports the client's art.** web/page has no import rule (web/tui's, never client/, stays: the doors aren't drawn by the stand-in), and door-art.ts and lamp-art.ts are pure, so the build bundles them with doors.ts as one module for the page, apart from the workers' bundle, since the page draws them and not a worker. web:check's `?doors=off` rides on the no-storage scenario, which already plays a ready-made character to the map, rather than a page load of its own.

### Checked

At the last commit, each on ports of its own: `npm run check` (the type check clean, the repo's and the module's; 1098 tests of 1098; the screens 802 of 802, 28 of them reporting's and 12 the page's ready-made characters'), `npm run web:screens` 802 of 802 on the page's OpenTUI, the campaign check with no problems for campaigns/caerwyn and campaigns/zoo, the bindings regenerated with nothing changed, `npm run e2e` on 40059 and 33043 (every step, 41 with step 41's 17 checks, 392 checks in all, 425.7 s), and `npm run build:web` (27 files, 5.72 MB) then `npm run web:check` on a port it picked (116 checks: the report downloaded and read back, the five ready-made characters and the rogue played to the map, the map's seven doors and its lamp post drawn and placed at 1x and 2x, in step as the map scrolls, hidden under the menu, the yard's three, and `?doors=off`). By hand: `npm run report -- restore` with no dev stack up started one in runs/report-stack on free ports, restored a report's game into it for a session, and `npm run play -- say` carried it on a turn (the GM answered); `npm run report -- stack down` stopped it.

### Open

- **web/server/fight.test.ts** (M6's) fails now and then under a full run's load: the page host's fight rolls initiative from its own random seed, and when the bandit goes first the test's expected "22/28 HP" isn't what's drawn. It isn't this milestone's, and combat is the user's to change, so it's left.
- **A page's report counts only what the client took out** in `redacted`: what the server worker took out of the game it gave (the player's key) isn't counted, since it's gone before the client sees it.
- **A very long game's report** is one procedure's answer (every row, the last 300 calls and reviews, every doc): fine for the games played so far (a few hundred rows), untried for a long campaign.
- **Not exercised here, as the prototype's check did:** a door's picture changing with it (Fireball at a door) and a lamp lit by night with its glow, and `?lamps=off`. They're copied whole from the prototype, whose check had a wizard's game by the lane put in the browser's saves; v2's check has no such game to put there yet.

### On M2's QA round 3 fixes (the restack)

`claude/v2-zoo-fwd3` (M6 with M4, M3 and M2 at `06160fa` merged in: QA round qa3-1018's fixes, the plugin authoring `onStart`, M4's files reading what M2 changed) is merged into M5 (`claude/v2-reports` at `9eeff59`, cut from M6 at `203d694`) as `claude/v2-reports-fwd3`. Two files conflicted:
- **hosts/play.ts:** the usage, where this branch had added `play report` under `play map` and M2 had rewritten `play map` for `--full`: M2's `map` and then this branch's `report`. The verbs' switch merged on its own (`map` with `--full`, then `ring` and `report`).
- **This log:** M6's note on its third restack ends M6's section, before M5's, and this note ends M5's.

**The game's rows for a report:** M2's round 3 fixes added no table and no column, and changed none (the owed name is a step on gm_state's `path`, the arrivals shown the stage's `on_stage` rows, the quest `onStart` starts a `quest` row), so games/tables.ts's lists stand as they were: every table on one list or the other, tables.test.ts says, and a report carries the owed steps and the quest started with the game as any other row. Nothing else of this branch's met M2's: the AI service's new settle field (`owed`) and reply direction are the turn's, not the report's, and the headless client's map (M2's legend by what the player sees) and report verb sit side by side.
