On the merge, each on ports of its own (never 3330 or 8817): `npm run check` (the type check clean, the repo's and the module's; 1121 tests of 1121, this branch's 1098 and M2's 23 new, tables.test.ts's lists among them; the screens 802 of 802), the campaign check with no problems for campaigns/caerwyn ("6 scenes (28 beats, 65 routes, 20 endings), … 15 looks …") and campaigns/zoo, the bindings regenerated with nothing changed, `npm run e2e -- --port 3871 --ai-port 9371 --root runs/e2e-fwd3-m5` (namespace mode: every step, 41 with step 41's 17 checks, the report's game 216 rows in 45 tables, 399 checks in all, 434.5 s; step 19's longest gap 1168 ms of the 5000 allowed), and `npm run build:web` (27 files, 5.74 MB) then `npm run web:check -- --port 0` (116 checks).

### On M1's fixed dice, and M6's (the merge)

`claude/v2-zoo-dice` (M1's "Dice in tests" by way of M2 to M4, and M6's: its page host tests, its own fight tests and the e2e's fight on fixed dice, the page's `?seed`) is merged in. Both sides are kept where they met: README's page bullet has `?seed` beside M5's ready-made characters, and its web:check row M5's doors and lamps with the zoo ring's `&seed=1`; the page host passes M5's `aiGet` and the seed; the module registers `restore_game` beside `set_dice_seed`; and this log has M6's dice sections closing M6's part. M5's own: `dice_seed` is in `NOT_A_GAMES` (the owner's, not a game's, so a report never carries it and a restore never writes it; tables.test.ts holds every table to one list or the other), and hosts/report.test.ts gives its page host a seed (7), as every page host test does now. The bindings regenerate as M5's with the reducer and `DiceSeed`'s type beside them. **Checked:** the type check clean, `npm test` 1125 of 1125; the battery at the stack's top below.

## After the migration: spells and blows said in a scene

The user played the hanged clerk on #673's preview as the ready-made rogue (Wren Ashdown, a halfling rogue 3, Thief, with no spells) and typed "(I cast firebolt at the boy)", then "(I cast firebolt at cob)". Their report (`kingmaker-report-20261018-124509.json.gz`, turns 9 and 10) has no rules at all: no attack roll, no damage, no slot, no refusal, and the mouthpiece narrating the bolt as cast and missing ("Your firebolt strikes the stones at Cob's feet", "it scorches the old elm beside them"). Since M2 the two-part GM narrated whatever was said, and only the map's act, its mechanic chosen already, was resolved by the rules: the old pipeline's "is this a spell or a blow?" was parked for combat when its decide went (M2's "The old pipeline's salvage", "Kept, parked for combat"). They said: "I feel like spells aren't resolving like they used to?" Now a spell, a blow (a stab, a shot, something thrown) or a class feature said in words in a scene is resolved by the rules before the GM speaks, as the map's act is, and a line that isn't one goes to the GM as fast as before. On `claude/v2-spells`, cut from `claude/v2-reports` at `831994a` (the top of the migration stack). The old pipeline's way is in history at `7cd49e1^` (ai/service/endpoints/decide.ts, the decide commit in simulation/src/moves/say-commits.ts) and the prototype's at `44c6d4a:src/runtime/engine.ts` (playerTurn, :517-656).

### What changed

- **The line check** (`5d246bd`, shared/rules/acting.ts `mightAct`): whether a player's line might be an act the rules resolve, read from its words with no model. Words that are nearly always an act count anywhere in the line (casting, a spell, a cantrip, magic; attack, stab, punch, kick, shoot, slash, smite, headbutt); words that are as often something else count only in what the character does (each bracket and asterisk, and each sentence narrated in the first person, `deedsIn`), in the present tense: hit, strike, smash, bash, kill, a slap or an elbow at someone, something swung, thrown, fired or loosed at someone ("I take a swing at Kell", "loose the hand crossbow at his sword hand"), and one of the character's weapons brought onto, into or through someone or something. A spell counts by its name as typed ("firebolt", "Fire-Bolt" and "fire bolt" are Fire Bolt), the character's own or any of the rules' (the SRD's 339), those that are everyday words (Light, Sleep, Shield, Message, Gate) only written as a name, with a capital letter in the middle of a sentence and not the first word of a longer one ("the Mending House"); a class feature of the character's counts by its name (Second Wind, Rage). `spellNamed` gives the spell a line names, the longest when several fit; `spellsOf` the rules' spells, made once for the isolate's engine, so the pattern of all 339 is compiled once.
- **The act step** (`282c81f`): a new think before the reply, `POST /v1/turns/act` (key `<uid>:s<scene>:t<n>:act`, 20 s, its request the turn's working set; shared/protocol `ActRequest`, `ActResponse`, the step in `STEPS` and the keys' pattern). Its endpoint (ai/service/endpoints/act.ts) is the old pipeline's decide as far as the rules go: `rulesOptions` (the sheet's spells, weapons and features; from the world what's near enough to aim at, the people outside the cast, where the player might act from), then `mechanicOf`, the mechanic question in a call of its own (`director.rules`), offering a scene's acts (`SCENE_ACTS`: a spell, a blow, a class feature, or none), and for an act its details in a second (`director.mechanic`: which spell at whom or at what, which blow at whom with what, where from; `director.feature`), Luna's decision mode both. A spell for a character with no spells needs no second call: whatever they reach for isn't theirs (`not_theirs`). The canned GM finds no act; the request check wants the turn and its line; `exit-after-store:act` is a fault the tests can name.
- **The turn in the simulation:** `twoPartStart` (simulation/src/moves/two-part.ts), after the rules first, asks the line check (`actingIn`: a scene of the story, a character the rules have made, the sheet's own spells, weapons and features and the rules' spells); a line it lets through plans the act step, every other line the reply. The act step's commit (say-commits.ts `afterAct`) resolves the act it read with `takeAct`, as the map's act is resolved (`resolveAct`: its dice lines in the transcript before the reply, its directive the reply's, its step on the path, the world, the character, the sheet and the pockets written), or, when the line wasn't an act after all, writes nothing and goes on to the reply. An act that was violence, a line's or the map's, then has the reactions step, as the old pipeline's had (`beforeReply`). The turn shows `replying` throughout (a retried act step too); a talk and a closing reply ask nothing.
- **The reactions start no fight** (shared/rules/scene-acts.ts `react`): whoever saw the player's violence reacts as the decider has it (`POST /v1/turns/reactions`, `director.reactions`: the fleeing leave the place and the stage, the cowering cower, someone steps in), but someone who'd fight only turns on the player: no brawl, and no blow left hanging to land as the next turn starts. The prototype's way, a fight if anyone wants one, is parked for combat behind `react`'s `fights`. The reply's direction for them says so (gm/mechanics.ts `reactionDirective`: "turns on the player, ready to fight", the reply ending before anyone's blow lands, "whether it comes to a fight is for the story to decide").
- **The reply is told it's decided** (ai/gm/two-part.ts `actsDirective`, from the reply endpoint): the act's directive (and the reactions') in the per-turn note's "For this reply" is led by the rules' word: they resolved it before this reply, the player has seen their dice, what they decided is what happened, played exactly so and never against it whatever the player hoped (a miss misses, a spell does what they say and no more, magic the character hasn't got doesn't come), with no check for the act itself and one for anything else risky still the mouthpiece's.
- **The co-GM plays the fallout** (`actNote`, `SettleRequest.act`): the settle tells it the act's dice lines and who reacted how, under "The player's act this turn", with its fallout the roles' (whoever it hurt and whoever saw it, what each does about it now) and a fight only a route's.
- **A refused spell is named from the player's words** (shared/rules/acts.ts `castSpell`): a spell that isn't on the character's sheet comes from the decider as `not_theirs`, which the rules refused as "That isn't a spell…"; now it's the spell the words name: "(Fire Bolt isn't a spell Wren Ashdown has ready: /sheet shows what they have.)", and the directive "(Fire Bolt). It doesn't come: nothing happens beyond a gesture, maybe a fizzle."
- **What a spell said in words conjured is the spell's** (`SayPlan.read`): the settle marks what the stage manager made of it (a spectral hand, a floating disk, a spiritual weapon) as conjured, to fade with the scene, as it did the map's act's.
- **`npm run report`'s turn** shows what the rules were asked (qa/report/inspect.ts: each `director.*` call's questions and choices, "mechanic: cast (0.96)"), so a report of a typed act can be read for what the act step made of it.
- **Tests** (`282c81f`, `3d9a76c`): the line check's own (the report's lines, 21 acts, 17 ordinary lines, most of them the QA rounds' own, the names as typed, the deeds); the act endpoint (the wizard's Magic Missile at the hoist rope with its details and a scene's acts offered, the report's rogue with no details call, a line that's no act, a sheet with no build) and its plumbing (a 400 without the line, kept by its key, exit-after-store); the simulation's turn (a spell said in words, its dice rows before the reply, the world and the sheet changed, the reactions and the co-GM told; the report's turns 9 and 10 refused with nothing spent and nobody reacting; a spell with no slot left for it refused, its reason the rules'; a conjuring marked; an ordinary line with no act step, and a false alarm that answers none; the map's act drawing reactions; a fight reaction starting nothing); the mechanic question's scene offer; the reply's word and the co-GM's note; and the report's turn. The e2e's step 27 is a spell said in words in the wizard's game (step 26's, whose map's act now draws reactions too, which it checks): "(I cast Magic Missile at the hoist rope)", the rope destroyed and Prout fallen, the dice rows before the reply's first draft for both clients, the act and the reactions asked once each, no fight.
- **The docs:** README.md's "The rules resolve an act" (no longer the known gap), its e2e row and its layout's simulation row; ARCHITECTURE.md's line said in a scene; ai/README.md's routes (the act's, the reactions' and the reply's and settle's rows), the GM pipeline's steps, the fake's choices, and "Spells and blows said in a scene" beside what's still "Parked for combat"; docs/two-part-design.md's As built (a turn, the settle, what scenes no longer use, and a part of its own); hosts/dev/README.md and fake.json's about; the live run's world script's about; the live record of the world script's eight lines on real models (docs/live/world-2026-10-18.md, beside 2026-10-16's of the old pipeline), which README.md lists; and this log.

### Judgement calls

- **A cheap line check before any think, rather than the question beside the reply.** The old pipeline asked the mechanic question of every line inside its one decision; the two-part GM has no such call, and asking one of every line would add a model call (and its round trip) to each turn. Running it beside the reply and using it only when it says so would cost a call a line and hold the reply's stream until it answered, or start the reply again when it found an act. So the simulation reads the line first, in its own transaction, with no model and no round trip (20 to 60 µs), and only a line it lets through has the act step. It errs towards asking, since a false alarm costs one decision call (about 0.3 s) and a miss is the GM's to narrate as before: on the QA rounds' 561 player lines it asks of 16, 12 of them acts (Mending murmured over spectacles, a Floating Disk conjured, Detect Magic, Dancing Lights, Grease, a dagger stabbed down, a hand crossbow loosed, Mage Hand, a greataxe swung in a Rage, Message, Detect Evil and Good, Minor Illusion), one a plan (Disguise Self, later), and 3 not (a page stabbed with a finger, a sword-cane kicked away, "No spell on you"): false alarms on 0.5% of ordinary lines. In the e2e, of every line its games say, only step 27's spell asked. A first cut asked of 17% (everyday spell names in deeds: "watch the gate", "catches the light"; the past tense of a murder: "strangled", "struck"; "slap a crown down"), which the rules above took out.
- **What counts in a scene: a spell, a blow (a shot, a throw), a class feature.** The task's list, and the user's own reply format (two-part.ts FORMAT, in their words since 4ea45ee) gives grappling, shoving and jumping to the mouthpiece's Athletics checks, so the question offers neither those nor a rest (the map's, once a time of day: town-design §3.3), though the old decide did (`EVERY_ACT`, parked). The attack's criterion already says a grab, a shove or a threat isn't one, so such a line answers none and the mouthpiece rolls for it.
- **The map's act draws reactions too.** The old pipeline ran them for both, a line's act and the map's (its decide took the forced mechanic and went to the reactions), and M2 had parked them for both; one path now resolves either and has whoever saw violence react. The e2e's map's acts at Kell (steps 26 and 37) draw them now, the fake's quiet answers having Kell step in, Cob flee and Moll cower, and both games play on as before.
- **No reaction starts a fight**, as the task asks (the user is building combat, and fights aren't to change here): the reactions themselves run, as the old pipeline's did, but `fight` is someone turning on the player, the reply ending before any blow, and whether it comes to a fight is the scene's routes' and its co-GM's (a route into a fight ending, as the Heron has). The old way, the brawl and the blow left hanging that the next turn's rules first landed, is parked behind `react`'s `fights` rather than deleted, since it's the combat work's to keep or drop; the co-GM is told a fight starts only by a route.
- **The fleeing still leave**, as the old pipeline had them, even one who holds a required discovery (the mouthpiece's `inReach` keeps a failed roll from sending a holder away, but the player chose violence): in the live run Moll fled at the first spell.
- **The details call's riders stay parked:** `alsoQuestion` (a route's check the act would hold back) and `meansQuestion` (the act only the means to something risky) were the old decide's; on the two-part GM the mouthpiece calls the checks, and the rules' word tells it that one for anything else risky the player tries is still its own.
- **The mouthpiece is told plainly that the dice have spoken.** The user's prompt says to default to yes, which is how the report's Fire Bolt was played; the act's own directive ("Narrate exactly this", "It doesn't come") was the old pipeline's, and the lead before it is ours, in the per-turn note (not the instructions, so the prompt's cache holds). In the probe and the live runs every act was played as the rules decided.
- **A talk and a closing ask nothing.** A talk has no world and no dice (its reply format has none); a closing reply plays the ending, as the old pipeline's wind-down asked only whether the player spoke out of character.
- **No new phase.** The turn is `replying` through the act and the reactions (the client shows "The GM is speaking…" with no words yet), rather than bringing back the `deciding` phase the client lost with decide: the act step is about half a second.
- **The refused spell's name comes from the words** in castSpell, which already has them (`said`), rather than from a second answer of the act step's: the decider answers `not_theirs` for a spell off the sheet, and the rules name what was reached for. `violence` still sees `not_theirs` and finds nothing to react to.
- **The live record is kept** (docs/live/world-2026-10-18.md): the world script's eight lines, said in words on the two-part GM, beside the old pipeline's record of the same lines (world-2026-10-16.md).

### Latency

Measured on a dev stack with real models (namespace mode, ports 3470 and 8970): the world script's eight lines as the ready-made wizard (`npm run live -- --script world --lines 8`), and the report's rogue saying an ordinary line, "(I cast firebolt at the boy)", "(I cast firebolt at cob)", "(I throw a gutter stone at the hoist hook)" and another ordinary line (`npm run play`). First words are from the line being said to the reply's first text (the turn rows).
- **Ordinary turns:** 0.97 to 1.27 s (five turns), with no step before the reply; the line check is microseconds.
- **The act step**, from the line to the reply's start (the call log): 0.37 to 0.59 s for the rogue's refused spell (one decision call, `director.rules`, 0.28 s) or her stone thrown at a thing (two, no reactions); 0.85 s for the wizard's refused Fireball (its details asked too); 0.89 to 1.58 s for a spell or a blow with its details and the reactions (three calls at 0.26 to 0.84 s each, about 0.3 s typically).
- **Act turns' first words:** 1.46 to 2.34 s on six of eight; 3.9 s and 7.7 s on two whose reply was slow to start (its own first text 2.9 s and 5.5 s, after 1.0 and 1.6 s of act and reactions). The old pipeline's act turns on the same lines (world-2026-10-16.md) had 2.4 to 3.3 s.
- **On the fake** (the e2e's step 35): the act step and the reactions add about 0.35 s of round trips (the map's act's turn 372 ms and the spell said in words 441 ms to first words, an ordinary turn 24 to 95 ms).

### The probe

On the report's own turns 9 and 10 (`~/git/kingmaker-scratch/v2/spells/probe-report.mts`, the key at ~/secrets): the act step's request rebuilt from the report's job rows (the scene before each turn, its line), and the mouthpiece's logged call replayed with the act's word in its per-turn note.
- **The act step** answered `{ kind: "cast", spell: "not_theirs", targets: [] }` for both lines, every time (three asks of turn 9 and one of turn 10), one call of 0.41 to 0.50 s and $0.00016 each: the question read the cast, and a rogue's spell needs no second call.
- **The rules' line:** "(Fire Bolt isn't a spell Wren Ashdown has ready: /sheet shows what they have.)"
- **The reply**, three tries a turn: six of six played the magic not coming, with no fire, no damage and no check ("You make the gesture for a firebolt, but no spell answers. Cob flinches anyway…", "You gesture for a spell that isn't ready to hand. Nothing answers—not even a respectable fizzle.", "You reach for the shape of a firebolt, but the spell does not answer. A small, unimpressive spark gutters out at your fingertips."), where the logged replies had "Your firebolt strikes the stones at Cob's feet" and "it scorches the old elm".
- **On the stack**, the rogue saying the report's lines: "🎲 (Fire Bolt isn't a spell Wren Ashdown has ready…)" then "You make the gesture, but no fire answers it; the spell isn't among the magic you have ready. The boy pauses with a stone in hand and looks you up and down. 'That all, palace pigeon?'", twice; the stone thrown, "🎲 Improvised weapon: your attack on the hoist beam: 5 -1 = 4 vs AC 15: miss", then "The gutter stone whistles past the hoist beam and clatters against the granary wall."
- **The wizard's eight** (docs/live/world-2026-10-18.md): Magic Missile cut the rope and Prout fell, Moll fled, Cob squared up, Kell stepped in, and no blow came after; Burning Hands set the gate alight and burned the satchel; the staff hit Kell for 5 ("Your staff catches Sergeant Kell across the shoulder with a hard crack"); Fire Bolt set the elm alight; Fireball, a 3rd-level spell a wizard of 3rd level can't cast, was refused ("the magic will not answer"); every reply played what the rules decided.
- **Spent:** $0.0063 on the probe, $0.104 on the stack's two games: $0.11 in all.

### Checked

On ports of its own (never 3330 or 8817): `npm run check` (the type check clean, the repo's and the module's; npm test 1135 of 1135, the screens 802 of 802), whole on its third run, the first two failing only M6's page-host fight test on its random initiative (2 runs in 10 of it alone fail so; M5's Open), then npm test 1136 of 1136 with the last test added (a spell with no slot left), 15 of them new; both campaign checks with no problems (caerwyn: 6 scenes, 28 beats, 65 routes, 20 endings, 154 lore docs; the zoo: 1 scene, 1 exhibit); the module built (`npm run simulation:build`); at `3d9a76c`, `npm run e2e -- --port 3460 --ai-port 8960 --root runs/e2e-spells` (namespace mode: every step in 441.0 s, 415 checks, 26's reactions and 27 among them); `npm run build:web` (27 files, 5.77 MB) then `npm run web:check -- --port 0` (116 checks); and, beyond the battery asked for, `npm run arc -- --port 3480 --ai-port 8980` (15 of 16, the fight's own check off until V12 as before). No table, column or reducer changed (the act step's plan is the job row's JSON): the bindings regenerated with nothing changed.

### Open

- **A line the check misses is narrated, as every line was:** an act in none of its words ("I put a crossbow bolt in his knee" with no crossbow on the sheet, "my blade finds his ribs") goes straight to the mouthpiece. The QA rounds have none such, and the list is easy to grow; a model read beside the reply would catch the rest, at a call a line.
- **An unsure read is no act:** the mechanic question's bar is the old pipeline's (0.6); below it the line is the GM's to narrate.
- **The NPC's side is narrated:** someone who turns on the player strikes no blow by the rules, and a blow they land in the narration has no dice. That's the combat work's (the fight on the map, or a scene's own fight).
- **Grabs, shoves and jumps** are the mouthpiece's Athletics checks, not the rules' contests and distances, and a rest said in a scene is narrated (the map's action menu rests): the rules' `wrestle`, `leap` and `rest` stay reachable from the map's menu and from `EVERY_ACT`.
- **A witness who flees can take a required discovery with them**, as the old pipeline's could; the scene may then have no good ending left but the co-GM's.
- **The stage manager can bring back someone who fled** (the live run's "girl in a shawl" appearing after Moll ran, as on 2026-10-16): the world's, not this change's.
- **A talk's spells** are the GM's to narrate, as before.
- **M6's page-host fight test** fails now and then on its random initiative (M5's Open).

## After the migration: the act step's reads, tuned on the user's report

The user played the hanged clerk as the ready-made wizard (kingmaker-report-20261018-144627: "a few cases of wrong target, or not picking up the action"). Of her spell turns, the act step read three wrong and one by the rules' own lights:

| Turn | The line | What happened | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | "(I summon some fire to my fingertips)" | Fire Bolt cast at nothing | `director.rules` read cast (.89), the details Fire Bolt, aimed at nobody (Cob .48): the rules cast it anyway |
| 12 | "(I cast a firebolt at the elm in rage)" | Fire Bolt at Cob, who went down | the elm burned down at the turn's start (turn 1's fire), so `aimable` left it out; Cob read yes at .66, which counted |
| 13 | "(I cast thunderwave at the constables)" | narrated, no rules | `director.rules` read cast at .58, under the bar (0.6) |
| 4 | "(I cast sleep on the boy)" | Kell slept too, and the line says "catching you" | the pack's Sleep: a 5-foot sphere, its HP pool by lowest HP, and Wren and Kell were within 5 feet of Cob. The rules as written, not a misread |

### What changed

- **A spell cast by its name is a cast:** a casting word and one of the sheet's spells, named in the same deed (a bracket, *asterisks*, a first-person sentence; an everyday name like Sleep straight after "cast"), skips `director.rules` and the details aren't asked which spell (`shared/rules/acting.ts castNamed`). On the QA rounds' 561 lines it takes 7, every one a real cast (Floating Disk, Detect Magic, Grease, Mage Hand, Message, Detect Evil and Good, Minor Illusion), and saves their first call.
- **The thing the player names stays an aim when it's destroyed** (`aimable`): a charred elm is still there to hit. Other destroyed things aren't offered.
- **A doubtful person beside the thing named isn't a target:** aimed at the thing the player's words name, a person counts only read at .9 or named in the words.
- **Who the deeds name is the aim when nobody's read as one:** for a spell that's aimed or fills an area. The real model reads no for Tull's group at .77 on turn 13 (a spell from the caster "targets" nobody), and the cube would have gone off round the player. A group's words count before its "with" (the dead-cart isn't Tull).
- **A spell that has to be aimed, at nothing, isn't cast at nothing** (`needsAim`: a range beyond Self, and an attack, a dart, a save or a knockout): named, the rules ask at what, nothing spent; read from words that don't name it, it's no act, and the GM narrates the flourish. Thunderwave, Shield and Light go off as before.
- **The act step is told each spell's reach:** the simulation sends the sheet's spellFacts (range, effects' kinds, area) in place of the bare spells (`requests.ts spellsOf`); a sheet whose facts aren't worked out sends the spells as before, and `needsAim` says no.

### Checked

The report's three turns replayed through `mechanicOf` on the fixture's yard at the dead-cart beat, the real decision model (`luna-decisions`) answering the new questions on the report's own recorded state (transcript and world), five runs each, identical every run: turn 11 no act; turn 12 Fire Bolt at the elm (the elm offered, read at 1.00; Cob's .66 dropped); turn 13 Thunderwave at Tull's group, with no `director.rules` call. The type check clean; 410 targeted tests of 410 (ai, shared/rules, shared/protocol, the simulation's world and opening moves, the report tool), the report's turns among them on the model's recorded answers.

### Open

- **Sleep catches the caster and anyone beside the aim**, as the pack has it (no "creatures of your choice"): a rules choice, left as it is.
- **A spell named but not the sheet's** still goes through `director.rules`, as before: the rogue's Fire Bolt is refused there.
- **"The boy" isn't Cob by name:** a person the words call by a role counts only on the model's read.
